---
title: Changelog
description: What changed for developers on OnlyWorlds (the API, the clients and these docs), newest first.
---

What changed for developers, newest first: the API on onlyworlds.com, the clients and tools these docs describe, and the docs themselves. Each day lists what a page now says; every release of a package is in its own changelog: [TypeScript SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/sdk/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md), [Unity SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/blob/main/Packages/com.onlyworlds.sdk/CHANGELOG.md), [Python package](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/python-sdk/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md).

What changed on onlyworlds.com for everyone is on its own [Changelog](https://www.onlyworlds.com/changelog); this page stays the developer log.

## 2026-11-28

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) v5.16.1: the set-up never writes over a file, and says which working files it left.** Its writer refuses an upload file path where anything already is, before it writes anything, where before it wrote over it without a word. Everything it prints is UTF-8 through a pipe. Your AI says which working files it made and where, and its report says where the plan file is and, for an online world, the upload file, each yours to keep or delete. What your yes guards is said exactly: nothing of the world, its folder or the skill copies is written before it.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.61.2](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.61.2): a world folder write refuses an element id that cannot be one file name inside the folder.** `OWFolderWriter` and `OWFolderExport` refuse, before writing anything, an id that is empty, holds `/`, `\`, a colon or a control character, or is `.` or `..`, and a name the archive's own checks refuse, with `OWFolderFormatException`. An archive entry or file name holding a C1 control character (U+0080 to U+009F) is refused too, as the other OnlyWorlds SDKs refuse it.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.13.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.13.1): a world folder write refuses an element id that cannot be one file name.** `write_folder` and `pack` refuse, before writing anything, an element whose id is empty, holds `/`, `\`, a colon or a control character, or is `.` or `..`, and a file name made from an id that the archive's own name checks refuse; `OWWorld.write_refusal` names the type, the id and why. Every path a write makes is checked to lie inside the folder, and `pack` never writes an archive its reader refuses. A patch release: update from any earlier one.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) [0.158.5](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): `write_folder` no longer writes outside its folder.** An element whose name gives no slug is written as `<id>.json`, so an id holding a path, from an export or a server, could write a file outside the folder it was asked to write. Now an id that is empty, holds `/`, `\`, `:` or a control character, or is `.` or `..` is refused before anything is written, and every file name made from an id is checked to stay inside its folder. Every import and `export_world` write through it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) [4.224.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a world held as plain objects becomes a folder's files.** `worldFolderFiles(world, elements)` from `@onlyworlds/sdk/convert` writes them in memory byte for byte as the Python package writes them, ready for `writeWorldFile`, and refuses an element id that cannot be a file name.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.24.1: a fix to Export as OnlyWorlds folder. Please update.** The export names each element's file from the element's id. If a note's `id` had been edited to hold path characters, an export to a folder you picked (**Choose a folder**) could write a `.json` file outside that folder, replacing a file of that name. Ids that come from onlyworlds.com or from importing a folder are not affected. If you open vaults or paste worlds that someone else made, this is the update to take. Every version since 3.0.0 has it. Now a note whose id holds `/`, `\`, `:` or a control character, or is `.` or `..`, is left out of the export and named in the report with the reason, and every file an export writes is checked to stay inside the export folder.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) v5.16.0: setting up a team world shows the house rules before you say yes.** The set-up skill runs a check that writes nothing and shows the Charter it would write. A guessed flag no longer writes a folder. A keeper who seats their own AI as a contributor is told it hands in reports like everyone's ([A team world](/docs/getting-started/team-world)).
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.13.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.13.0): a game opens the file an owner downloads from their world's page.** `OWWorldExport` reads the [export file](/docs/development/api/changes#the-export-file) with no key and no connection, as a world folder opens or as a held world that stays as the file had it.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.15.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.15.0): a player joins a world by an invite link, from a Blueprint graph.** Read the link, show what it opens, redeem it for a key of the player's own. The key is handed over once and never logged, and an error from a redeem or an accept carries no body, since a body may hold the key.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.24.0: your own text fields are written once.** A field you added with Add custom field is written as its `###` section only, and a Download that next rewrites the note (one whose element changed on the server) removes the unread copy at the top, so a Bases or Dataview query on that property stops matching. An older note whose two copies differ is held back until Save element asks which to keep, after a copy to `Kept edits`. Import OnlyWorlds folder no longer reads a file nested more than 200 levels deep, and names it as failed.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.14.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.14.0): a game can set an element's image and attach files to it.** The image and file calls the TypeScript SDK has, in C++ and on Blueprints, where an upload takes bytes or a file's path. A Blueprint node's request times out after 30 seconds, which can cut off a large upload.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.12.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.12.0): a game can set an element's image and attach files to it.** The image and file calls the TypeScript SDK has, through a transport that now sends raw bytes: an upload takes a ticket from the API, then sends the bytes to the host the ticket names. Not yet tested in a web export.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.11.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.11.1), [Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.61.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.61.1) and [Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.13.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.13.1): an answer cut off while its body is read is a transport failure that says so.** In Godot it was read as a value from half a body, and a DELETE whose answer never came was reported as done; now it is kind `transport`. Unity and Unreal already failed it as transport, and now say `the answer was lost while it was read`, with the status.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.61.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.61.0) and [Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.13.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.13.0): a game polls the proposals queue cheaply.** Pass back the list's `ETag`, and while nothing moved the answer is `304` with no rows, refunded against the request budget. Unreal 0.13.0 adds [proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals) themselves, in C++ and on Blueprints. In both, the wrong-kind refusal names a NaN or an infinity by its own name.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.223.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): an answer lost while its body is read throws `OwNetworkError`.** Before this, a connection that dropped while an answer streamed in, or a signal aborted then, rejected with fetch's own `TypeError` or `AbortError`. A body that is not JSON still throws its `SyntaxError`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.223.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a wrong-kind refusal has a class of its own, `OwKindError`.** The writes throw it for the first value `kindFaults` names, in the same words as before. It extends `TypeError`, so an existing catch still holds it, and carries `type` and `fault`. A catch that reads any `TypeError` as offline can now tell the two apart. A connection that never reached the server is still `OwNetworkError`.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.11.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.11.0): a game can poll the proposals queue cheaply.** `list_proposals` and `world_proposals` take `if_none_match`, and while nothing moved the page comes back `unchanged` with no rows, the 304 refunded against the request budget; the wrong-kind refusal names a NaN or an infinity by its own name. The page names 0.11.0 and its zip.
- **[Errors](/api/errors/#invalid_request): `param` names the culprit, or is null.** The API document's summary of `invalid_request` now says what the envelope already did: many refusals name no field (a body that is not JSON, a refusal about a whole world, a value the database cannot hold). The errors table and the proposals snapshot follow it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.222.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): `kindFaults` names every wrong kind in a standard field.** The predicate the writes refuse by, as a function: `[{ field, kind, value }]` in the element's key order, `[]` when there is none, never a throw for a value, so a tool can show which element and field before a write.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#proposed-changes) [4.221.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a poll of the proposals lists costs little when nothing moved.** `worldProposals` and `listProposals` take `ifNoneMatch` and hand back `etag`; while the list is unchanged keel answers `304`, refunded against the request budget, and the call returns `{ unchanged: true, etag }`. New types: `OwListTagged`, `OwListUnchanged`.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.60.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.60.1) and [Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.10.3](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.10.3): a date written with its weekday parses back.** `OWCalendar.ParseDate` and `parse_date` read a weekday beside a whole date and refuse one that is not that date's own, naming both, as the TypeScript SDK 4.220.1 does. The pages name the new versions and Godot's zip.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.220.2](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a wrong-kind refusal names NaN and Infinity by their own names.** A NaN or an infinity in an integer or a text field read as `null` in the refusal of 4.220.0 and 4.220.1, as if the caller had sent null; it now says `NaN`, `Infinity` or `-Infinity`.
- **[A value the database cannot hold is a 422](/docs/development/api/writes#field-rules)**: a NUL character in text, in a write or a list filter, or a number that is not finite in an extension field, answers `422` `invalid_request` with no `param`, where it answered `500`.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.60.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.60.0): a value the server would store as another is refused before sending.** `CreateAsync`, `UpsertAsync`, `PatchAsync`, `BulkAsync` and `ProposeAsync` throw an `ArgumentException` naming the field, the check the TypeScript SDK 4.220.0 makes; `OWElementTypes.IntegerFieldNames`, `IsIntegerField` and `IsTextField` are new.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.12.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.12.1): a value the server would store as another is refused before sending.** `Create`, `Upsert`, `Patch` and `Bulk` answer kind `Refused` naming the field, and the Blueprint write nodes meet the same check.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) [4.220.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.158.4](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a date written with its weekday parses back.** `parseDate` and `parse_date` read a weekday name beside a whole date, so a calendar whose `format` prints `{weekday}` reads its own dates, and refuse a weekday that is not that date's own, naming both. The separators are unchanged, and nothing that parsed before answers differently.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot#your-own-world) [0.10.2](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.10.2): a value the server would store as another is refused before sending.** `create`, `upsert`, `patch`, `bulk` and `propose` answer kind `refused`, naming the field, for `2.5`, `"7"` or `true` in an integer field and a number or a list in a text field: the check the TypeScript SDK 4.220.0 makes. The page names 0.10.2 and its zip.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.12.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.12.0): a body that cannot go whole is refused before sending.** A NaN or an infinity in a request body answers kind `Refused`, named with its place, where it went as `null`; a body nested past 256 levels from its top is refused too.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.11.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.11.0): an error names the size limit a write passed.** `OWApiError::Limit()` and its three predicates in C++, and `Limit` on Blueprints' `FOWApiError`, on a refused write and a bulk item's slot.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.59.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.59.1): a body that cannot go whole is refused before sending.** A NaN or an infinity in a request body throws an `ArgumentException` naming its place, where 0.59.0 sent it as `null`, which cleared the field; a body nested past 256 levels is refused too. The producer line names 0.59.1.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.220.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a value the server would store as another is refused before sending, and [the Waymark](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn) takes the page's face.** `create`, `upsert`, `patch`, `bulk` and `propose` throw a `TypeError` for `2.5`, `"7"` or `true` in an integer field and a list or a number in a text field, which the server would store changed, as the Python package's check does; a page sets `--ow-waymark-font` for the menu's face. The TypeScript and Python pages no longer say the API is still adding `limit` to its size refusals.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot#your-own-world) [0.10.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.10.0) and [0.10.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.10.1): an error names the size limit a write passed, and a body that cannot go whole is refused before sending.** `OWApiError.limit()` and its three predicates read the limit; a NaN or an infinity in a write body is refused by name with its place, where it went as `null`, and the depth line is 256 levels counted from the body's top. The page names 0.10.1 and its zip.
- **[The practice world takes no part in the Council](/docs/getting-started/keys#the-practice-world)**: a motion, a vote, an endorsement, a delegate or a dispatch sent with its key is refused with `403`, as a demo key is, after the PIN is checked. The [Council page](/docs/tools/council#the-agents-chamber) says which keys act there.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.23.2: a Save no longer sends older text over what a Download brought.** A Save that waited, for a PIN for example, while a Download rewrote the note is now refused as out of date and sends nothing; a note renamed while auto-sync waits or a Save runs is saved once.
- **[The three size refusals carry `limit`](/docs/development/api/writes#field-rules)**: an element past its 1 MB or its 5,000 link ids, and extension fields past 64 KB, now answer with the key of the limit they passed (`element_bytes`, `element_link_ids`, `extensions_bytes`), read by the TypeScript SDK's `err.limit` and the Python package's `ApiError.limit` since 4.219.0 and 0.158.0.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.59.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.59.0): an error names the size limit a write passed.** `OWApiError.Limit` and its three predicates read which limit a write passed, on an error and on a bulk item's failed slot. A NaN or an infinity in a request body is now sent as `null`, no longer as a quoted string.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.219.2 and 4.219.3](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python#write) [0.158.2 and 0.158.3](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a body holding a number that is not finite, or nested past 256 levels, is refused before sending.** NaN or an infinity anywhere in a write's body is refused by name, with where it sits; before, the TypeScript SDK sent `null` in its place, which clears the field, and the Python package sent tokens that are not JSON. A body deeper than the 256 levels [the server reads](/docs/development/api/writes#field-rules) is refused the same way. 4.219.2 and 0.158.2 drew that line at 200, which refused a `bulk` item holding a value 199 or 200 levels deep that the server stores; 4.219.3 and 0.158.3 draw it at 256.

## 2026-11-27

- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.23.1: a Save no longer marks a note the world did not take whole.** After a Save the next Download overwrote a note with no copy when the Save had sent less than the note held: a text property edited beside its `###` section, a decimal in a whole-number field, a second link in a field that holds one, a link to a note not in the world. Now a note is marked only when the world's answer, written the way a Download writes it, is the note; otherwise the next Download copies it into `Kept edits` first. Ordinary edits stay marked.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot#what-is-in-it) [0.9.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.9.0): proposals in a game.** A player proposes a change without making it and sees their pending proposals; the owner accepts or declines each from the queue, a player withdraws their own, and a new element written as proposed is settled with `review_new_element`, as in the Unity SDK.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.58.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.58.1): `OWSync` holds an upserted element under its own id.** A body whose `id` differed from the change row's was held under the row's; and a 409 from the read of an upsert that came without a body is that read's error now, not a reset of the whole world, as the Godot and Unreal SDKs answer it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.219.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.158.1](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a body the client cannot send fails at once, under its own name.** A value JSON cannot hold in a write's body threw `OwNetworkError` in TypeScript, and in Python was retried five times with waits before a `ConnectionError`; each now raises its own `TypeError` before anything is sent.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.23.0: Download world keeps text typed in an open note, and Paste world keeps a copy.** Download saves an open editor of a note before it replaces the note, so the last seconds of typing are in the copy in `Kept edits`; Paste world copies a note it replaces there too. Paste world does not save an open editor first.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.10.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.10.1): every string keeps its characters through the plugin.** The engine's own conversion wrote `?` for U+FFFE and U+FFFF, so an element id holding one named no element from a Blueprint node; the plugin now converts with its own loop.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#errors) [4.219.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.158.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a size refusal can be read by its limit.** `err.limit` and `ApiError.limit` read a key the API is adding to its three [size refusals](/docs/development/api/writes#field-rules) (`element_bytes`, `element_link_ids`, `extensions_bytes`, each a key of `info.x-ow-limits`), with a predicate for each; the code stays `invalid_request`. Until the API sends the key, read the sentence as before.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#export-to-okf) 0.158.0: the OKF export writes Open Knowledge Format 0.3.** The pin moves to the spec's version 0.3 in its new repository; the bundle's root index carries the world's name and a description; a text YAML 1.2 would read as a number is quoted.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.22.1: Download world copies fewer notes after an Upload world.** Upload world now keeps the record of a note you have not typed in, so the next Download copies only the notes you did type in.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.15.0: the develop skill reads a world from a short page.** A session that only reads a world no longer downloads the API document first; writing, building, agents and lenses have pages of their own inside the skill.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.10.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.10.0): a game holds a world and keeps it current.** `OWWorldCache` and `OWSync` in C++, `UOWWorldCache` with the `SyncWorld`, `BaselineWorld` and `HasWorldChanges` nodes on Blueprints, by the Unity and Godot SDKs' sync rules; a poll of an unchanged world is answered `304`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#the-mcp-tools-on-a-world-folder) [4.218.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the MCP tools on a world folder take `list_elements`' `fields`**, as the MCP server does: each row holds only the fields named, with its id, type, supertype, subtype and marks kept, and a name the type lacks refused as the list route refuses it.
- **[A first changes read that finds nothing answers the feed's position](/docs/development/api/changes)**, the value `?head=true` gives, not the start, so a reader that keeps it no longer replays the world. **The MCP server's [`list_elements`](/docs/development/mcp) takes `fields`**: each row then holds only the fields you name, with its id, type, supertype, subtype and marks kept.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.9.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.9.0): how an element is shown.** `OWViews` in C++ and `UOWViews` on Blueprints read the schema's views, with the same answers as the Unity and Godot SDKs.
- **[Unreal](/docs/development/games) [0.8.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.8.0): a world's maps and layouts reach Blueprints.** `UOWMapSpace` reads each Map's frame, its Zones and Pins, how a point carries to a parent map and which Pins a reader knows; `UOWLayout` lays out a graph by kind, around one element or by links in 3D, each answer equal to the C++ core's on the shared cases.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot#a-world-kept-current) [0.8.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.8.0): a game holds a world and keeps it current.** `OWWorldCache` holds it and keeps itself in one file between launches; `OWSync` fills it and applies the change feed, as the Unity SDK's sync does.
- **The engine packages take the TypeScript SDK's group names: [Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.58.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.58.0), [Godot](/docs/development/godot) [0.7.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.7.0) and [Unreal](/docs/development/packages) [0.7.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.7.0).** `GroupOf` in Unity and Unreal, `group_of` in Godot, with Unity's `GroupOrder`, `ColorOfGroup` and `ResolveCalendar` beside them; the old family names are marked to leave in 1.0, as the [Stability page](/docs/development/api/stability) says.
- **[An element holds at most 1 MB of text and links](/docs/development/api/writes#field-rules), and 5,000 link ids.** Counted as the UTF-8 bytes of its text fields plus 40 for each id in its link lists; extension fields keep their own cap. A write past either is a `422` [`invalid_request`](/api/errors/#invalid_request) and nothing is changed; in `/bulk` that item fails. The [limits table](/docs/development/api-reference#limits) and [Fields](/docs/schema/fields) say it too. Nothing stored on OnlyWorlds is near either limit.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.22.0: Download world keeps text you had not saved.** Before it rewrites a note that may hold unsaved typing, it copies the note into a `Kept edits` folder and says which notes it copied.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.217.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a calendar past a size bound can be opened to cut it down.** [`readCalendarShape`](/docs/development/typescript-readers) reads a calendar for editing, every check but the size bounds, and names the first bound it passes in the validator's words.
- **The engine packages make the written promise too.** The [Stability page](/docs/development/api/stability#the-standard-the-packages-and-this-site) now says it for Unity, Godot and Unreal: a patch fixes, a minor adds, a name that will go is marked first in each engine's own way and stays at least six months, and a check in each package's CI holds it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.216.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.157.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): the [Kanka importer](/docs/development/python-import#kanka) reads Kanka 4.0's exports.** A Character with Kanka's own Dead status gets `[Deceased] ` at the start of its `physicality`, a family's member list is kept with its reason in the report, and races and families are read on a real export. Exports of Kanka 3.15 come out as before.
- **A claude.ai page can read a world through the MCP connector.** The [MCP page](/docs/development/mcp#sign-in-from-an-ai-assistant) says what was tried: an artifact read worlds as its viewer, with no key in the page, and the Claude desktop app installed it as an app in its own window.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.215.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the pending sign-in's resume, read at once.** `pendingSignInResume` gives a return page the tool's `resume` before `completeSignIn` answers, so it can route to where the person was without waiting for the exchange ([Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#in-the-sdk)).
- **The client packages make a written promise.** The [Stability page](/docs/development/api/stability#the-standard-the-packages-and-this-site) now says what a release of the TypeScript SDK and of the Python package may change: a patch fixes, a minor adds, neither removes or renames a public name; a deprecated name says since when and the major it leaves in, stays at least six months, and goes only in SDK 5.0 or onlyworlds 1.0. A check in each package's CI holds it on every change.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.214.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.156.5](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): every deprecation says since which release.** The [list of deprecated names](/docs/development/typescript#deprecated-names) shows a date for each; the Python package's two marks say they leave in onlyworlds 1.0.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.214.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): every sign-in refusal carries the resume.** Each `OwSignInError` that `completeSignIn` throws now holds the tool's `resume`, not only a press of Cancel, so a return from an older sign-in page no longer loses a held link or code ([Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#in-the-sdk)).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.213.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a press of Cancel at sign-in is `cancelled`, with the resume.** `completeSignIn` answers a return from Cancel on onlyworlds.com with the new reason `cancelled` and the tool's `resume` on the error, so the tool can put the person back where they were; before, it was `state_mismatch` and the resume was lost ([Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#in-the-sdk)).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.212.3](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the world file writer refuses a folder the readers refuse.** `writeWorldFile` throws `name_case` for a folder that holds `world.json`, `elements/` or `spatial/` only in another case, as `readWorldFile` and the Python package do; before, a `Spatial/` folder beside `elements/` was left out of a copy, and a share left out every element. A `world.json` nested too deep is refused with a message in words only; the code stays `worldFault`.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [v0.57.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.57.0), [Godot](/docs/development/godot) [v0.6.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.6.0) and the [Unreal SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk#first-run) [v0.6.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.6.0): a join preview names the Character a person's invite link offers, and a refused `world.json` says why.** The preview's Character name is null (absent in Unreal's C++ core) when the link names none. A world file or folder refused for its `world.json` names the fault beside `not_a_world`: `too_deep` or `not_json`, and in Unity and Godot `number_out_of_range` for a number too large for a double, which the TypeScript reader reads. Read the field, never the message.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.212.2](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.156.4](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a calendar's validator names every size bound.** `validateCalendarConfig` now names a calendar with no months whose `year.steps` is past 2^53 - 1, which the reader already refused; Python's `validate_calendar_config` gives the npm SDK's words for every bound (more than 256 eras, 64 months, a year past 2^53 - 1 steps, and the rest), where it gave none.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) [0.156.3](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): the depth check before a parse reads a dense file in about half 0.156.1's time** (61 ms on 50,000 small objects where 0.156.1 took 129), its answers held to 0.156.1's.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.212.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.156.2](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a calendar's duration never throws on a step name that is not text, and a whole count is worded exactly.** `formatDuration` and `format_duration` read a `step_name` that is not a string as absent and word the duration in the grain, the calendar profile's rule for an optional key of the wrong kind; before, they threw. A whole count is now shown as it is up to 2^53: `9007199254740990` steps were worded one day short.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) [0.156.1](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): the depth check before a parse takes time linear in a file's length.** Since 0.155.0 the check that a file opens no more than 200 levels took time growing with the square of the length on some texts (a string left open and full of escaped quotes: about a second at 16 KB). Every reader of a file someone else made, a folder, a world file or an import, now scans it in one pass, with the same answers.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public): a keyless call to the MCP server is a `401`.** The page still said a keyless tool call answered a tool error with HTTP `200` and that the schema tools needed no key. Since 2026-11-24 a call with neither a key nor a sign-in answers `401` before any tool runs, as the [MCP page](/docs/development/mcp) says.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [v0.56.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.56.0) on serial 149: a calendar has size bounds, and a date too far out to be exact is a plain number.** A calendar past a bound (more than 256 eras or 64 months, among others) or with a year longer than 2^53 - 1 is no calendar, and its dates read as plain numbers; a date past the exact range of whole numbers is shown as its plain step, and `OWCalendar.IsPastExactRange` names it. `StepBreakdown.AbsYear` and `Year` can now be null, for such a date. All 191 of the calendar profile's cases pass.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [v0.5.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.5.0) and the [Unreal SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk#first-run) [v0.5.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.5.0) on serial 149: the same calendar bounds.** A calendar past a size bound is no calendar, and a date too far out to be exact is shown as its plain step (`OWCalendar.is_past_exact_range` in Godot, `OWCalendar::IsPastExactRange` in Unreal). A breakdown's year can be absent for such a date: `null` in Godot, an empty optional in Unreal's C++ core, and in Blueprints `FOWStepBreakdown` gains `bPastExactRange`. All 191 of the profile's cases pass in each.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.212.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.156.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/) on serial 149: a calendar has size bounds, and a date past the exact range is its plain number.** Both readers refuse a calendar past the calendar profile's bounds (256 eras, 64 months, a week of 64 steps and names, 16 days outside the months, an `except` of 8, names and templates of 256 characters) or with a year longer than 2^53 - 1, and show a date past ±(2^53 - 1) as its number, its breakdown keeping only the step. A world file refused `not_a_world` for a `world.json` it cannot read now says why: `worldFault` in TypeScript, `world_fault` in Python.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) 1.125.25, 1.125.26, 1.125.33 and 1.125.35: a folder or world file nested past 200 levels, a folder in the wrong capitals, and where a page publishes from.** An element file nested more than 200 levels deep is left out and named with the files that cannot be read; a `world.json` that deep is refused, in a folder or a world file; a folder that holds `world.json`, `elements/` or `spatial/` only in other capitals is refused, with the name to change. Publishing needs the world on onlyworlds.com, and Publish offers Make online for one kept in the browser or a folder.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.21.3 and 3.21.4: deleting your own last property removes it from the world.** Save element and Upload world clear it on onlyworlds.com, once the note has been downloaded or saved with its properties; an entry named `type` that repeats the note's type, or a field the server refuses, stays inside `x_properties`.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.14.0: the timeline speaks the calendar the World names.** `--calendar`, else the World's `x_calendar`, else the world's only calendar; the drawing still never changes the world, and the session asks before it names a calendar.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#invite-links) [4.211.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): `previewJoin`'s answer types `character_name`**, the Character a person's link names for its person, and `createWorldLink`'s `character` is documented for a person's link for one contributor or co-builder. Nothing the client sends changes.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links): an invite link for one contributor or co-builder can name the Character they will play.** `character` on `POST /api/v2/account/worlds/{world_id}/links` for a person's link; once they join they play it, or join as if the link named none when it has been deleted, hidden or taken since. `POST /api/v2/join/preview` answers `character_name`, the Character they would play if they joined now, or `null`.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#sign-in-from-an-ai-assistant): Claude, ChatGPT and other apps that sign in can write to your worlds when you choose Read and write.** The sign-in page offers Read and write (with your account PIN, each time), Read only and Cancel; an account with no PIN is offered Read only, and an app connected before today keeps reading until you connect it again and choose Read and write. Signed in, the [write tools](/docs/development/mcp#write-a-write-key-and-pin-or-a-sign-in-granted-read-and-write) take `world`, and [`get_me`](/docs/development/mcp#read-a-key-or-a-sign-in)'s `scope` is `write` while that sign-in may write there. A connector asks for [`scope=world:read world:write`](/docs/development/api/signin#ai-assistants); `world:write` alone is refused. Stop writing on your account page ends writing for that app and leaves it reading.

## 2026-11-26

<!-- toolkit v5.14.4: no docs change (its own scripts leave out an element file nested past 200 levels and name it too_deep, as the folder format says since serial 148, which the Folders page already states; the same 11 skills and agents) -->

- **[Errors](/api/errors/#write_not_granted): `write_not_granted`.** An app connected to the MCP server with Sign in with OnlyWorlds tried to write without a grant to write. It comes back only as the text of the MCP tool's error, never as an HTTP status.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#giving-your-ai-access): the instructions a seat's card copies for your AI now ask about the world's skills.** After the Charter, the AI reads each Object in `front_door.charter_skill`; when everything else is done, it names each one that is a skill it holds no copy of and asks you where the copy goes before writing one. A person who plays no Character in the world is told so on the card.
- **[Writes](/docs/development/api/writes#field-rules): a value under an extension key, or under a World's `source` or `producer`, sits at most 200 levels deep.** The element (or the World record) counts as the first level, as the folder readers count since serial 148. Deeper is a `422` [`invalid_request`](/api/errors/#invalid_request) with `param` the key, and nothing is changed. A request body nested more than 256 levels deep is not read at all: a `422`, "The body is nested too deep to be read." [Fields](/docs/schema/fields) and [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads) say the same bound.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot) [v0.4.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.4.0) and the [Unreal SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk#first-run) [v0.4.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.4.0) on serial 148.** Both leave out an element file nested more than 200 levels deep by name, `too_deep`, counted in the text, and read the rest; both name finders pass over web addresses, and over Markdown's code and HTML when told the text is Markdown. The Unreal plugin also turns an element read over the API into its Blueprint struct (`<Type>FromJson`), and names its engine (5.8), platform (Win64) and icon.

- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [v0.55.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.55.0) on serial 148: a file nested past 200 levels is left out by name.** A folder or a `.owworld` skips such an element file as `too_deep` and serves the rest, counted in the text as the other readers count it, and every reader reads to 200 deep where it stopped at 64. An export declares folder format 0.3.25. The name finder passes over web addresses, and over Markdown's code and HTML when told the text is Markdown.

- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members): an AI seat's own Read mark under the propose switch.** With **AI seats propose, a person accepts** on, an AI seat writes its own Read mark at once only to make it or to change its `narratives`; any other change answers `403` [`narratives_only`](/api/errors/#narratives_only), a new code on the errors page.

- **[Folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools) (serial 148): an element file nested past 200 levels is left out by name.** A reader leaves out an element file in which an object or array sits more than 200 levels deep, names it `too_deep` and reads the rest of the world; the depth is counted in the file's text, and `world.json` the same way. schema-dist's [checker](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools) counts it itself, the same on every Python. The folder format is 0.3.25.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) [4.210.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python#a-world-as-a-folder) [0.155.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/) on serial 148.** Both packages count depth in a file's text before they parse it and write folder format 0.3.25; a world file whose `world.json` is past 200 levels is refused. The TypeScript folder source's 500 names why a `world.json` cannot be read (`worldFault` in the read report). In Python, `push` never sends `date_labels`.

<!-- toolkit v5.14.2: no docs change (the context skill's answer ends by naming the update skill for writing a session back and the import skill for a folder of plain notes; no page states the old ending) -->
<!-- obsidian-plugin 3.21.5: no docs change (a Pin pointed at another element sends its type and id together, and a link to a name with square brackets saves; no page states either failure) -->

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#the-mcp-tools-on-a-world-folder) [4.209.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the MCP tools on a folder give a date its words too.** `get_element`, `list_elements` and the element resource of `@onlyworlds/sdk/mcp` carry [`date_labels`](/docs/development/mcp#dates-in-the-worlds-words) by the server's rule. Read beside onlyworlds.com on the demo world: 122 bodies, 62 with labels there, every one the same from the package. The server holds back a calendar past its own bounds; the package words it.

- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#dates-in-the-worlds-words): a date comes with its words.** `get_element`, `list_elements` and the element resource now carry `date_labels`, the last key of a body that has one: `{"start_date": "Year 30 YSL"}` beside `"start_date": 280`, where the World names its calendar in [`x_calendar`](/docs/schema/profiles#calendar). A world that names none gets none, and a page with more than 200 different dates carries none. The number stays the value, and a write that carries `date_labels` is refused. `search_elements`, `get_draught` and the REST reads are unchanged. `GET /world` now leaves `x_calendar` out for a reader who may not read the calendar it names, and every world's `ETag` there changed once. An element at the id a World names is created only by its owner or a co-builder; anyone else's create answers `409` [`id_conflict`](/api/errors/#id_conflict).

- **[Python](/docs/development/python#a-world-as-a-folder): a world as a folder, with an example that runs.** The page said `push` compares a folder with a baseline and showed no call. It now saves the practice world with `export_world`, changes one file and pushes the one field back. [Your first hour](/docs/getting-started/first-hour#what-you-can-leave-for-later) points at it, and at two things a reader had to guess: how to read every element of a type ([Reads](/docs/development/api/reads)) and that a page in a browser may call the API from any origin ([CORS](/docs/development/api/cors)). Found by two fresh agents handed that one page.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) [4.208.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.154.1](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a calendar's eras cost in proportion to their count.** The era walk was quadratic and ran for every label: one label under 15,000 eras took 42.4 ms in TypeScript and 331 ms in Python, now 4.1 ms and 101 ms. Every label is the same as before.

## 2026-11-25

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers) [4.208.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.154.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): the name finder passes over what is no mention.** In any text it now skips a bare web address and an autolink, beside the addresses and Markdown links it skipped before. A call for a text known to be Markdown (`{ markdown: true }`, `markdown=True`) skips inline code, fenced code blocks, HTML tags and comments and reference definitions too, so a link written over a found name never lands inside them.

- **[Unreal SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk#first-run) [v0.3.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.3.0) on serial 147: a pack can be a share.** The two Blueprint pack nodes take `bAsShare`, which leaves out the elements marked hidden and the files the folder reader would not serve. The C++ core's `OWCalendar::SpeakingCalendar` reads the World's `x_calendar` as the other SDKs do (no Blueprint node yet), and a new element's file name is the one the other SDKs give it. The [SDKs and clients](/docs/development/packages) page names no version, so nothing else moved.

- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) [v0.54.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.54.0) on serial 147: which calendar speaks.** The Unity page names `OWCalendarLens.SpeakingCalendar`, which reads the World's `x_calendar` as the two packages do, and its producer line says 0.54.0. A new element's file name is now the one the TypeScript and Python packages give it on every runtime, Mono included.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) [4.207.0 and 4.207.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.153.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/) on schema-dist serial 147: the calendar the World names.** The World names the calendar its dates speak in, in `x_calendar` ([the calendar profile](/docs/schema/profiles#calendar)), and `speakingCalendar` and `speaking_calendar` read it: the named calendar, or the world's only one when none is named. In TypeScript, 4.207.1 lets `speakingCalendar` take any World type, an interface of your own included. Both packages replay the serial's new address cases and its world-file set too.

- **[Godot SDK](/docs/development/godot) [0.3.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.3.0) on serial 147: a pack can be a share.** The Godot page says that with `include_hidden` false a pack leaves out the elements marked hidden and the files the folder reader would not serve, and names the 0.3.0 zip. `OWCalendar.speaking_calendar` reads the World's `x_calendar` as the two packages do.

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.13.3: its world-folder page and its read and draw scripts take the files every reader takes since serial 146.** A file directly in a type folder, its name ending in `.json` in lower case and not beginning with a dot; the fixed names read as written; a file passed over for its case named in the not-read list; the lint's exit 3 explained. The [World folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools) page states the same rules.

- **[llms.txt](https://onlyworlds.github.io/llms.txt) gives the element pages' address and names the five SDKs, and [Your first hour](/docs/getting-started/first-hour) says what a read of a deleted element carries.** An agent reading `llms.txt` found one element page linked, Character, and guessed the address of the others: the line now gives the path, `docs/schema/element_categories/<type>.md`. Its opening named the TypeScript, Python and Unity SDKs; Godot and Unreal are named now. The first hour's `410` now names the body's `change_seq` and `deleted_at`.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#the-mcp-tools-on-a-world-folder) [4.206.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the MCP tools on a folder follow the server's `list_worlds` order and its several-worlds refusal.** A row carries no `change_seq`, since a folder holds no write counter.

- **[MCP server](/docs/development/mcp#read-a-key-or-a-sign-in): `list_worlds` gives each world's `change_seq` and a stated order, and a sign-in that names no world is told which it holds.** Its rows are your own worlds first, then your memberships, each by name, and each carries the world's write counter. With several worlds and no `world`, a read is refused in the API's error envelope with the worlds beside it as `worlds`. [`GET /api/v2/account/worlds`](/docs/getting-started/keys) lists in the same order.

- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) [v0.53.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.53.0) and the [Unreal SDK](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk#first-run) [v0.2.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unreal-sdk/releases/tag/v0.2.0) on serial 146.** The Unity page says its archive leaves out the five author fields unless asked and refuses a fixed name held only in another case, its thirteenth cause. Unreal 0.2.0 makes the same three changes and adds the name finder, the Pins one reader knows and the most linked of each type; no docs page names its version.

- **[Godot SDK](/docs/development/godot) [0.2.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/godot-sdk/releases/tag/v0.2.0) and [World folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools) on serial 146.** The Godot page names 0.2.0, whose pack leaves out who wrote each element unless asked. The folders page says the three fixed names are read as written and a folder holding one only in another case is refused, that under `spatial/` only the four are read, how a file's slug is made (NFKD, the marks U+0300 to U+036F dropped), and the checker's exit 3 for a file it could not read.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) [4.205.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) [0.152.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/) on schema-dist serial 146; the Python lint says when it could not read a file.** Both packages replay the serial's new address and calendar cases and its world-file set of 109 archives, with no reader changed. `python -m onlyworlds.lint` gives a file whose JSON Python cannot hold one line saying it was not read, and exits 3 when that is all it found. The archive's refusal causes are thirteen in the set now: the Python page says thirteen, and the [Unity](/docs/development/unity) page says its 0.52.0 has twelve, without `name_case`.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) [4.205.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): `misnamedFixedName` from the package root.** A tool that lists a world folder itself refuses a fixed name held only in another case by the same rule as the folder source and the world-file reader.

- **[MCP server](/docs/development/mcp#connect-from-claude-code): Claude Code signs in with no key, and the try-it command uses the practice world's key.** Run with neither header, the command offers the same sign-in as the chat apps. The try-it command now carries the [practice world](/docs/getting-started/keys#the-practice-world)'s published key, counted by your own address, in place of a demo key's shared budget. The [Resources](/docs/development/mcp#resources) table said the two schema resources need no key: they take any key or a sign-in, and the two world resources take a sign-in to an account with one world as well as a key.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#reading-a-world-file) [4.204.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.151.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a folder name in the wrong case is refused, with the cause.** A world file or a folder holding `Elements/`, `Spatial/` or `World.JSON` where the name in lower case is absent is no longer read as a world with no elements: a world file is refused with the new reason `name_case`, a folder in the TypeScript source answers every route 500 naming it, and Python's `read_folder` raises `FolderNameCaseError`. Python now reads the three names exactly as written on every disk.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#writing-a-world-file) [4.203.2](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.150.2](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a world file never changes a number nobody edited.** A file holding an integer no double holds (`9007199254740993`, which JavaScript reads as `...992`) now travels byte for byte, its author fields with it; the SDK had rewritten it rounded and the Python package exact. An integer past 2^53 that a double holds exactly is rewritten as before.

## 2026-11-24

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#writing-a-world-file) [4.203.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) [0.150.1](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): a world file leaves the author fields out of every element-folder file again.** Since 4.197.0 and 0.145.0 a copy kept `created_by` and the four door fields in a file under `elements/` or `spatial/` that the folder reader passes over (a duplicated id, no `id`, a `.JSON` name, a deeper or unknown folder). Now every such file that is one JSON object loses them; anything else travels byte for byte.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com): the Changelog's switch reads Plain and Full.** A History row shows its own sentence in the plain view, and in `/changelog.json` its `url` is its own place on the page, the outside address it names in its `links`.
- **[Limits](/docs/development/api-reference#limits): where a request spends the budget.** The request rate's row names every place that counts against the 300 and the 60: the routes under `/api/v2`, the MCP server at `/mcp`, the Classic API's two metadata routes, a world's public pages under `/w/` and its Lenses pages on the site. The API document now lists them, and the build checks the row against that list.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.52.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.52.0): the most linked elements of each type.** `OWMostLinked.Of` answers a few of each type by degree from a reader's graph, as the TypeScript and Python packages do; a calendar's date label no longer costs seconds on a long label.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [0.51.0](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.51.0): which Pins one reader knows, and the name finder reads a text once.** `OWMapSpace.PinsKnownTo` answers which of a map's Pins place an element that reader was served; `OWNameFinder` no longer slows on a long name a text repeats, and `Shared` lists the names elements share.
- **[Godot](/docs/development/godot#install): the steps with no editor.** The page names the release zip's file, the one command that registers the `OW…` classes without opening the editor, and how to run the first run as a scene from the command line. [Games](/docs/development/games#engines) no longer says Unity is the only engine with an SDK of its own: Unity, Godot and Unreal each have one.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-most-linked) [4.203.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) [0.150.0](https://pypi.org/project/onlyworlds/): `mostLinkedOf` and `most_linked_of` list the most linked elements of each type.** A few of every type from what a reader's graph holds, never one ranking, the same answer in both packages. It is "the most linked", not "where to start": a world's secret is often its hub, and where a world has a Guide, the Guide is the start.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.149.0: `calendar.format_steps` labels a page of dates in one calendar.** The same words as `format_step` for every step, with the calendar's year shape, eras and leap rules read once for the call and each year's start and days once per year: about six times faster on 200 dates in a calendar of 64 eras, three to four times on two of the profile's real calendars.
- **[The MCP tools on a world folder](/docs/development/typescript#the-mcp-tools-on-a-world-folder) [4.202.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): they count, as the server does.** `folderMcpTools` gains `count_elements`, how many elements `list_elements` serves by type with the same filters, and every `list_elements` page carries `total`, so the folder's read tools answer as [the MCP server](/docs/development/mcp#tools)'s do again.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.13.2 and [AI](/docs/development/ai): an MCP line with no key signs in.** With an empty key header the server answers `401` to every call, the schema tools included, so the develop skill now says to load `.env` before the line; added with no header, the server takes a sign-in on onlyworlds.com, and the AI page says so beside its `claude mcp add` line: a signed-in client reads every world your account owns or is a member of and writes nothing ([Sign in from an AI assistant](/docs/development/mcp#sign-in-from-an-ai-assistant)).
- **[MCP server](/docs/development/mcp#sign-in-from-an-ai-assistant): an AI assistant can sign in to your account and read your worlds.** Add `https://www.onlyworlds.com/mcp` as a connector in a client that speaks MCP's sign-in, such as claude.ai or ChatGPT, and it sends you to onlyworlds.com to sign in and allow it. The sign-in reads only, every world your account owns or is a member of, each read naming its `world` ([`list_worlds`](/docs/development/mcp#read-a-key-or-a-sign-in) lists them); writes still need a key and PIN. A request with neither a key nor a sign-in now answers `401` with the sign-in's challenge, where it used to get the three schema tools: those tools take any key now, a demo world's included. The connector's side of the door, registration and the token endpoint, is on [Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#ai-assistants). A connector added with another address goes on reading there: add it again with this one.
- **The TypeScript pages now hold what the package's README used to say.** The README is a start page now (on GitHub today, on npm from the next release), and its reference moved here: [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) (removing an image, `uploadFile`'s options and refusals, `patchWorld`, the proposal predicates, the world file reader's `maxBytes`, `maxEntries` and `skip`, the room's pairs, the colour groups, `PUBLIC_FIELDS`, the token resource), [Accounts, members and sharing](/docs/development/typescript-accounts) (`listAccountWorlds`, `mintWorldKey`, `me()`'s fields, `listProposals`), [Reading worlds as a tool](/docs/development/typescript-readers) (what `buildAskContext` leaves out and its limits, a lens's recipe, read marks, `formatYearLabel` and `breakdownStep`, addresses as text, the Waymark's options and Copy link) and [Kanka in TypeScript](/docs/development/python-import#kanka-in-typescript) (`convertKanka`, which had no page). The world file table now says a spanned zip is `not_openable`, as the reader refuses it, not `method`.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.21.1: six ways it could lose or overwrite your writing are closed.** A note with the same heading twice is refused by every command that sends or copies it, with the heading named; Manage fields leaves alone a note with text above its first section or with properties that are not valid YAML; Save reads a note's id from the note itself, not from Obsidian's index; and a pasted world is no longer uploaded note by note under auto-sync.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) [4.201.2](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.148.2: a stored calendar no longer sets what one date label costs.** A long run of spaces or commas in a template or a month name, or a month of millions of days, could make one label take up to a minute. Each now formats in under a millisecond, with the same words as before; eras and leap rules still cost in proportion, so the calendar section says what a tool labelling a world it does not own can bound.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.21.0: Copy world, Import folder and Update world format read your notes wherever they sit.** They follow the rule 3.20.0 set for an element note, an `id` and a type in any folder of the world's folder, where before they read only the type folders under `Elements/`. Where a command used to decide for you, it now stops and names the note: Copy world writes `World Data File.md` only when it is absent or carries Copy's own mark, and copies neither of two notes with one id; Update leaves a note alone when a text field is written both in its properties and in its body, and leaves an old-layout note byte for byte, naming Migrate.
- **[The MCP tools on a world folder](/docs/development/typescript#the-mcp-tools-on-a-world-folder) are the server's read tools as they stood before today.** The SDK's folder tools have no `count_elements`, and their `list_elements` page carries no `total` yet; the page said they answered as the server does.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#reading-a-world-file) [4.201.1](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.148.1: a world file opens when a tool keeps its own `world.json` in a dot-folder.** `readWorldFile` and `read_world_file` refused a world that held, say, `abbey/.atlas/world.json` as a second world, though the folder reader never reads a dot-folder. A dot-folder is never a world now, wherever it sits, so the same world opens as a file and as a folder; every other check still runs on its entries. A nested `world.json` outside a dot-folder and outside `records/` is still refused.
- **[Images](/docs/development/api/images#removing-an-image): a removal ticket's `referenced` counts the elements you can see whose `image_url` shows the image.** An image linked inside a text, set as the world's own image or kept in a published page's stored copy is not counted, as the API document now says.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#images) [4.201.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): `preparePhoto` keeps transparency where the browser cannot encode webp.** The fallback is now PNG when the source is a PNG or the picture as drawn has any pixel that is not fully opaque, and JPEG otherwise: a transparent WebP, AVIF or GIF came back as an opaque JPEG, and now comes back as a larger PNG. A `quality` outside 0 to 1, or a `maxBytes` that is not a positive number, is refused with a `TypeError` before the file is decoded, and a result over `maxBytes` gives its size and the limit in bytes.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#tools): `count_elements` says how many elements a world holds, and every `list_elements` page carries `total`.** The server has 18 tools. `count_elements` with no arguments counts each of the 22 types and gives their total, with how many room elements, reading aids and proposed elements its two flags would add; with a `type`, it counts that type alone, and it takes `list_elements`' own filters. `total` on a `list_elements` page is how many elements the whole list holds with the same arguments. The descriptions of `get_me`, `list_elements` and `get_changes` now send the size of a world to `count_elements`, never to a count of the change feed.
- **[AI seats](/docs/development/agents#what-you-can-get-back): a change that came through a proposal keeps the earlier values, and a whole replace empties what it leaves out.** An accepted proposal keeps the element's earlier values for the fields it named, as its `base`; any other overwrite keeps no earlier version. A `PUT`, or a `/bulk` item with an `id`, replaces the whole element and empties every field it leaves out, with no `If-Match` check. The API document now says this before its first write, in a paragraph of its own on what a write cannot take back.
- **[Godot SDK](/docs/development/godot): a page of its own, now that the addon is in Godot's Asset Library.** The [OnlyWorlds SDK](https://godotengine.org/asset-library/asset/5621) for Godot 4.7 installs from the editor's AssetLib tab at 0.1.1. Its page covers the install, a first run that reads the sample world with no account, keys and where a write key must not go, a world on disk, a player's link, and what the addon holds. It has no share yet: a pack carries hidden elements too. [SDKs and clients](/docs/development/packages) and [Games](/docs/development/games#engines) link the page.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) 1.124.27 to 1.125.18: your world opens without your PIN, a read key needs none, and a page says what it shares.** Since 1.125.0 your own world opens from onlyworlds.com without your PIN; Atlas asks for it at your first change and keeps what you changed until OnlyWorlds has accepted it ([Linking a world](/docs/tools/atlas#linking-a-world), and step 3 on [onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)). Since 1.124.31 a key that can only read opens a world to read with no PIN, and Atlas sends none beside it. Since 1.125.13 the Publish section lists what a page sends before you publish, and since 1.125.14 a page no longer sends when an element was made, who made it, or the id of a hidden element a card pointed at: a page published earlier keeps what it had until you press Republish ([Published pages](/docs/tools/atlas#published-pages)). Since 1.124.34 several new elements sent at once never replace one already online ([Working with others](/docs/tools/atlas#working-with-others)). The other drops move no sentence here: wording for unnamed elements, PIN and seat messages, the Data room's review, and what a pin's Full switch says of itself, and what Atlas does when another tab holds a world or a world fails to open or download.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-pins-one-viewer-knows) [4.200.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.148.0: the Pins one viewer knows.** `pinsKnownTo(pins, served)` and `pins_known_to` answer which of a map's Pins place an element one viewer was served, for a map drawn as one Character sees it. Each known Pin says whether its element reached the viewer whole or as a stub it only knows of; the Pins whose element was not served are a count, never ids. The function reads nothing but its two lists, so the map cannot show a viewer more than the world told them. A rule of the two packages, held by one file of cases both run.
- **[Addresses](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose): onlyworlds.com draws an `ow://` address in an element's text as a link.** On the element's page (the owner's, a member's and the public page) the address is a plain link to its human address, labelled exactly as written. Nothing is looked up, and a site address in a text stays plain text.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#giving-your-ai-access): the start line goes in a file in the working folder itself.** The instructions a seat copies, `GET /join` and the join reply's `next` now name the file as the toolkit does since 5.13.1: `AGENTS.md` for most tools; for Claude Code, `AGENTS.md` when the session started with one loaded and the folder's own `CLAUDE.md` otherwise, asking first before a new `CLAUDE.md` is made beside or under an `AGENTS.md`.
- **The API document describes every group, and names where to start.** All 33 groups of `/api/v2/openapi.json` carry a description (ten did), the 22 element types in the standard's order, and its `externalDocs` points at [The first hour](/docs/getting-started/first-hour). No route, field or answer changed.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) [4.199.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the calendar for a page with no build step.** `@onlyworlds/sdk/calendar` is the calendar names of the main entry as one file that imports nothing, so a `<script type="module">` loads it from the package as it ships. Read in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#names-in-prose) [4.198.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.147.0: the names elements share, and a text read once.** `find.shared` on the finder's answer lists every name two or more elements hold, each with its elements: `find` never answers such a name, and a tool can now say why it stays plain. And the work for one text now grows with its length alone, whatever the names: a 5,000-character name whose first word the text repeats took the TypeScript package about 1.5 seconds on 80,000 characters and now takes about a millisecond. No answer moves; the two packages share one file of cases, now 62. Ordinary prose costs the TypeScript package a quarter more to about twice as much, and about the same in Python.
- **[SDKs and clients](/docs/development/packages): the Godot and Unreal SDKs.** Both are public on GitHub at their first releases (Godot 0.1.1, Unreal 0.1.0), each with a first run that reads a sample world with no account. Each has a row on [SDKs and clients](/docs/development/packages) and on [Games](/docs/development/games#engines), with what it lacks: neither has a share yet, and Unreal's Blueprint nodes over the API answer JSON text. The Unity row's install now leads with OpenUPM.
