## 2026-11-23

- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.146.0: `read_folder` takes a file's own `type` only when it is one of the 22 types as the folder format writes it.** The reader took any non-empty word in lower case: `"type": "Location"` in `elements/character/` was served as a location, and `"type": "widget"` as an element of a type that does not exist. Each is now read as a character, the type of its folder, flagged `type_inferred`, its body left as the file wrote it. A type written as the format writes it still beats the folder: `"type": "location"` in `elements/character/` is a location. The TypeScript SDK's folder source already read it this way, and the two packages hold the same 21 cases.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.13.1: the start line goes in a file your tool reads.** 5.13.0 chose the file by which files stood in the folder, and for Claude Code that could be an `AGENTS.md` it does not read. The line now always goes in a file in the folder itself: `AGENTS.md` for most tools; for Claude Code, `AGENTS.md` when the session started with one loaded, and the folder's own `CLAUDE.md` otherwise. Where that would mean making a new `CLAUDE.md` beside or under an `AGENTS.md`, the AI says so and asks first. A folder that already holds the line keeps it.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.50.0: element names found in prose, and a share that carries only what the reader serves.** `OWNameFinder` lists every whole element name in a text, by the rule and the 47 shared cases of the TypeScript SDK's name finder. A world file made to hand on (`includeHidden: false`) carries under `elements/` and `spatial/` only a file the folder reader serves as an element with no hidden mark, and names every other file there in `UnreadLeftOut`. A number the reader cannot hold is refused by name (`OWNumberOutOfRangeException`), a file is one JSON value and nothing after it, and a save never writes over a file it could not read. The package is also on OpenUPM: `openupm add com.onlyworlds.sdk`.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.13.0: your next session starts in the world.** When `start` connects a folder to a world on onlyworlds.com, it offers one line for the file your AI tool reads at the start of every session there (`AGENTS.md`, or the folder's own `CLAUDE.md` for Claude Code), added on your yes; a later session then signs in to the world and reads its house rules, where it has any, before it answers, where until now nothing on disk told it the folder belonged to a world. Setup writes the same line for a world that lives online. And an AI handed an invite link fetches the page onlyworlds.com serves for it and does what that page says.
- **The docs are set in Lato.** Lato is the house font of OnlyWorlds since 21 November, and these pages are the first to wear it; until today they were set in Open Sans. Lato has no semi-bold, so the names and headings inside the figures are bold now. Nothing else on a page moved.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#writing-a-world-file) 4.197.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.145.0: a share carries under `elements/` and `spatial/` only what the folder reader serves.** A world file made to hand on (`includeHidden: false`, `include_hidden=False`) now asks the package's own folder reader which files are elements, and carries those with no hidden mark. Every other file there is left out and named apart from the hidden: a file with another extension, one deeper than a type folder (a hidden element's file moved into `old/` by hand), one directly under `elements/`, one in a folder that is no type, and an object with no `id`. An id that two files hold travels in neither. Until now a share carried a file with another extension, or one not directly in a type folder, whatever it held, and judged each file of a twice-held id by its own mark. A copy carries every such file byte for byte and no longer rewrites one for its author fields. Both readers now read names as written: `.json` and the type folder's name in lower case (the Python reader read both in any case), and ids compared as written (the SDK's [folder source](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) took one UUID in two spellings for one element); the folder source names a `.json` in another spelling among the skipped, as `extension`.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#writing-a-world-file) 4.196.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.144.0: a share leaves out a file it could not read as an element.** A world file made to hand on (`includeHidden: false`, `include_hidden=False`) now carries a file in a type folder only when the writer read it as an element and found no hidden mark. A file that is not JSON (a write cut short), is nested more than 200 deep, holds a number too large to hold or is not an object is left out too, and named apart from the hidden ones (`unreadLeftOut` and `unreadPaths` in the report; `unread_left_out=`). Until now a share carried a file that was no JSON, so a hidden element whose file was cut short travelled with its text. A copy carries such a file byte for byte, and the refusal 4.195.0 and 0.143.0 made earlier today is gone.
- **[AI agents](/docs/development/agents#what-you-can-get-back): what you can get back.** A new section for the owner who is about to give an AI write access: a delete and an overwrite happen at once and keep no earlier version, a read-only key or the **AI seats propose, a person accepts** setting limits an AI beforehand, and the owner restores a nightly file from the world's page, with what a restore puts back and what it leaves alone. A world's own delete a person cannot undo.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.143.0: a share is refused when a file it must read cannot be held.** `write_world_file(include_hidden=False)` in 0.142.0 could carry a hidden element whose file was nested more than 200 deep, with its author fields: the package's reader refused the file, and the writer took that for "no element". The writer now raises `ValueError` naming such a file and writes nothing, whenever it would have had to read it; a copy that leaves nothing out still carries every file byte for byte. The reader also refuses a number that is not finite (`1e400`, `NaN`), so the folder reader, the doctor and the Kanka importer skip that file and say so. If you share world files from folders you did not write, upgrade.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) 4.195.0: a file the package cannot hold is skipped or refused by name.** The folder source counted an element file nested 100,000 deep as served and then failed every read that held it, and served a number too large for a double as `null`; both files are now skipped, with `too_deep` or `number_out_of_range` in the read report. `writeWorldFile` throws a `TypeError` naming such a file when it must read it to leave out the hidden or the author fields, where it threw the runtime's error or wrote `null`. `convertKanka` skips such a file with a note, as the Python importer does.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.142.0: the doctor names what it could not read.** On a folder, the report now lists first each file it did not read as an element, with why (not JSON, no `id`, a second file of one id), and exits 1 when there is one. Until now it said nothing: with one element file cut off mid-write it reported the rest as the whole world and listed every link to the unread element as a link to a missing one. A file holding a value nested more than 200 deep is skipped the same way; on some platforms it used to stop the doctor and `pack` with a traceback.
- **Every page names [llms.txt](https://onlyworlds.github.io/llms.txt) in its footer.** Until now no page pointed at the site's index for AI agents, so an agent that read one page as text could not learn it exists. Each page's Markdown copy stays where it was, in the page's menu.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#deprecated-names): every deprecated name in one table.** The reference now lists each name the package's declarations mark as deprecated, 21 in 4.194.0, with the version it leaves in and the mark's own comment (what to use in its place, where there is one). The table is read from the pinned package at every build, so it follows each release, and the build stops on a mark it cannot put a name to.
- **[Stability and deprecation](/docs/development/api/stability#the-standard-the-packages-and-this-site): how a change to the standard reaches the API.** A motion that drops or renames a field falls under the same rule as any removal: the API keeps answering the old field until its removal has been announced six months ahead. A field a motion adds arrives without notice.
- **[The export file](/docs/development/api/changes#the-export-file): two sentences corrected against a downloaded file.** Its element bodies are the API's less `doors` and `hidden`, where the page said they were exactly what the API returns; and its `world` carries every key `GET /api/v2/world` serves but `counts`.
- **[Stability and deprecation](/docs/development/api/stability): a page of its own.** What can change under a tool built on the API: the policy in the API document's own words (additions without notice, a removal announced at least six months ahead), every change announced and not yet in effect (today one, the draught's compact default from 2027-05-02), and where an announcement appears. The [API overview](/docs/development/api-reference#stability) and the [Classic API](/docs/development/api/classic) page now point at it.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#giving-your-ai-access): the instructions for your AI say where its next session starts.** **Copy instructions for your AI** on a seat now also has the AI read the world's Charter when `GET /api/v2/me` names one in `front_door.charter`, and add one line to the file its tool reads at the start of each session (`AGENTS.md` for most tools, `CLAUDE.md` for Claude Code), once per world. A [new key](/docs/getting-started/keys)'s instructions gain the Charter sentence. The [join answer](/docs/development/agents#redeeming)'s `next` has one more sentence after "tell your human you joined": offer your human that line, and add it on their yes. [A team world](/docs/getting-started/team-world) says the same.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#names-in-prose) [4.194.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk) and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.141.0: element names found in prose.** `nameFinder` and `name_finder` read a world's element names once and find them in any text, each with where it starts and ends and the element it belongs to, so a page can mark the words that are elements. They find and never write. A whole name only, by a word edge that knows every script; the longest name where several start at one place; a name two elements share left plain; nothing read inside an address or a Markdown link; `case` (`exact` or `folded`) must be given. Both packages run one file of cases. [Addresses](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose) now says a plain name is not an address and where the name finder is.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles) open on all 19 in one place.** The page now starts with every profile in one line of what it lets a person or a tool do, in five groups (at a world's door, a world people and their AIs work in, reading and drawing, across tools and worlds, a world of another kind), says that none is required and that tools read them for you, and keeps each profile's builder section below.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)'s join page: a link that cannot be used says where your world is.** Under "This invitation cannot be used", a person who used the link themselves reads that the world is on their account page, with a link to it, or to sign in first. No route or API answer changed.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links): a person's invitation says what OnlyWorlds is and what an account asks.** Under its heading, what OnlyWorlds is and that a free account opens what was shared; under its button, what an email sign-up asks (an address, a username and a password, one confirming mail, no card); and its one-use line now says the link opens again, on any device, until you join ([onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)).
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) [v0.49.1](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/unity-sdk/releases/tag/v0.49.1): a draught left without `Compact` asks for the full packet, and a blank join code is refused before sending.** A patch release, no new API: the draught's answer no longer turns compact the day the server's default moves (2027-05-02), and `OWJoin.PreviewAsync`, `OWJoin.RedeemAsync` and `AcceptJoinAsync` throw for a code of white space alone, as the TypeScript SDK does since 4.191.0.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links): a person's invitation is joined on the site.** The join page no longer offers Open in Lantern and Open in Atlas on a link made for a person: under its button it says an account is free and brings them back to join, and the world is then on their account, which both apps list after sign-in (the link can still be pasted or shared into either app). An agent's link still offers Open in Atlas, and a player's both, Lantern first ([onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com), [Lantern](/docs/tools/lantern)). The API is unchanged.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) [4.193.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): `countsPath`.** The folder subpath's `countsPath(path)` says whether the folder source reads a file at all (`world.json`, element files under `elements/<type>/` and the legacy `spatial/`, nothing dot-prefixed, no swap file), the reader's own test, so a tool that watches a folder reads again only for files that count.
- **[onlyworlds.com's Changelog](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com) opens in plain words.** `/changelog` now shows each change by its title and one plain sentence (`?view=plain` still answers the same page), and `/changelog?view=full` shows every change's whole notes; it lists every release of each package, not only the newest hundred. The Atom feed and `/changelog.json` keep their shape.

## 2026-11-22

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) [4.192.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): what a world folder answers, and where it is not onlyworlds.com.** The folder section now says each read a folder answers, the one refusal and what it covers, and a list of where a folder's answer differs (offline elements last, a cursor is its source's own, no `410`, no ETag, which files are passed over, the declared type). `onRead`'s report lists each file served as `{ path, type, id, name }`; `folderMcpTools([])` serves no world, so a server started before a folder is added answers the schema tools and an empty `list_worlds`. [World folders](/docs/schema/folders#element-files) says what declares a type: exactly one of the 22 slugs as the standard spells them.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#the-mcp-tools-on-a-world-folder) [4.191.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): the MCP tools on a world folder.** `folderMcpTools` from `@onlyworlds/sdk/mcp` answers the MCP server's eight read tools from a world folder, with the same names, arguments and answers. Its invite calls refuse an empty code before sending ([Invite links](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#invite-links)), and a folder source's lists no longer slow with each page.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.49.0: a join preview carries the glimpse.** `OWJoinPreview.Glimpse` is up to five things of the world and the links among them, null for a guest's link, as the TypeScript SDK's 4.189.0 reads it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#a-world-folder-as-the-source) [4.190.0](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@onlyworlds/sdk): a world folder as the source.** `folderFetch` from `@onlyworlds/sdk/folder` lets the client read a world folder or a world file with the same calls as an online world, and no key; what a folder cannot answer is refused as `501` `not_in_a_folder`.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links): the invitation page shows its glimpse.** A link whose role reads the world opens on a small picture of up to five things of it and the links between them, before an account is made; a guest's link shows none, and the day a link expires reads as a date. Nothing on the API moved.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.48.0: the map-space frame reading, work items, and names in the profiles' order.** `OWMapSpace.CarryOf` carries a point on a child Map up to each Map above it; `OWPacket` reads the packet profile's work items; `OWProposal.House` names the house rules a proposal would change; `OWApiError.FieldsRefusal` says what a refused `fields` list held; `OWText.TrimAndFold` and `ByCodePoint` give the fold and the order the profiles define. Parity with the TypeScript SDK from 4.179.0 to 4.186.0.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.20.0: a note is an element wherever it sits, and your own properties sync.** A note with a UUID `id` and a type is an element in any folder of the world's folder; the first type folder on its path decides the type, and a note outside every type folder is read by its `type` property. Every other note is yours and never stops an upload. A property the type does not have stays in your note under its own name and is sent inside one key, `x_properties`, where before it went as `x_` plus its name and an edit never synced.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-glimpse): a join preview carries a glimpse of the world.** `POST /api/v2/join/preview` answers `glimpse`, always present: for a link whose role reads the world (contributor or co-builder), up to five things of it by name and type and the links among them, chosen from the world's strand or from its most linked thing; `null` for a guest's or a player's link, and when no two things a visitor could see are linked. It names no id. The [agents page](/docs/development/agents#preview-before-redeeming)'s example shows one. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.189.0 types it as `OwJoinGlimpse`. The old mobile app's two key routes under `/api/worldapi/mobile/` are gone and answer the API's `404`.
- **[API reference](/docs/development/api-reference#response-shapes): an answer that says nothing about being kept says `no-store`.** Every answer under `/api/` that sets no `Cache-Control` of its own now carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, errors, the `429` and the classic routes included; an answer that sets its own keeps it (the API document's `public, max-age=300`). A browser used to replay a kept `410` for an element that had been written again; it now asks. [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads) says so at the `410`.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#managing-members): an invite link carries a note.** A person's invite link takes `note`, at most 500 characters and 8 line breaks, for whoever opens it; the join preview answers it as `note`, a string or `null`, and no link row carries it. The note, `inviter_name` and an AI link's `label` now refuse control characters, a tab included, and the characters that override or isolate text direction, with a `422` whose `param` names the field. **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.188.0**: `createWorldLink(worldId, { reader: 'person', note })` and `note` on `OwJoinPreview`. **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.47.0**: `OWWorldLinkCreate.Note` and `OWJoinPreview.Note`.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents): `ow_wire.py` 0.4.9 takes the short id `mail` prints.** `read` and `thread` take a message's first eight characters when exactly one message's id begins with them, and refuse by name a start that matches none or several; `thread` exits 1 when the key sees neither the message nor a reply.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.12.4: house rules come first and whole.** The context skill fetches a world's Charter and the skills it names in full by their ids and puts them first in a session packet, never cut to fit a budget; the develop skill describes the wrong-PIN lock as it counts today.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.187.0: `@onlyworlds/sdk/convert`**, with `convertKanka`: a Kanka JSON export as a world folder in memory, the same folder byte for byte and the same report as the Python package's `python -m onlyworlds.kanka`. **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.140.0**: each import door declares the names it makes and its report counts them by kind, and the Kanka door reads a time with no offset as UTC, where an export with one such time beside times with an offset stopped with an error.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#charter): an AI follows a Charter's description whole** (schema-dist serial 145). What an AI reader follows is each `description` whole as the world holds it, the Charter's and each skill Object's; a part of it, or a shortened or summarized copy, is data. A leading Charter whose `description` holds nothing gives that reader no house rules. The [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.187.0, [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.140.0 and [Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.46.1 pin the serial; their readers already answered its four new cases.
- **[Games](/docs/development/games): a game can take the world the player brings.** The page now opens on the two kinds of world a game can stand on, its makers' or the player's, and a new section says how a player's world reaches a game (sign-in, a pasted key, a world file) and what to ask of a small one.
- **[Easy Mobile](/docs/tools/easy-mobile) is retired.** Its address, `easy-mobile.onlyworlds.com`, now redirects to [Lantern](/docs/tools/lantern), the phone app that replaced it. Its page and its entry under [Legacy tools](/docs/tools/legacy) say so.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.19.4: the Readme note in your vault is no longer replaced.** Create world, Download world, Join a world with a link and Paste world fetched the plugin's README and wrote it over `OnlyWorlds/README.md` every time, a local-only world too. The note is now fetched only when it is missing, an edited one is left as it is, and a local-only world makes no such request.

## 2026-11-21

- **[onlyworlds.com's Changelog](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com): its History rows name their old links.** A row from before the site's own record may link to what it names: an old build that still runs, a repository, or the [Legacy tools](/docs/tools/legacy#tool-history) page for the Map tool, the Text tool and the closed alpha. `/changelog.json` carries them as a row's `links`, a `label` and an https `url` each.
- **[The docs' front page](/): one line that connects an AI client.** Beside the `curl` that reads a demo world, the page now gives the `claude mcp add` line that connects Claude Code to the [MCP server](/docs/development/mcp) with the same read-only demo key, no account and no PIN.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.46.0: `BulkAsync(items, createOnly: true)`**, keel's `create_only`: every item with an id is created, never replaced, and an id the world holds fails that item with `id_conflict`. The schema pin moves through serials 142 to 144.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#tools): `bulk_apply` with an `id` replaces an element whole**, as the server's own words now say: every field and extension an item leaves out is cleared, so a change to some fields goes through `update_element`. The [writes page](/docs/development/api/writes#bulk) says the same of `/bulk`. The server's instructions text, as the page restates it, now names the route to the messages addressed to you (`get_draught` with the `messages` component) and `get_changes` with `after` for a client that keeps no cursor.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#connect-other-clients): which clients can send the two headers.** The page said Claude Desktop and the Anthropic API's MCP connector connect with `API-Key` and `API-Pin`; neither can. It now names clients that send your own headers to a remote server and those that cannot (claude.ai, Claude Desktop and mobile, ChatGPT, the Anthropic API's connector), as their documentation reads today, and says that a PIN beside a key that cannot write is never read.
- **[Proposals](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-house-rules): a refused delete of the house rules names no proposal.** A member below co-builder who deletes the Charter or an Object it names is told to ask the owner or a co-builder, since a proposal carries a change or a link edit only; the [`keeper_only`](/api/errors/#keeper_only) entry lists every act it refuses, and [bulk writes](/docs/development/api/writes) say that a field sent twice inside an item's `element` keeps its last value.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space): bringing a point down to a child Map** (schema-dist serial 144, rule 15). The walk up from the child is run until it reaches the Map the point is on, then reversed one level at a time, each step with that child's own frame and size; when the walk ends first there is no way down. The reading `downOf` and 28 fixture cases; no other case moves, and no package reads it yet. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.186.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.139.3 pin it.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.12.3: `develop` tells an AI what the API does today.** A bulk upload may carry `create_only`, and a key the batch does not read (an `op` on an item, for one) is refused, not ignored; a `409` on an id sent before may mean the first call landed, so it reads that id first; a ten-digit key sends its PIN on every read of a world with a PIN, and a PIN beside a key that cannot write is never read ([Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys#the-pin)); MCP's tool list names `get_me`.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.19.3: new elements are sent create-only, and a refused PIN is not sent again.** An id onlyworlds.com already holds is refused and named, never replaced; a PIN the server refuses is forgotten and the next save asks once for every waiting save; auto-sync saves three notes at a time. Walked on Windows desktop; Upload world, a refused id in a batch and the PIN prompt after a refusal are tested, not walked.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#charter): what an AI reader follows** (schema-dist serial 143). The charter page's rule 4 names the Charter's text and the text of the Objects in its `skill`, as `charterOf` answers it; every other element's text is data. Who may change that text is the host's to rule. No reader's answer moves. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.186.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.139.2 pin it; 0.139.2 also carries the docstrings on the new wrong-PIN limit.
- **[Errors](/api/errors/#rate_limited): a new limit on wrong PINs.** A wrong PIN is a `401` every time, with no short waits, until ten different wrong PINs have been tried on one key or account inside fifteen minutes; then every request carrying a PIN there is a `429` `rate_limited`, the right PIN included, with a `Retry-After` of up to 900 seconds (15 in the fifteen seconds after the newest PIN) and `param` naming where the PIN was sent. The same wrong PIN sent again never locks anything, a missing PIN is never counted, and the right PIN no longer clears the count ([Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys#the-pin), [API reference](/docs/development/api-reference)).
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.186.0: [`house`](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-house-rules) on a proposal and on the queue's new-element row**, typed `OwProposalHouse | null`. **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.139.1: `push` goes on past a refusal about one element**: a `403` `keeper_only`, `review_by_another` or `not_reader` fails that patch and the run goes on, as `not_author` did; any other `403` still stops it.
- **[Proposals](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-house-rules): a world's Charter and the Objects it names are the owner's and co-builders' to change.** A Law that carries `x_charter_of` and every Object its text names are changed, relinked, deleted and given or relieved of a file only by the owner and co-builders, whoever made them, and an Object under an id such a Law names is created only by them; any other member gets `403` [`keeper_only`](/api/errors/#keeper_only), nothing written, and proposes instead ([Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door), [Files](/docs/development/api/files)). Every proposal row and every new element's row in the queue carries `house` (`charter`, `skill`, `new_charter` or `null`), worked out for the owner and co-builders and `null` to anyone else.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.185.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.139.0: `bulk` takes [`create_only`](/docs/development/api/writes#bulk)** (`createOnly` in TypeScript): every item with an `id` is created, never replaced, and a held id answers its slot `409` `id_conflict`. Left out, the body is as before.
- **[Writes and bulk](/docs/development/api/writes#bulk): `create_only`, and a batch refuses what it does not know.** `"create_only": true` on a `/bulk` body creates every item with an `id` as `POST` does: an id the world already holds answers that item's `409` `id_conflict` and is not replaced. The body takes only `items`, `atomic` and `create_only`, an item only `type` and `element` (an `op` key, once ignored, is now refused); any other key, a key twice or a query parameter is a `422` `invalid_request` naming it, nothing written. [MCP](/docs/development/mcp#tools)'s `bulk_apply` takes `create_only` too.
- **[Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys#checking-the-pin): a PIN sent with a key that cannot write is never read.** A read key or a player's key answers as it would with no PIN, so a wrong one counts against no one ([`GET /me`](/docs/development/api/members) says so too).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.138.2: `push` never sends `atlas_baseline_unknown`**, the mark [Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) 1.124.30 keeps on the device for a copy with no record of what OnlyWorlds held; it stays out of every patch, as Atlas's other local-only fields do.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.184.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.138.1, and [Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space): a Zone outlined on a child Map and on its parent keeps two outlines** (serial 142). No reading joins them or adds a point on the edge of the child's rectangle or of its parent frame; what a tool draws there is the tool's. The map-space fixture holds 46 cases; no earlier answer moved.

## 2026-11-20

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.12.2: `update` reads back every write it makes.** Writing a session into a folder or through the MCP server, it reads what was there, writes, and reads back what changed; a difference you did not approve is told to you then, with the change that would put it back, and written under your change list. A cut-off update is picked up from its change list by the next session, and the skill no longer commits your world folder to git.
- **[LLM guide](/docs/development/llm-guide): `GET /api/v2/me` checks a PIN sent beside the key.** Its route list said the route checks the key and not the PIN; a PIN that comes with the request is checked, and a wrong one counts as a wrong PIN on a write does ([Checking the PIN](/docs/getting-started/keys#checking-the-pin)).
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.45.0** follows serials 134 to 141: `OWLayout.LayoutByLinks3d` lays a world out in three dimensions by its links on all 116 cases of its fixture, `OWLayout.UnplacedByKind`, `UnplacedAround` and `UnplacedByLinks3d` name the elements a saved layout map has no Pin for, a co-builder reads the world and the graph as a keeper (`OWLensReader.CoBuilder`), a contributor reads its own hidden creations, and `OWRoom.TalksOf`, `OWReport.TalksOf` and `OWCharter.DesignPartsOf` read the talks and the design parts. A folder it writes names 0.45.0 on its producer line.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.12.1: with MCP alone, an AI takes the Charter, and in a company or software-project world the skills it names, from the MCP server's `get_me`**, never from a list or a search. A world set up with 5.12.0 changes one sentence by hand, as each pack's `PACK.md` says ("A world set up before toolkit 5.12.1").
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#tools): `get_me`, the seventeenth tool.** It answers what `GET /api/v2/me` answers for the same key, the front door included: `front_door.charter` names the world's Charter and `charter_skill` the skills it names. The server's instructions now send an AI there for the Charter, never to a list or a search. [AI agents](/docs/development/agents) says an agent that speaks only MCP reads its Character there.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.12.0: online, an AI takes the house rules from the Charter its sign-in answer names** (`GET /me`, `front_door.charter`), never from a search, and a team AI's skills are the ones that Charter names. A work item says who asked for it (`x_packet_from`), and the packs say AI seat with the world page's control names. A world set up before keeps its skills; each pack's `PACK.md` says what to write in by hand.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-as-a-graph) 4.184.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.138.0, and [the graph](/docs/lenses/graph): a co-builder reads the graph as a keeper** (serials 140 and 141). The flags `hidden` and `proposed` are a keeper's, the owner's or a co-builder's, and a co-builder's graph without a lens counts the hidden and proposed elements it leaves out as the owner's does; `not_visible` and `anchor_not_visible` stay the owner's. Serial 141 adds cases only, and the charter page now says that folded Domain names compare by code point, as the [front door's](/docs/development/api/members#reading-aids) `domains` order does.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#managing-members): what the invite cap counts.** The 50 a world holds are pending email invites and person and player links that can still be used: a new person or player link counts them together, a new email invite counts the email invites alone, and AI links count with the AI seats.
- **[AI agents](/docs/development/agents): the world page and the API say AI.** On the world page the choice for inviting an AI is **An AI**, its button **Make an AI link**, and the narrator switch **AI seats narrate as themselves**; the API document calls a seat an AI seat and its link an AI link, and the narrator `422` reads "an AI seat's Narrative". The [members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links), [reads](/docs/development/api/reads), [writes](/docs/development/api/writes#agent-writes), [draught](/docs/development/api/draught) and [onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com) pages, the [errors page](/api/errors/#proposed_only) and the team world guide use the new names; Recent changes marks a seat's change "by Archivist, an AI". Nothing on the wire changed: a link's `reader` is still `agent`, and the doors are still `seat`, `mcp` and `declared`.

## 2026-11-19

- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) 1.124.23 to 1.124.26: more of Atlas works from the keyboard, and each control says its name one way.** Tab reaches the names on link and Referenced by chips, field sections, the element image toggle and chart cards; in the Maps tab a map's name is its own button beside its fold, and a fold is named for what it folds. While sync runs, the pill, its tooltip and the refresh button all say Saving or Fetching.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-child-map-on-its-parent) 4.183.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.137.0, and [Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space): a child Map's place on its parent** (serial 139). A Map's `x_parent_frame` says where it lies on the Map its `parent_map` names, a box in the parent's pixels, and `carryOf` / `carry_of` carry a point on a child Map up that chain, saying why the walk ended. The fixture's 66 `frame_cases` hold the rule in both packages.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-laid-out-in-3d) 4.182.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.136.0: the 3D layout lays out every piece of a world, and a co-builder rung.** `layoutByLinks3d` / `layout_by_links_3d` now lay out every piece of linked elements, the pieces beside the main one on a ring round it, and answer an element linked to no other and pinned on no map in a new `unplaced` instead of a shelf; `BY_LINKS_3D_VERSION` is 2, so a tool that kept points under 1 lays the world out fresh. The lens readers take the rung [`co-builder`](/docs/development/typescript-readers#what-every-reader-takes), and a contributor's view holds the hidden elements it made. Both packages read schema-dist serials 137 and 138.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#default-layouts): `by-links-3d` is a default layout, and a Map's whole-number size is read as written** (serials 137 and 138). The 3D method's page and its fixture of 116 cases are published beside `by-kind` and `around`. In [map space](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space) a `width` or `height` that is a whole number, 0 and under 0 included, is answered as written, and a size measured on a Map with no placed point of its own is `null`. Serial 138 writes the hidden mark's reach into the lens, charter, report and packet pages: a keeper (the owner or a co-builder) and the element's maker see a hidden element, no one else does, with new cases on each fixture.
- **[Changes](/docs/development/api/changes#the-feed): an element hidden while a page is read stays out of it.** A contributor, a guest or the public reader is no longer served the body of an element hidden while its page was being read. The [errors page](/api/errors/#seat_above_sponsor)'s `seat_above_sponsor` no longer mentions a seat made for an address, and the [limits](/docs/development/api-reference#limits) snapshot carries the request budget's new description of what it counts.
<!-- toolkit v5.11.6: no docs change (the packs' skills follow serials 135 and 136, the standing work item, done only from open or taken, the design state on any Object or Construct; the profile and reader pages already say so, and the toolkit page restates no pack wording) -->
- **[Changes](/docs/development/api/changes#the-feed): a page's `head` is read before the page.** A page now holds only the changes up to the world's change sequence as it began to be read, and its `head` is that number, so a write made while a page was being read is never skipped: it comes on a later read from the page's cursor. An element deleted while a page is read is left out of it, with its `delete` on this page or later, where such a page could answer `500` before.
- **[Limits](/docs/development/api-reference#limits): what the request budget does not count.** A browser's `OPTIONS` preflight is never counted, and a read of the Classic API's metadata routes with an account token is counted, as one with no key is.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers#talks) 4.181.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.135.0: schema-dist serials 135 and 136.** `talksOf` / `talks_of` answer the talk on an Object or a Construct that a line in the room is in, `reportTalksOf` / `report_talks_of` the talks a report is shown with, and `designPartsOf` / `design_parts_of` the [design parts](/docs/development/typescript-readers#reading-aids) a reader reads where it finds a Charter. In the TypeScript SDK only, [work items](/docs/development/typescript-readers#work-items): `workStateOf`, `isOpenWork`, `askerOf` and `workOf` read a work item's state (now four, with `standing`), whether it is open work, and who asked.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles): a standing work item, who asked, the talk on an element, and design parts** (serials 135 and 136). A work item's state may be `standing`, a kept order of work that is never open work, and `x_packet_from` names who asked. A line in the room that names an Object or a Construct is in the talk on it, and in a world with a Charter an Object or a Construct whose subtype is `in design` or `locked` is a design part.
- **[The site's pages have a limit of their own](/api/errors/): 250 a minute for one address.** Past it a page of onlyworlds.com answers a plain-text `429` with `Retry-After`, not the API's error shape. A world's public page and the API keep their limits.
- **[Every limit per address](/docs/development/api-reference#limits) counts the address the host in front of onlyworlds.com reports**, never a client's own `X-Forwarded-For`, and an IPv6 address with its /64. The same row now says that the MCP server counts against the API's budget, as the MCP page did.
- **[`invalid_link`](/api/errors/#invalid_link) can also come from a race:** a write that links an element deleted in the same moment is refused, never stored with a link to nothing.
- **[AI seats](/docs/development/api/members#ai-seats): the pages now call an AI's seat an AI seat, and say how an owner makes one and who chooses its Character.** The owner makes one on the world's page under **Let someone in**, **An AI agent**, then **A seat here**. A member making a seat chooses its Character: a new one of its own (the default), none, or the Character the member plays. The owner can set it afterwards, except on a seat that follows its member.

## 2026-11-18

- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants) 4.180.0: `trimAndFold` and `byCodePoint` compare and order names the way the profiles do.** `trimAndFold` trims Unicode's White_Space and folds case by Unicode 15.1's simple case folding. A byte order mark stays, which JavaScript's `trim()` strips, and the long s folds to s, which `toLowerCase()` leaves. `byCodePoint` sorts by code point, never by UTF-16 unit or a locale. A tool that compared names with the language's own functions answered differently on some names.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) 1.124.0 to 1.124.22: a write the web host refuses is said for what it is, and more of Atlas works from the keyboard.** A change the host refuses waits in its own words while the rest of the world syncs, where it read as a wrong PIN and stopped every change; a name it refuses gets advice that fits a name. An element's fields, aliases and links, the sync pill, map tabs, the pane dividers and page blocks work from the keyboard. Links in your writing are read by the [address rules](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses), a list of links names each element once, and new world folders are written at format 0.3.22.
- **[A message sent with your account token](/docs/development/agents#in-world-messages) can link the relations, objects and constructs it is about.** The send route takes `relations`, `objects` and `constructs`, each up to 200 ids of the world you can read; they address no one, and any other id is a `422` naming it.
- **[The draught's `via`](/docs/development/api/draught#the-packet) says what a work item reached through a Title names.** In `work`, `via` `holders` alone is an item that came to the Character only through a Title they hold, whose `holders` name them; `by_role` names the Titles. The API document says so; the page now does too.

## 2026-11-17

- **[A write the host refuses](/api/errors/) never reaches a browser page on another site.** The host's Blocked page comes without CORS headers, so a browser hides it and `fetch` fails as a network error: `isHostBlocked` names it outside a browser only. The errors page says so.
- **[The strand](/docs/schema/profiles#strand): an empty string in any member is no mark, and nothing is trimmed. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.179.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.134.1 move to schema-dist serial 134.** The profile page said an empty `narrative` was a mark whose slots are all empty; it is no mark, as an empty `event` or `location` already was, and as both packages already read it. A member of white space alone (a space, a tab, an ideographic space) or an id with a space on it is a string that names no element: its slot is empty, and the strand's other slots still fill. The fixture holds these as 42 cases, from 29. No reader's answer moves. The same serial says what the checks profile's specimen is and, on the lens profile, that the reader is the one who asks.
- **[A Character's context read](/docs/development/api/draught#the-policy) adds your starting lenses only on your own Character.** A lens frame in the packet is your own reading and your own Character's mail, never theirs. The plain read (no parameter, or `compact` alone) added a frame for each lens you start with on any Character you read; it now adds them only on the Character `GET /api/v2/me` names as yours, and none on any other. `lens=<id>` still adds its frame on any Character. If you read someone else's Character with the plain read and used `lenses`, ask for each lens by id. The pages also say that your starting lenses are the ones you chose or, for a seat that never chose, the ones its Titles give it.
- **[A `fields` list with unknown names](/docs/development/api/reads#expansion-and-sparse-fields) is refused once, with all of them named: [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.179.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.134.0.** A read whose `fields` names fields the element type does not have was a `422` that named the first. It is still one `422` with `param` `fields`; its message now lists every unknown name, and the error carries `unknown_fields`, `types_with_field` (for each one another element type has, those types) and `allowed_fields` (the names this type takes). `err.fieldsRefusal` and `ApiError.fields_refusal` return the three as served, and the [errors page](/api/errors/#invalid_request) says so. A list with one unknown name keeps its message.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.44.0** follows serials 132 and 133 and the API's newer answers: each entry of the reading aids and each filled slot of the strand carries its `Type`, `OWFrontDoor.CharterElement` and `CharterSkillElements` give the Charter and its skills as elements, `OWSeat` reads whether a seat may change its Character (`MayChangeCharacter`, `MayChangeCharacterNote`, `FollowsSponsor`), and `OWApiError.IsHostBlocked` names a write the web host refused before the API saw it. A folder it writes names 0.44.0 on its producer line.

- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#install) 4.178.1: installing it into a JavaScript project no longer installs TypeScript, and `@onlyworlds/sdk/waymark` has types on the older module resolution.** TypeScript was a required peer of the package, so npm placed the newest compiler beside it in every project, used or not; it is optional now. And a TypeScript project with `moduleResolution` set to `node` got "Cannot find module" for the `/waymark` import while the package's root resolved; that import now finds its types there too. Projects on `node16`, `nodenext` or `bundler` read what they read before. No call changes.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#sign-in-with-onlyworlds): on Node 18 the sign-in needs one flag.** The SDK's tests now run on Node 18 as well as 20 and 22, and the Python package's on 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14, the oldest versions each admits included. On Node 18 everything passes but Sign in with OnlyWorlds, which needs Web Crypto as a global: Node 18 has it only when started with `--experimental-global-webcrypto`, and without it `signInWithOnlyWorlds` refuses with a `TypeError` that names Web Crypto.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python-import#pages) 0.133.1: the LegendKeeper importer says what each of its two roads leaves unread.** Through LegendKeeper's API an image inside a page does not come in (their API answers it as unsupported, with nothing in it), and the report now says that the export file holds it and that `--file` reads it; from the export file an image is a link to LegendKeeper's host, never copied. The file road names any key of a document's content it does not read. The message for a write the web host refused names the whole way out, in the TypeScript SDK's words.
- **[A Message read in a Character's voice](/docs/development/api/draught#the-packet) is served as they would read it.** A context read with `mode=voice` and `messages` named holds, beside the Messages their own reading holds, each Message your view shows that names them and is not hidden from them. Such a Message was served as you read it, so one about them addressed to someone else carried the addressee and every element it links to. Now its links to elements outside their reading are left out, and `x_served_trimmed` says when an addressee or the sender was cut: an owner or a co-builder [meets that mark](/docs/development/agents#in-world-messages) when reading as someone else, never in their own reading. Which Messages are gathered does not change, `mode=act` serves your own reading as before, and the MCP's `get_draught` reads the same. When the Character's own element is hidden from them, `via` still names the field that names them while the body serves that link `null`.
- **[Links](/docs/development/api/links#setting-and-clearing-links): a `null` entry stored in a list of links is left out of what a read serves.** A write has refused one since 2026-11-13; one stored before then no longer shows in an element read or in the MCP's link edits, and `expand` on such a list answers instead of failing.
- **[The Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.19.2 moves to [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.176.0** and changes nothing else it does, with one exception: when the web host refuses making an account world, the notice says what happened, where it read "OnlyWorlds API error 403".
- **[The Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#sync) 3.19.1: a save the web host refuses says why in one line.** The notice names the host's refusal and its HTTP status, and for a page titled Blocked the known cause (a command such as `python -m` or `curl` directly after a backtick, a semicolon or a pipe) and what to write in its place. Before, the notice held the host's whole page. The note stays unsaved and nothing reaches the server.

- **[The docs' front page](/) and `llms.txt` open with what a builder types**: `npm install @onlyworlds/sdk`, `pip install onlyworlds`, the API's base and the [world folder](/docs/schema/folders) that needs no account. Neither page named a package before; a build check holds the lines.
- **[The draught's work says what is theirs by role](/docs/development/api/draught): [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.178.0.** Every row of `work` carries `by_role`, the ids of the Character's Titles that the work item names, and a row that reached them only through a Title has `via` `["holders"]`. Read as the Character (`voice`) a work item still reaches them only through a field of its own, so an agent asks with `mode=act` for its work by role. A work item for their role that also names them in a field of its own now sits in `work`, where it used to sit in `names` or `room`. The MCP's `get_draught` answers the same.
- **[The Blocked page](/api/errors/) is in the API document**, and the errors page follows its words: the same command also passes behind a dollar prompt or with no backtick, semicolon or pipe directly before it, and the rule is the host's and can change. The SDK's message says so from 4.178.0.
- **[The reading aids say each element's type](/docs/development/api/members#reading-aids) (serial 133): [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.177.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.133.0.** The Charter, the Guide, each Domain and every filled slot of the strand carry `type` beside `id` on `GET /me` and an agent's join reply, and in `charterOf` and `strandOf` (`charter_of` and `strand_of` in Python): the word the element routes use, so a reader handed only the packet knows which route fetches each. A Domain's hub is an element of the kind its field links to. The charter fixture holds 47 cases and the strand fixture 29.

- **[Where to reach us](/docs/contact) no longer lists GitHub Discussions**, and the 22 element pages no longer link their Discussions category. Nobody was reading that board; a question or a request goes to an issue on the repository it is about, or to [Feedback](https://www.onlyworlds.com/feedback).
- **[An owner can give an AI a Character without the right to change it](/docs/development/agents#after-joining).** `may_change_character` `false` on the member's `PATCH` gives the Character and withholds the right; `true` gives the right back. Sending the same Character again no longer restores a cleared right, and the members list's note now also says why a seat can change its Character all the same. A co-builder's [adopt](/docs/development/api/members#created_by) of a Character an AI plays is `403 not_author`: the owner changes who made it.
- **[The front door types its ids](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door): TypeScript SDK 4.176.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.132.1.** `charter_element` and `charter_skill_elements` give the Charter and its skills as `{type, id}`, so a reader knows which route fetches each.
- **[A write the host refused](/api/errors/): [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.175.0's `isHostBlocked` and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.132.0's `is_host_blocked`.** The host in front of onlyworlds.com answers some writes with a 403 HTML page titled "Blocked" before the API sees them: text that holds a shell command such as `python -m` right after a backtick, a semicolon or a pipe. Both packages now say so, and how to write the command so it passes, where they showed "OnlyWorlds API error 403" or the page's own markup.
- **[A Character given to an AI stays when the AI is removed](/docs/development/agents#after-joining).** Removing a seat deletes the Character it plays only while it is as the join left one: made by the join, named as the seat, supertype `Agent` and nothing else, last written by the join, linked from nothing and played by no one else. A Character given to the seat to play, edited or linked to since, or played by someone else too, stays.
- **[When a 3D layout's points have moved](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-laid-out-in-3d): TypeScript SDK 4.174.0.** `BY_LINKS_3D_VERSION` is raised exactly when a release moves an answer of `layoutByLinks3d`, so a tool that keeps a picture to hand back as `held` can tell when to lay the world out fresh. No point of the method moves.
- **[What a saved 3D layout does not place](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-laid-out-in-3d): TypeScript SDK 4.173.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.131.0.** `unplacedByLinks3d` and `unplaced_by_links_3d` answer the ids of the graph's nodes that a saved `by-links-3d` layout map has no Pin for. The method's shared fixture gains eight cases and a suite of eight for this reading; no point of the method moves.
- **[Your seat list](/docs/development/agents#making-a-seat) says whether each seat may change its Character.** `GET /api/v2/account/worlds/{world_id}/seats` serves `may_change_character`, `may_change_character_note` and `follows_sponsor` on every row, in the words of the owner's [members list](/docs/development/api/members#agent-seats). Where a seat's Character is kept from you, the row reads `character` `null`, `may_change_character` `false` and no note. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.172.0 types the three on `listSeats()`'s rows.
- **[A world laid out in 3D](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-laid-out-in-3d): TypeScript SDK 4.171.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.130.1.** A Map whose width or height is under 0 no longer stops the layout: a width or height that is not over 0 makes the Map no plate, and the world is laid out by its links. A layout that hands earlier points back no longer works out the push between two elements that both stand still, so keeping a picture still costs less than laying it out from nothing; no point of any answer changes. The page now says how to count what is new: by the ids you did not hand back.
- **[A world laid out in 3D](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-laid-out-in-3d): TypeScript SDK 4.171.0's `layoutByLinks3d` and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.130.0's `layout_by_links_3d`.** One method in both packages lays a whole graph out in three dimensions by its links, with the world's map as a flat plate, and held points keep a picture still as the world grows. Its word, `by-links-3d`, is the tool's own until its page in the standard is published.
- **[The default layouts are a profile](/docs/schema/profiles#default-layouts) (serial 132).** The two methods that draw a world nobody has laid out, `by-kind` and `around`, are written out in the standard, with a fixture of 17, 10 and 15 cases: the same graph gives the same points in every tool and language. A method's word is its version. The [map-space profile](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space)'s rule on layout maps now ends on what reading may do: reading or drawing a layout map changes none of its Pins, and laying it out again is a write like any other.
- **[What a saved layout does not place](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-worlds-default-picture): TypeScript SDK 4.170.0's `unplacedByKind` and `unplacedAround`, and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.129.0's `unplaced_by_kind` and `unplaced_around`**, answer the ids a layout method would place that a saved layout Map has no Pin for, so a tool can say how many elements a world has gained since the layout was saved. Both packages now replay the profile's own fixture from the standard's distribution.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.43.1** follows serials 130 and 131: the pin and the fixtures move (the room page's exact case fold, a value that is no object read as no element, a body that is not a message never unknown). No reader changes; a folder it writes names 0.43.1 on its producer line.
- **[Who an AI's seat answers to](/docs/development/agents#the-human-sponsor), said for a link an AI made.** A seat from a join link answers to the person who made the link, and for a link an AI made, to the person who answers for that AI; it draws on that person's token rating and image storage. On the owner's [members list](/docs/development/api/members#agent-seats) the note on a seat's right to change its Character has two more states: a seat handed over after its right was cleared, and a Character that is hidden. A member can now [pick their own Character](/docs/development/api/members#picking-your-character) on their page for the world, as well as through the API. Nothing changes who may do what.

## 2026-11-15

<!-- toolkit v5.10.3: no docs change (a knowledge rule for secrets in the skills and the draw skill on serial 128; no page here states either) -->
- **[A member's AI changes the Character it plays](/docs/development/agents#after-joining) only while the member could.** When the owner sets the Character of a seat a member answers for, the seat may change that Character only if the member is a co-builder or made it; lowering the member takes the right away, and saving the seat's Character again gives it back once they could. On the owner's members list an agent row carries `may_change_character_note`, a sentence saying why a seat may not ([Agent seats](/docs/development/api/members#agent-seats)).
- **[A world's graph page](/docs/development/api/public) draws the picture of up to 3,000 elements.** For a larger world the page shows its list where the picture would be, and says the packages draw a larger one ([`layoutByKind`](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-worlds-default-picture) has no such limit). Maps, Pins and Markers are not counted.
- **One case folding for every reader (serial 131).** [The room profile](/docs/schema/profiles) now says what "without regard to case" is: simple case folding by Unicode's own table at 15.1, never a language's lower-casing. A supertype written `MEſſAGE` (two long s) is the word `message`; `Meßage` is not. The API's [`supertype` and `subtype` filters](/docs/development/api/reads#filters) and the MCP's `list_elements` compare the same way from today, where the database had its own rule. A value that is not an object (`null`, a list, a string) is no element to any message or room reader, and none fails on it. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.169.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.128.2 follow: the TypeScript readers already answered every new case, and the Python readers no longer raise on such a value.
- **Old addresses of these docs lead somewhere again.** The site was reorganised several times, and search engines still list pages from the older layouts (`/docs/specification/…`, `/docs/getting-started/parsing-and-migrating`, `/docs/tool-directory/…`), which answered 404. Each such address now sends you to the page that replaced it: the old element pages to [the schema's](/docs/schema/), the parsing guides to [the toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit), the old tool pages to [the tools](/docs/tools/).
- **[An address on an agent link is a label](/docs/development/agents#the-human-sponsor), never a binding.** An email address typed in **For** is no longer matched to an account: the agent answers to the person who made the link and uses their budget, whoever holds the address, and the seat shows the address as who it was made for. The owner hands the seat to that person once they are a member. Such a seat is raised like any other, up to the role of the person who answers for it, which replaces the line below about a seat that still carries its email. Its [front door](/docs/development/api/members#who-am-i) names the link's maker as `sponsor`, and it is listed in their `my_seats`.
- **[An AI seat that plays your Character follows you](/docs/development/agents#the-human-sponsor), and no AI is set above its person.** A seat a member made with `character` `mine` plays what that member plays and may change it only while they may; the owner's members list marks it `follows_sponsor`, and setting its Character is `422` [`seat_follows_sponsor`](/api/errors/#seat_follows_sponsor) until the seat is handed over. A `role` above the person who answers for a seat is `422` [`seat_above_sponsor`](/api/errors/#seat_above_sponsor); a `sponsor` sent with a `role` is now set first, so one request can hand a seat to the owner and raise it.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.43.0: a report's lines.** `OWReport.ReadLines` and `ReadLine` read a report's `consequences` as the report profile's rule 10 does, beside the TypeScript and Python readers.
- **[The AI door](/docs/development/ai)'s `lens` use answers `charged`**: true when the call's spend was recorded, so a retry under the same `Idempotency-Key` replays it and bills nothing; its refusals carry it too.
- **[A message read with no key](/docs/development/typescript-readers) (serial 130)**: a body that is not a message (a Story, a Note) has no recipients, so `hasUnseenRecipients` is false for it with a key or without; only a message read with no key answers unknown. The [message profile](/docs/schema/profiles/messages) says so. TypeScript SDK 4.169.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.128.1 carry the serial; both already answered this way.
- **[A report's lines](/docs/development/typescript-readers#report-lines) (serial 129): TypeScript SDK 4.169.0's `readReportLines` and `readReportLine`, and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.128.0's `read_report_lines` and `read_report_line`**, read a line of a report's `consequences` as the [report profile](/docs/schema/profiles#report)'s rule 10 says: where the text breaks into lines, which address is the line's, and which words are shown with it taken out. Both are held to the profile's second fixture, `report-lines.fixture.json`.
