- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.40.1: an address glued to a word is prose** (serial 127): `OWAddress.ReadLinkAt` finds no address that begins inside a word, as `readOwLinkAt` reads it; the folder writer's producer line says 0.40.1.
- **[onlyworlds.com's Changelog](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com) reads in plain words too**: `/changelog?view=plain` shows each change by its title and one plain sentence, and `/changelog.json` carries that sentence as `plain`. The site's changes carry one from today; the rest show their title alone.
- **[A world's default picture](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-worlds-default-picture)**: TypeScript SDK 4.167.0's `layoutByKind` and `layoutAround`, and the [Python package](/docs/development/python) 0.127.0's `layout_by_kind` and `layout_around`, lay out a world nobody laid out from its graph. The points are the same in every browser and in both languages.
- **[An AI that joins by link](/docs/development/agents#making-a-link) is seated at most at its sponsor's role** on the day it joins: a link made when the sponsor stood higher seats it at the lower role. The preview and the join answer say the role granted.
- **[A multi-link names each element once](/docs/schema/folders) (serial 128).** A reader served a list that names one element twice reads it once, in its first place: [the graph](/docs/lenses/graph) draws one edge, and [only a message has recipients](/docs/schema/profiles/messages), so a Story's `characters` address nobody. [Addresses found in a text](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose) do not overlap. Folders are written at format 0.3.24. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.166.0 and [Python package](/docs/development/python) 0.126.0 read it so.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers) 4.165.1 and 4.165.2: `findOwLinks` reads a line of `ow://` starts that hold no address faster**, whether a start has no id or an id after a word that is not a type. No answer changes.
- **[An address in prose](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose) begins only outside a word (serial 127).** An `ow://` glued to a letter, a combining mark or an underscore before it holds no address (`elbow://…`); a hyphen, a digit, a bracket or a quote before it does. TypeScript SDK 4.165.0 and Python package 0.125.0 read it so, and each finds every address in a text in order: [`findOwLinks`](/docs/development/typescript-readers) and [`find_ow_links`](/docs/development/python).
- **[The AI door](/docs/development/ai): a fault after the answer was charged replays under its key.** A `500` after the charge is kept like a billed refusal, so the same key and body answer it again and never run or bill a second time.
- **[CORS](/docs/development/api/cors#origins): the API answers a browser page on any origin**, a page opened from disk (`Origin: null`) included, with the request's own origin echoed and never `*`; the key decides what a request may do, and credentials stay off. The list of hosting platforms is gone from the page: it now governs only the site's own pages. [Listing your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool) no longer asks a tool on its own domain to check CORS first.
<!-- toolkit v5.10.2: no docs change (its deployment page's CORS lines say what the CORS page says; its draw and read scripts read an empty room or charter mark as none, which the profiles page already says) -->
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.40.0: an address found in prose, a read-only sign-in, and three fields of a changes row.** `OWAddress.ReadLinkAt` reads [an address in prose](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose) as `readOwLinkAt` does; `SignInWithOnlyWorlds(clientId, redirectUri, OWSignIn.ReadScope)` asks a [read-only token](/docs/development/api/signin#a-read-only-token), with `OWSignInResult.Scope` and `OWAccountToken.Scope`; `OWChange` reads `UpdatedAt`, `DeletedAt` and `Reason`. The folder writer's producer line says 0.40.0.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#a-read-only-token): a read-only token.** A tool that only reads asks `scope=world:read` on the authorize request; the answer says `"scope": "world:read"`, and every write with the token but its own revoke is `403` `permission_error`. The [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.164.0 takes `scope: 'world:read'` and answers the scope granted.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.39.3: an empty list or object is no room or charter mark** (schema-dist serial 126), so such an element shows no view banner; the folder writer's producer line says 0.39.3.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.163.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.124.1: the room and charter readers' descriptions say an empty list or object is no mark**, as 4.163.0 and 0.124.0 already read it. No code changes.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles): an empty list or an empty object is no room or charter mark** (schema-dist serial 126): `x_room_of` and `x_charter_of` set to `[]` or `{}` read as absent, as the review mark already reads its own, so a full and a compact read of one element agree; `false` still counts. The checks profile's `present` reads an empty object as short. The [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers) 4.163.0 and the [Python package](/docs/development/python) 0.124.0 read it so.
- **[Layout maps](/docs/schema/profiles): empty-string ids and links written down** (serial 126): a row without a non-empty string id is no element, and an empty-string link is null; the fixture holds 26 layout cases beside 39. Every published reader already answered so.
- **[Addresses](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose): what follows a world in prose.** `/<type>/<id>` continues the address only when the type word is one of the 22 in lowercase; an upper-case or misspelt type word leaves the world's own address (serial 126's four prose cases).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.124.0: the pack and the OKF export read an in-prose type word as the address profile does**, lowercase and one of the 22; an id is still read as written.
- **[Changes](/docs/development/api/changes#the-export-file): the export example carries `schema_version` 00.34.01**, the standard since 2026-11-13.

## 2026-11-13

<!-- sdk v4.160.2: no docs change (list's doc comment names its parameters; the TypeScript reference prints it from the pinned release) -->
- **[Map](/docs/schema/element_categories/map): `hierarchy` says which maps it orders** (the standard 00.34.01, serial 124): the maps with the same `parent_map`, without one the maps of the same `location`, without either the maps of the world itself; lower is deeper or earlier, and nesting is said by `parent_map` and `location`. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.162.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.123.0 carry the version.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.19.0: the relationship canvas places the notes that link to yours in order of their path**, the same in every vault; the plugin runs on SDK 4.161.0.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#the-address): a world's id is read only in its written form.** `onlyworlds.com/w/<id>` and `?world=` take the id with its four hyphens; 32 hex digits without them are an address, which a page may take, and no longer open the world. A key that names its own world that way answers `422`.
- **[An address in prose](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#an-address-in-prose) (serial 123).** A reader that finds an address inside text reads it from its prefix; a full stop, another query key or a fragment after it is prose. After `/w/` the run is read whole: exactly an id's form is the world's id, 32 hex digits without hyphens are a slug. TypeScript 4.161.0's [`readOwLinkAt`](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) and Python 0.122.0's [`read_ow_link_at`](/docs/development/python) find one.
- **[Messages](/docs/schema/profiles/messages): the read mark among several** is the one with the newest readable `updated_at`; at the same instant, or when none can be read, the higher id (serial 123).
- **[Lenses](/docs/lenses): `changed_within` reads each element's clock**, the newest readable of `updated_at`, `server_updated_at` and `local_updated_at`, and never cuts an element with no readable clock (serial 123).
- **[Records](/docs/schema/profiles#record): `played_at` is read by the message profile's time grammar.** A lower-case `t` and `z`, an offset and no zone read; a time without its seconds is no record (serial 123).
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas): a folder on your drive needs desktop Chrome, Edge or Brave.** The page said Chrome, Edge or Brave without the word desktop; [the toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit)'s README says desktop since 5.10.1.
- **[Links](/docs/development/api/links#try-it-on-the-practice-world): try it on the practice world.** Three Run buttons in order make a Location, a Character who lives there, and add the Character to the Location's `founders`; each fills in the ids the ones above it made.
- **[Tools](/docs/tools/): the OnlyWorlds mapper is listed.** A world's maps and layouts in the browser, read only, at [maps.onlyworlds.com](https://maps.onlyworlds.com); an element's link opens it on every map it is on.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#reference) 4.160.3: the reference names `list`'s parameters** (`limit`, `cursor`, `filter` and the rest) in its row.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#guests): a guest, a player and a Character's AI see the Titles she holds and the Relations she acts in.** A Title whose `holders` names her Character and a Relation whose `actor` is it are hers to read, as an element naming her in `characters` is; hiding still keeps either from her, and the `characters` filter is unchanged. The [draught](/docs/development/api/draught)'s `role` in `voice` now carries them too.
- **Run buttons on [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#filters) and [Writes](/docs/development/api/writes): press Run and a sample sends its request from your browser, with its answer shown under it.** A read runs with the public demo key it shows; the Location create runs on the practice world and offers to delete what it made. The search example on Reads now asks for three results, the third found in a description. The [worked example](/docs/schema/example), [Changes](/docs/development/api/changes) and [Files](/docs/development/api/files) carry Run on their demo-key reads too.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.39.1: ids compared as written, on the newest schema distribution.** A Charter's skills and the Open with check read an id held in the world exactly as it is written, the same rule the TypeScript and Python packages follow; the tools list it carries names Windrose. No call is added or changed; an export's `producer` line says 0.39.1.
- **[Your first hour](/docs/getting-started/first-hour): a new page that takes you from nothing to a working read and write, with no account.** Read Moppetopia and count it, write two linked elements to the practice world, change one, read it back and delete both, in TypeScript, Python or curl; then what the rest of these docs cover, for later. The home page, Getting started and both package pages link it first.
- **[World folders](/docs/schema/folders#folders-and-onlyworldscom): the hidden mark stated on the page**, and the folder format's rules quoted where they apply instead of cited by section, on the folders, Python, TypeScript and Unity pages.
- **[Getting started](/docs/getting-started#3-write): a first write needs no account.** The Write step names the practice world's key and PIN, and says that the server mints an id when you send none while both packages mint it on your side.
- **[Writes](/docs/development/api/writes): the practice world, for a write without an account.**
- **[Home](/): Moppetopia's demo key in the quickstart, and the practice world for a first write.**
- **[Packages](/docs/development/packages#plain-rest): the plain-REST sample sends the PIN only on its write.** A read sends the key alone.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#read-a-world): counts, one element, a world as files, and a delete.** `get_world()["counts"]`, `get`, `export_world` with `read_folder`, and in the Write example a read back and a `delete`, whose read afterwards is a 410 `gone`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#read-a-world): counts and one element**, and on [Errors](/docs/development/typescript#errors) the 410 `gone` after a delete.
- **[Worlds](/docs/schema/worlds) and [World folders](/docs/schema/folders): a world's name.** onlyworlds.com refuses an empty name on a write; a folder may hold one.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.160.1: the Waymark draws the OnlyWorlds mark in true curves.** The mark is the logo redrawn with exact arcs, so it stays clean at any size; the same drawing is now this site's logo and favicon.
- **[Links](/docs/development/api/links#setting-and-clearing-links): a list of links keeps each element once, and a `null` entry is refused.** A write whose list names one element twice stores it once, in its first place, and the answer shows the list as stored; a `null` inside the list is a `422` naming the field, and nothing is written ([Writes](/docs/development/api/writes#partial-update) says so too).
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy): `ow_wire.py` 0.4.6 names each recipient once.** One Character given twice to `send` gets the message once, in the first place given.
- **[Public pages](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees): the landing file reads its lenses as written.** An entry of `x_landing.lenses` in another spelling than the lens's id greets nothing.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy): `ow_wire.py` 0.4.5 reads times as instants and takes a typed id in lowercase.** `mail`, `thread` and `--since` read a time by the message profile's one form, and a time that cannot be read comes after every readable one; a tally's newest vote is by time, then the later change, never the order the server lists them in; an id you type in upper case (`OW_CHARACTER`, a recipient, a thread's root) is taken in lowercase, as the platform serves every id.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.160.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.121.0: schema-dist serial 122, [the same id](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses#the-same-id).** Inside a world an id is written lowercase and compared as written. It is read in either case only at an address and in a text a profile searches, such as a Charter's description, and the lens key in an address is `lens` exactly. The [Open with menu](/docs/development/typescript-readers#open-with) refuses an element whose own id is held in upper case, the [folders page](/docs/schema/folders) says an id is written lowercase, and both packages' baked tools lists carry Windrose.
- **[Tools](/docs/tools): Windrose is listed.** A map of one world, Moppetopia, in the browser, read only; read against the [listing's checklist](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/tools) at its build of today.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.39.0: reports as threads and how an element is shown.** `OWReport.ThreadsOf` reads the report profile's threads and `OWViews.ViewOf` the views table, as the TypeScript SDK's `reportThreadsOf` and `viewOf` do; an export declares folder format 0.3.22, and the producer line carries 0.39.0.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.159.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.120.1: schema-dist serial 121.** The report and message profiles state what their readers already did: a report's parent that is no string, padded or given twice, a ring of reports, a `created_at` with no offset read as UTC, and the whole time grammar. The [readers page](/docs/development/typescript-readers#times) now says what a year is: any four of the digits 0 to 9, 0000 among them, on the Gregorian calendar run on in both directions.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.159.0: the time reader is exported.** `instantOf(value)` and `compareInstants(a, b)` are the reader the message, report, charter and lens readers order `created_at` by, so a tool that sorts with them orders as those readers do ([times](/docs/development/typescript-readers#times)): the whole value, `T` and `Z` in either case, no offset read as UTC, a fraction to its sixth digit; a date alone, a space for the `T`, a time without seconds and `+0200` are not read.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.158.1: the baked tools list carries Subs.** `openersFor` offers Subs beside a Character, as the Python package's list already did. The [readers page](/docs/development/typescript-readers#report-threads) and the [Python page](/docs/development/python) say that a report list read with `expand=parent_narrative` gives `reportThreadsOf` no parents: pass the ids.
- **[Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#filters): who is served a single link as stored.** A key with no member behind it (one the owner made, a classic or demo key), the owner's account token, a co-builder and a contributor while nothing in the world is hidden are served each single link as stored, whatever it names, and the link filters and the Pin list's `element` match on it; any other reader matches only what it is served. The page had named only the owner and co-builders. The [MCP](/docs/development/mcp) list tool's `link` reads the same.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door): `sponsor_character` and `my_character` can be one id.** A member can seat their own AI under the Character they play; then the AI works under its person's own Character and there is nobody to address ([agents](/docs/development/agents)).
- **[A team world](/docs/getting-started/team-world): a member's AI reports when there is something to tell.** The page said the company and software-project packs end each session with a report; since toolkit 5.10.0 a report is handed in at any point of a chat, when a stretch of work is worth telling the team.

## 2026-11-12

- **Both packages on schema-dist serial 120: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#report-threads) 4.158.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.120.0, report threads.** The [report profile](/docs/schema/profiles#report) gains rule 9: a report that continues earlier work names the first report of that work as its `parent_narrative`, and a closed talk's report keeps its board entry there. `reportThreadsOf` and `report_threads_of` group the reports you give them by that parent, read as written and never walked further up, and say what each parent is (`none`, `report`, `board`, `missing`, `other`, `self`), with a `chain` or `self` lint where a report names a later report or itself.
- **[Tools](/docs/tools/): Subs is listed.** A convoy-escort game of the Second World War: one of your Characters captains the lead escort, and the crossing is written back into the world as a record. Python 0.120.0 bakes the row; the TypeScript SDK takes it at its next release.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.10.0: a team's world gets a fourth skill, report.** A company or software-project world set up from it can hand in a report at any point of a chat, as often as there is something new; a later report on the same work names the first. The work skill keeps the keeper's step. A world set up before keeps its three skills.
- **[Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#filters): the Pin list filters by element.** `GET /api/v2/pin?element=<id>` lists the Pins you are served that place that element, of any type, in one call, so an element's maps no longer take a read of every Pin; with `map=<id>` beside it, whether it is on that map; with `expand=map`, each map's name. The [MCP](/docs/development/mcp) door's `link` argument does not take it.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#managing-members): accepting an invite takes no Character someone already plays.** A `roster_character` that is the owner's Character, or one another live member plays, is a `422` naming it, and nothing is joined.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy): `ow_wire.py` 0.4.2 lists a malformed vote row instead of failing on it**, and the world page's proposed Laws card now counts exactly as `tally` does.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public): the review-mark sentence names one mark.** Only `x_review_checked` reaches the public with `by` `null`: an element whose `x_review_proposed` holds a value is never served there, and a settled one carries it empty. A vote pressed on the site is now named by its choice ("Vote: aye" or "Vote: nay"), so its name no longer carries the voter's or the Law's.
- **Both packages on schema-dist serial 119: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.157.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.119.0.** The [map-space profile](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space) gains rule 11: a layout map is never the Map a tool picks for the world's space, and a Pin or Marker on one is a place in that drawing, never in the world. Both readers answer as before; the fixture holds 23 layout cases and 35 map-space cases. Python's folder writers stamp [folder format](/docs/schema/folders) 0.3.22.
- **[Folders](/docs/schema/folders): format 0.3.22.** A draft (a world no account holds yet) is an ordinary folder whose element ids go online unchanged, and an element file carries no `world` key.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.118.3: an imported LegendKeeper board is marked a board.** Each board's Map now carries `x_layout` `{"method": "hand"}`, the [map-space profile](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space)'s mark for a board, so a tool that picks a Map for the world's space leaves it out and its Pins read as places on the board ([importing from LegendKeeper](/docs/development/python-import)). The profiles page's count of the layout reading's fixture cases is corrected to 21.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.118.2: one source, one world.** Every importer's `--help` now ends with the rule the [import page](/docs/development/python-import) states: the world's id and every element's id are made from the source, so importing it again gives the same ids, and onlyworlds.com refuses an id another world already holds.

## 2026-11-11

- **Both packages on schema-dist serial 118: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.157.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.118.1.** The [checks profile](/docs/schema/profiles#checks) says a field is present only when something is left after trimming by the room profile's whitespace, so a unit separator alone is present, and gains two rules a team's world keeps (every work item and every report carries the room's mark), which the Python doctor replays. The [room profile](/docs/schema/profiles#room) says a sitting's own mark holds the world's id and that a sitting names its winning spec in `x_star`. The views table's conversation layout says a tool that shows one element and no thread draws it as a document. No answer of either package moved.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.157.0: six answer fields admit `null`.** A `/changes` upsert row's `updated_at` and a delete row's `deleted_at`, a `/bulk` slot's `id`, `created_at` and `updated_at` (a failed slot carries no id), and a member row's `x_served_trimmed` can each be `null`, as the API document says; the types said never. Nothing at runtime changes; code that reads one as a plain string now handles `null`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.156.0 and [the review queue](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-queue): a new element's `evidence` and `expires` arrive as stored.** The queue serves a review mark's `evidence` and `expires` as the mark holds them, any JSON value (a list of ids, a number, an object), where a non-string arrived as its text before; the SDK types both `unknown`, so code that read them as strings narrows first. An `expires` that is not a date or a date-time never lapses.

- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents): one answer for every bad join code, and the way back.** A bad code is the one `404` whatever the body holds (a missing `agent_name` was a `422` first); the 404 and the join's `next` say that an agent that joined before comes back with its key, never the link.

- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public): a review mark names nobody to the public.** A public element's `x_review_checked` or `x_review_proposed` is served with `by` `null`, its other keys as stored; every member is served the mark as stored.

- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.118.0: the doctor tells a room thing by the profiles.** A finding is set apart with the messages and room things only when its element is a message, or a room element by its pair and its `x_room_of` mark. A work item or a report made by hand without the mark is lore to every tool, so its finding now counts with the rest, and a [house rule](/docs/schema/profiles) such as `{"where": {"type": "relation", "supertype": "Work item"}, "require": {"field": "x_room_of", "present": true}}` lists each one.

- **Both packages on schema-dist serial 117: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.155.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.117.2.** The views table now says that a tool's own words for a heading or a fold are its drawing, and a fold's label is the word a tool uses where it has none of its own; which fields, in what order, open or folded, stay the row's ([`viewOf`](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants)). Its fixture gains a case: a Pin whose link has one key holding and the other blank holds nothing. No reader's answer moved.

- **A review mark's `by` is your own membership id exactly as `GET /api/v2/me` gives it.** Below co-builder, the same id in capitals, without hyphens, in braces or after `urn:uuid:` is refused like any other `by` ([`review_by_another`](/api/errors/#review_by_another)), and the [queue](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-queue) shows such a member the marked new elements whose `by` is exactly its own id. The [draught](/docs/development/api/draught)'s `proposed` section names what counts as no mark: `null`, an empty string or whitespace alone, `[]` and `{}`.

- **[The review queue](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-queue): a new element's `by` is the mark's own, and `null` when it names nobody.** The queue's `new_element` row serves the mark's `by` as the writer's word, a claim, and `null` for one that is not a string or is whitespace alone (it was the text of the value). The [`proposed_only`](/api/errors/#proposed_only) summary says "a new element whose mark names it". onlyworlds.com's own pages now say a mark as "the mark names Ada".

- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.117.1: the Markdown pack says "marked proposed".** Its opening line counts the elements "marked proposed and not yet reviewed by a keeper" it left out or kept, where it said "proposed by an AI agent": a review mark says an element is proposed, never who made it.

- **[Who a review mark's `by` names](/docs/schema/profiles#review) (schema-dist serial 116), in both packages: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.155.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.117.0.** A mark's `by` is the writer's own word for who made the mark, a claim: read as stored and never trimmed, naming nobody when it is not a string or is whitespace alone, and shown as the mark's word ("the mark names Ada"), never as a fact. `proposedMarkOf` and `proposed_mark_of` no longer trim it (`" owner "` came back as `owner` before).

- **[A file row says whether the owner added it](/docs/development/api/files#listing-files), and [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.154.0 reads it.** Every file row's `added_by` now carries `owner`, `true` when the world's current owner added the file. `membership` is `null` both for the owner's files and for a file whose membership no longer exists, so `owner` tells them apart, and a file whose member's account was deleted is named "a former member" (its name was empty before). `OwFileAddedBy.owner` in the TypeScript SDK.

- **Both packages on schema-dist serial 115: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.153.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.116.0.** A [lens](/docs/lenses)'s `first_line` row now carries the description's first line that holds something, as written with its own spaces, and no description when no line holds anything (a description opening with a blank line gave an empty one before). A Domain's `sentence` is said in its own rule: [trimmed, cut at the first line break, trimmed again](/docs/development/api/members). The [folder checker](/docs/schema/folders) reads the two review marks as warnings that never fail a folder, in schema-dist and in `python -m onlyworlds.lint`. The [report and packet profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#report) say who reported a report and that a packet's producer `version` is never invented. A Pin's `element` is drawn in `viewOf`'s order (`views.json` version 3).

- **[An element never takes a membership's id](/api/errors/#id_conflict).** Creating an element under an id a membership holds (a member's or a seat's, in any world, a removed member's too) is a `409` `id_conflict`, `param` `id`, at `POST`, a creating `PUT`, a `/bulk` item and the hand-over: mint a new id. Elements that exist keep their ids.
- **[A review mark's `by` is the writer's own](/api/errors/#review_by_another), and both packages read the refusal: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.152.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.115.0.** Below co-builder, an `x_review_proposed` or `x_review_checked` mark that carries a `by` is stored only when it is exactly the mark the element holds or its `by` is the writer's own membership id; any other `by`, a Character's id included, is a `403` `review_by_another` with `param` the mark, and nothing is written. The owner and co-builders write any `by`. `OwApiError.isReviewByAnother` and `ApiError.is_review_by_another` tell it apart; the [review profile](/docs/schema/profiles#review) says the rule.
- **[The account](/docs/development/api/signin#the-account): one rule for a username.** `PATCH /api/v2/account` now holds a new `username` to the rule sign-up holds it to: the letters a to z in either case, the digits and `@ . + - _`, with at least one letter or digit, at most 30 characters, not another account's name in any case, never the word `owner`. A refused name is a `422` `invalid_request` with `param` `username`, and nothing is written. Until now that route checked nothing. Existing names stay as they are.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#features) 1.123.18 to 1.123.25: a pin you place by hand on a laid-out map stays put, and an element's Files take images.** On a map laid out by a method, a pin you place, create or drag is marked as placed by hand, so the next layout leaves it where you put it, and removing the mark gives it back to the layout. In an online world, an element's Files section takes png, jpg, webp and avif up to 25 MB each. The rest of the run changes nothing these pages say: a report's work items and its room, a save that never undoes a check, a review mark that holds one element instead of the whole sync, and the mark's wording.

## 2026-11-10

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.9.1: the team packs after a cold read.** A member's AI marks a work item it made as taken, and the keeper's AI reads the source a report names before a claim changes a description. In every pack, a visiting AI's lens read asks for less, a 404 naming a lens means the visit goes on, and a folder skips retired and template lenses. A world set up earlier keeps the skills it was given.
- **Both packages on schema-dist serial 114: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.151.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.114.0.** A [lens](/docs/lenses)'s short rows (`name` and `first_line`) now carry `supertype` and `subtype` where the element holds them, as stored, in both packages. [`viewOf`](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants) returns `order`, the fields an element's page draws in the order drawn, from `views.json` version 2, and shows the trimmed-list mark only when the list names a field. A review mark that is an object with any key in it reads as proposed, whatever its keys, as both packages already read it.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.9.0: a team's world gets three skills and a work report.** A world set up from the company or the software-project pack carries three skills under one short word the setup asks for (start, design, work). A member's AI hands in a [report](/docs/schema/profiles#report) and changes no element; the keeper's AI, on a co-builder's seat, completes it and makes the changes; people read the reports. The world's **AI seats propose, a person accepts** switch stays off for this. The [team world](/docs/getting-started/team-world#for-the-ai) page says so. The draw and read skills now count a marked report as the world's working layer, not its lore.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents), [Files](/docs/development/api/files) and [MCP](/docs/development/mcp): a file is part of the world.** The agent policy and the line a file's text begins with through `read_file` say "part of the world" where they said "text in the world", since a file can be a picture. What an agent may read or do is unchanged.
- **[Search](/docs/development/api/reads#search) in both packages: [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.150.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.113.0.** `client.search(q, { type, limit })` and `client.search(q, element_type=..., limit=...)` call `GET /api/v2/search` and hand back its answer as served. The page now says what a word is: a run of letters, digits or underscores, so a key like `APP-142` is searched as two words, and a key that must appear whole is checked against the elements found.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members) and [lenses](/docs/development/api/lenses): `lenses_from` can be `none`.** A member or seat that never chose its lenses and that nothing brings a lens to (no Character, or Titles that name none) now reads `none`, apart from `chosen`, which a list chosen empty on purpose still reads. What anyone reads is unchanged. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.149.0 types the third word.
- **[Files](/docs/development/api/files#what-a-file-can-be): a file on an element can be an image.** `png`, `jpg` (a name may end `.jpeg`), `webp` and `avif` join the kinds: a team's screens and mocks, private to the element's readers, where an element's picture is public by its link. The upload host checks an image by its first bytes and keeps them as they came; svg, gif and heic are refused with what to export instead. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.148.0 reads the four kinds from a file's name.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants) 4.147.0: `viewOf`, how an element is shown by its kind.** From schema-dist serial 113's new `views.json`: the row that matches (a report, a charter, a message or a line in a room; anything else draws as its type), its one sentence for a card or a hover, and the marks to show at the head of its page. [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.112.1 is on serial 113 too.
- **[The public pages](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees): a long text keeps its line breaks.** On an element's public page a blank line in a text field starts a new paragraph and a single break a new line, in Details and in the description, as the signed-in page shows them.
- **[Filters](/docs/development/api/reads#filters): the empty `characters` filter agrees with what you are served.** `?characters=` lists every element you are served with `characters` empty and no `x_served_trimmed` naming it; a Message whose addressees were all hidden from you stays out, as before.
- **[The report profile](/docs/schema/profiles#report): a session's report is a room element (schema-dist serial 112).** A Narrative with supertype `Report` and the room's mark is what a session, or a closed talk, leaves behind: handed in by the reporter's AI, completed by the keeper's, with `x_report_answers` naming the work items it answers. The [room profile](/docs/schema/profiles#room) gains it as its tenth pair, and [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.146.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.112.0 count a marked report as a room element. A [work item](/docs/schema/profiles#packet) may name whom it is for by role, `x_packet_for`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.112.0: `ApiError.refusals`.** A 422 [`answer_not_parsed`](/api/errors/#answer_not_parsed) says each answer an AI use refused, as TypeScript's `err.refusals` does.
- **[A team world](/docs/getting-started/team-world): a new page.** How a team shares one world, step by step: the owner invites, each person joins and connects their own AI, and each AI reads, talks and writes as a seat that answers to a person, each step linked to the page that holds it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.145.0 on [the AI door](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name), and [the refusal](/api/errors/#answer_not_parsed): an AI answer that was refused says how it stopped.** An `answer_not_parsed` error carries `refusals`, one row per refused answer with its `word`, the provider's `stop_reason` and its `output_tokens`, so an answer cut off at `max_tokens` reads apart from one in the wrong shape; the SDK reads it as `err.refusals`.
- **[Lenses](/docs/lenses): the sentence box reads "everything", plurals and "all".** "Everything" alone is every element, "everything about" a name one step out from it, a plural finds its name, and "all" with a name a step out from what it matches.
- **[Filters](/docs/development/api/reads#filters): an empty link filter agrees with what you are served.** `?parent_location=` (and `""` in the [MCP](/docs/development/mcp) list tool's `link`) lists every element whose link you are served empty: unset, aimed at an element you cannot see, or one an element you know of does not show. Before, it read the stored link, so those were left out. The owner's lists do not change.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.38.0: the layout reading, and `CanPay`.** `OWMapSpace.LayoutOf(map, maps, pins)` reads a Map's [layout keys](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space) as `layoutOf` does, on the 21 shared cases; each `AiUsesAsync` row carries `CanPay`; the folder producer line says 0.38.0.
- **[The AI door](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name): a lens from a sentence, and `can_pay`.** A fifth use, `lens`, proposes a lens from one sentence by a model choosing among what the account sees in the world, beside the proposal with no model; it is served switched off for now. Each row of the uses list now carries `can_pay`, true when a call would be paid now by the same check the call runs. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.144.0 types it.
- **[Layout maps](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space): four more fixture cases (schema-dist serial 111).** The layout reading's shared cases grow to 21: ids past U+FFFF, a repeated Pin id where the last row wins, and a Pin row or a Map row without a string id, which is no element. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-layout-of-a-map) 4.144.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.111.1 pass all 21.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-layout-along-a-route) 4.143.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.111.0: the trail's default floors are named by their kinds.** `TRAIL_TIERS` keeps its heights and kinds, so every point is the same; a floor's name is now its kinds (`location, object, zone`, then `character, collective, creature, species`, `event`, and the other eleven), and that name is what a hanging Pin's `by` text and the recipe's `settings.tiers` carry. Python's `layout_of` also answers the empty reading for a Map row without a string id, as the npm reading does.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.37.0: the AI door.** `AiUsesAsync` lists the uses, `AiAsync` calls one (typed for `ask` and `sort`), `CompleteSignInAsync` takes `aiUses` for [an AI door token](/docs/development/api/signin#an-ai-door-token) and `MintAiTokenAsync` mints one from an account token; `AskAsync` and `SortAsync` are obsolete and keep working.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.142.1: two lengths counted as the server counts them.** `mintAiToken`'s `name` (at most 255) and `ai`'s `idempotencyKey` (1 to 200) count characters, not UTF-16 units, so a name of 255 emoji is no longer refused before it is sent. [The sign-in page](/docs/development/api/signin#an-ai-door-token) says a use named twice in `ai_uses` is kept once.
- **[Layout maps](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space): the map-space profile's rules 8 to 10 (schema-dist serial 110), and the layout reading.** A Map carrying `x_layout` with a `method` is a layout map, `settings.ground` names the Map it stands on, and a Pin with `x_hand: true` was placed by hand, so a new lay-out keeps it. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-layout-of-a-map) 4.142.0 reads them with `layoutOf(map, maps, pins)` and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.110.0 with `layout_of`, the same answer: `{layout, ground, hand}`.
- **[An AI door token](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name) sees only its own uses.** On such a token, `GET /api/v2/account/ai` lists only the uses it names; an account token's list is unchanged. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.141.0 carries `param` on a refused [code exchange](/docs/development/api/signin#an-ai-door-token), so the `422` for a wrong `ai_uses` can be told from other refusals. The MCP server's refusal of a malformed `ids` now reads "ids must be a list of ids".
- **[The AI door](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name): real-cost uses, and the two Subs uses.** The uses list names each use's `provider` and its `rating_need`, the rating a call needs left to run; `price_tokens: null` now means the call's real cost, counted in Haiku input tokens. The server runs `subs-jev` and `subs-captain` for Subs. A refusal the provider billed, the new `422` [`answer_not_parsed`](/api/errors/#answer_not_parsed) or a `502` after a billed answer, is kept under its `Idempotency-Key` and replayed, and counts against the use's own budget, never the rating. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.140.0 types `rating_need` and reads the new code with `err.isAnswerNotParsed`.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp): refusals carry the error envelope, `if_match`, and the list filters.** A refusal from the API now comes back as the tool error's text in the [error envelope](/api/errors/) as JSON (`retry_after` beside it on a `429`, `current` on a `412`); the server's own argument errors stay sentences. `update_element` and `edit_links` take `if_match`, the element's `change_seq`, and refuse a stale one with `412`. `list_elements` takes `subtype`, `ids`, `created_by` and `link`, so the pins on one map are one call: `link={"map": "<map id>"}`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.139.0: `ask()` and `sort()` deprecated.** Call `ai('ask', body)` and `ai('sort', body)` instead ([the AI door](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name)): the same answers, with the use's meter and an idempotency key, under which a retry is billed nothing. The body takes the API's spelling, `world_id`. The old names keep working until 5.0; the [readers page](/docs/development/typescript-readers) shows the new calls, and the reference marks the two.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.138.0: the AI door token in the SDK.** `completeSignIn({ ..., aiUses, aiLifetimeMinutes })` asks [the sign-in](/docs/development/api/signin#an-ai-door-token) for one and returns it as `aiToken`, and `client.mintAiToken(uses, { lifetimeMinutes })` trades an account token down to one ([the AI page](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name)). `err.isUseNotGranted` and `err.isWrongAudience` read its two refusals.

## 2026-11-09

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.7.0: the skills say what onlyworlds.com does today.** An audit of every skill against the live API document found seventeen sentences behind, each now true: the schema page at serial 109 (`Location.vehicle`), the draught's `depth`, `lens`, `since` and `compact`, the draw skill's map on the map-space profile, and the two page names, a public page and a world page.
- **[A token for the AI door only](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name).** A tool can ask the sign-in's code exchange for an AI door token with `ai_uses` ([the exchange](/docs/development/api/signin#an-ai-door-token)), or mint one from an account token with `POST /api/v2/account/tokens`. It calls only the AI uses it names, as the person and on their token rating, until midnight UTC at the latest. Another use is `403` [`use_not_granted`](/api/errors/#use_not_granted), and every other route `401` [`wrong_audience`](/api/errors/#wrong_audience). `on` in `GET /api/v2/account/ai` is now false for every use while the server runs no AI.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#features) 1.123.17: a laid-out map draws its ground map under its pins**: when it has no picture of its own, the map it stands on shows beneath it, its picture and its rivers, roads and regions as faint lines and areas.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#features) 1.123.16: a map laid out by a method shows its recipe as a card**: the method, the map it stands on, and that Atlas draws it flat, where its page showed raw custom fields.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.137.0: the AI door in the SDK.** `client.aiUses()` lists the uses and `client.ai(use, body, { idempotencyKey })` calls one with its own body, typed for `ask` and `sort`; the answer adds `use_remaining` and `wasReplay`, and `err.isUseCeiling` and `err.isUseInFlight` read the door's two `429`s. `ask()` and `sort()` keep their own routes. The [AI page](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name) now says how to retry an `idempotency_in_flight` key.
- **[The AI door](/docs/development/ai#the-ai-door-a-use-by-name): a tool calls a model by a named use.** `POST /api/v2/account/ai/ask` and `/ai/sort` take the old routes' bodies and answer `use_remaining` too; `GET /api/v2/account/ai` lists each use with its caps and calls left; an `Idempotency-Key` makes a retry safe. New refusals [`use_ceiling`](/api/errors/#use_ceiling) and [`use_in_flight`](/api/errors/#use_in_flight), and [`ask_unavailable`](/api/errors/#ask_unavailable) with `param: "use"`. The old routes are unchanged.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-layout-along-a-route) 4.136.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.109.0: a layout's recipe names its ground map.** `ground.map`, the ground Map's element id, travels in the recipe, so a tool reading a laid-out Map finds the picture under its pins.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-layout-along-a-route) 4.135.2: a tool that never lays out carries no tier table.** 4.135.0 and 4.135.1 put `TRAIL_TIERS` (about 450 bytes) into every bundle; now a bundler leaves it out unless the tool reads it.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp): three lines trued to the server.** Its instructions say some writes land as proposed; `create_element`'s description carries the room rule; `get_draught` leaves `messages` and `proposed` out unless named, as it does `room`; and the REST side of the comparison lists every list filter.
- **[Public pages](/docs/development/api/public#turning-it-on): the switch reads "Give it a public page at onlyworlds.com/w/…"**, as onlyworlds.com now says public page and world page everywhere; the two pages that quote it follow.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#credentials): calls count against the request budget.** The same 300 a minute per key and 60 per address as the API, with the three `RateLimit` headers on every answer and a `429` with `Retry-After` over it.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.108.0: `trail_by_place`, the same layout method as TypeScript's.** The same input and the same answer as `trailByPlace`, held by one shared set of cases.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-layout-along-a-route) 4.135.1: `trailByPlace` refuses a row of the frame with no free point left**, where it ran forever: more Pins on one row than the frame is wide plus one.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-layout-along-a-route) 4.135.0: `trailByPlace`, the first layout method.** It lays a world out along a route on a ground map and answers a Map frame and one Pin per element in the standard's own fields, which `mapSpace` reads back, with the route and the settings it ran with. You hand it the travel order and what hangs where; it places them.
- **One word for each page.** A world's [public page](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees) (`onlyworlds.com/w/<address>`) and an element's public page are called that throughout; the world page is the signed-in page where its owner and members keep keys, members and settings ([onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)); the 22 pages of the schema's element types are type pages.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.36.0: a lost key mint can be found, and the calendar holds to the TypeScript SDK's answers.** `MintWorldKeyAsync` takes an `idempotencyKey` and `MadeWorldKeyAsync` answers the key a lost mint made ([a lost mint](/docs/getting-started/keys#a-lost-mint)). `OWCalendar` replays the TypeScript SDK's recorded answers, gains `ValidateValue` and a `FormatDuration` for a span that need not be whole, and refuses an era `year_base` other than 0 or 1.

## 2026-11-08

- **[A lost key mint can be found](/docs/getting-started/keys#a-lost-mint).** The mint now takes an `Idempotency-Key`. It is never replayed, since a stored answer would keep the key, but the key's row remembers it, and `GET /api/v2/account/worlds/{world_id}/keys/made?key=` answers that row without the key, or `404` when the mint made none. A tool whose mint answer was lost revokes that id and mints again with a new `Idempotency-Key`: the same one sent again makes a second key, and the lookup keeps naming the first. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.134.0: `mintWorldKey` takes `idempotencyKey` and `signal`, and `madeWorldKey` is the lookup.

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.134.1 exports `OwWorldKeyRow` and `OwWorldKeyMember`**, the row `madeWorldKey` answers and the membership on a member-bound key, so `import { type OwWorldKeyRow }` compiles; 4.134.0 named them without exporting them.

- **A calendar era's `year_base` is 0 or 1 (schema-dist serial 109; the standard's version is unchanged, `00.34.00`).** The [calendar profile](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/schema-dist/blob/main/profiles/calendar.md) said so in its field table, but its refusal rule only refused a value that was not a whole number. It now refuses any value other than 0 or 1, and its fixture gains the case. A calendar with such an era reads as no calendar, so a tool shows the bare number ([TypeScript readers](/docs/development/typescript-readers)). [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.133.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.107.2 already refused it and now carry the case.

## 2026-11-07

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.6.2: a session on an account world can open with one character's draught or a lens**, beside the brief built from files, as the README and the start skill's routing now say.
- **[Deleting a world removes its images](/docs/development/api/images#removing-an-image).** Every image uploaded with a ticket for the world is removed with it, as its files already were, and a nightly sweep removes any whose removal the upload host didn't confirm. Before, a deleted world's images stayed served at their addresses.
- **[The practice world's key is counted by address](/docs/development/api-reference#limits), 60 requests a minute, not a key's 300**, so one loop never uses it up for everyone; the API reference's Limits row now says so.

- **[Making a world on the account](/docs/getting-started/keys#account-tokens) checks a PIN that is sent.** An account that has a PIN may now send it with `POST /api/v2/account/worlds` or the hand-over, and a wrong one is a `401` [`invalid_credentials`](/api/errors/#invalid_credentials) with `param` `pin` before any world is made; before, any `pin` from such an account was a `422`. Left out, the world takes the account's PIN as before.
- **[Both routes that make a world take an `Idempotency-Key`](/docs/getting-started/keys#a-world-with-its-first-elements)**, so a retry after a lost answer makes no second world. A replay of the plain create answers the world without its key, which is shown only once.
- **[A lost answer is found by its key](/docs/getting-started/keys#a-world-with-its-first-elements)**: `GET /api/v2/account/worlds/made?key=` answers the world a hand-over or create made under that `Idempotency-Key`, past the day the key replays. [Which world holds an element](/docs/development/api/signin#the-account) now answers for any element's id, not only a Narrative's.
- **[A public element page](/docs/development/api/public) shows its own element type's fields**: Details and Connected to follow the schema for every type, where every page used a Character's fields before.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#a-world-on-the-account): `createAccountWorld`'s `pin`** reads the new rule: an account with a PIN may send it to have it checked.

## 2026-11-06

- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#ship-a-world-with-your-game) 0.35.0: a world file a web player can read, and calls that return in one.** `StoreForWebPlayers` packs an `.owworld` with every entry stored, for a web player built with DiskSizeLTO, which halts on a deflated one; a web player's calls after the first now return. The Open with rows read `opens_public`, and the baked tools list offers Atlas live on a public page.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)'s Open with menu on a public page offers Atlas as a row that opens.** A signed-out visitor who picks it is offered a view-only copy of the world in Atlas; Lantern and Tactical Tangle still show grey with "Needs an account".
- **A listed tool can say its links open a public world with no account.** A row's new `opens_public` (beside `needs: "account"`) makes a world's public page offer the tool as a row that opens, where it said "Needs an account"; members' menus read as before ([List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool#open-with); the packages' menus since TypeScript SDK 4.133.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.107.0, [the readers](/docs/development/typescript-readers)).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.133.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.107.1 bake the tools list with Atlas's row that opens a public world.** A menu drawn from the packages' own copy of the list offers Atlas live on a public page, as the site's does.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#joining-from-a-link) 1.123.0 to 1.123.1: a link opens a public world with no account.** Atlas asks first, then brings the world into the browser as a view-only copy of what its owner made public; a link can also open the writing room on a chapter or scene.

## 2026-11-05

- **[The draught](/docs/world-graph/draught)'s picture moves to World graph.** The figure of a Character's sections, served by default or only when named, with the policy's parameters, now sits on the plainer page; [the route's page](/docs/development/api/draught) links to it and keeps the tables.
- **Three field descriptions are clearer (schema-dist serial 108; the standard's version is unchanged, `00.34.00`).** Event's [`constructs`](/docs/schema/element_categories/event) no longer says laws; the seventeen time fields, from a Character's [`birth_date`](/docs/schema/element_categories/character) to a Zone's dates, say in one phrasing that they count in the World's `time_basic_unit`; Map's [`hierarchy`](/docs/schema/element_categories/map) says it is a number to order or group the maps that share a parent, read as each tool chooses. No field was added or removed. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.132.4 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.106.3 carry the new words.

## 2026-11-04

- **The [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) pages explain their words.** Roles, doors, the proposals switch, keepers, seats, profiles, lenses, rungs and hands are said or linked where they first appear; the readers' person is the viewer. The SDK page gains short sections on [files](/docs/development/typescript#files) and [hidden elements](/docs/development/typescript#hidden-elements), a draught call, and its world file rules under details; the [readers](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) page gains a lens example and the server's lens calls; [Accounts](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#sign-in-with-onlyworlds) gains Sign in with OnlyWorlds and leads invite links with making one. The five kept version notes now trail what the call does today.
- **The [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) page keeps to the client**: install, read, write, bulk writes, errors (now right after bulk), images, following changes, context for an AI and world files. Signing in, invite links, a seat for your AI, a world on the account and proposed changes moved to [Accounts, members and sharing](/docs/development/typescript-accounts); search, calendars, messages, addresses, Open with and door history moved to [Reading worlds as a tool](/docs/development/typescript-readers), beside the readers already there. Write splits its rules into those every write follows and the optional ones, and a world file's refusals are a table.
- **The [Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) page says what the plugin does now**, without the version each part arrived in (the plugin's changelog holds that); the one kept says to update every device that shares a vault.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) reads in the order you meet it**: Getting started first (create a world, your first story), then keeping your world, the features, [syncing with onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/atlas#sync-with-onlyworldscom) and [sharing](/docs/tools/atlas#sharing), each in short sections, every button named as Atlas 1.122.0 shows it: the first story's boxes with their ⋯ menu, the main character's Born and the location's Founded.
- **[Import from other apps](/docs/development/python-import) has its own page.** The Python package's importers for Campfire, Kanka, LegendKeeper, Notion and World Anvil moved off the [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) page, one section each: what it reads, the command to run, then what becomes what.
- **Plainer words, round three.** [Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles#room) says once what a room is (a world's chat room, kept as ordinary elements), and [Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public), the [draught](/docs/development/api/draught), Python, TypeScript and [Lenses](/docs/lenses) use those words; Lenses introduces reading aids before its rule; [Reads and pagination](/docs/development/api/reads) lists the doors a change can come through; the draught pages say what a grade is; "fixture" is said as shared test cases; [SDKs and clients](/docs/development/packages) states a world's origin plainly.
- **[Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles) says once what a serial is**, and each profile names only the serial that first carried it; the history of each profile is in schema-dist.
- **"Not yet" lines say what to do instead.** [Python](/docs/development/python)'s missing calls point to the TypeScript SDK, the REST API and where to ask; [Hiding](/docs/development/api/hidden) says what body to send; [Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public) says a public world stays out of search results; [Members](/docs/development/api/members) gives the route for picking your Character from Python; [MCP](/docs/development/mcp)'s `world` leads with who can use it; [Packages](/docs/development/packages) says to pin the Unity SDK's version.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.132.3: `me()` is documented, and the reference names the server as onlyworlds.com.** The reference's `me()` row now says what `GET /me` answers and that the PIN is sent there. A refused `element_types` lists the 22 type words in the standard's order, as the other packages do. The shared Open with test set has 103 cases.

## 2026-11-03

- **[Little Lens](/docs/tools/little-lens) has its own page.** How to open a world in it (a public world with no account, a world file or folder, Sign in with OnlyWorlds, a link from another tool), its Web, List and Time views, the Lens box, a Character's draught at a moment, messages, and what it leaves out. The old Element Viewer address leads there.
- **Plainer words on ten pages.** Notes that read like the maintainers' own records now say what a reader can do: [World graph](/docs/world-graph#next-to-other-knowledge-bases) on why ids keep same-named elements apart, [Unity](/docs/development/unity)'s platform line, the archive test set on [World folders](/docs/schema/folders), and why [AI tools](/docs/development/ai) have no delete. Nothing these pages promise has changed.
- **The element pages print each bounded field's range in its row.** The standard gives 15 integer fields a range: the Character's six Personality scores and the Relation's `intensity`, the Species' `aggression` and the Ability's `potency` run 0 to 100, and the Trait's six Quantitative fields -100 to 100. Each now shows it in the field table's type column ("integer, 0 to 100"), read from the standard with the rest of the table, on [Character](/docs/schema/element_categories/character), [Trait](/docs/schema/element_categories/trait), [Ability](/docs/schema/element_categories/ability), [Relation](/docs/schema/element_categories/relation) and [Species](/docs/schema/element_categories/species). A range is a display hint: the API does not enforce it.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.34.0: a tool that opens one kind of element is offered only on that kind.** `OWOpeners.For` reads the tools list's `element_types` (`OWToolOpen.ElementTypes`), `OWTools` lists Tactical Tangle's world and Character doors, and the schema pin moves to serial 107; the same rows as the TypeScript SDK 4.132.2 and the Python package 0.106.2.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com): Copy address on your world and element pages.** A button beside the actions copies the address every OnlyWorlds tool opens, the same as Open with's Copy link; the browser bar's address on these pages is the site's own and opens in no tool.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.132.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.106.2: the schema pin moves to serial 107.** The standard's version is unchanged (`00.34.00`); two profile pages gained a sentence each and no reader changed.
- **[The Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#features) 3.18.0: one settings place, and a pasted address that reads as you meant it.** The default world, the default type for a new element and the **Create new <Type>** switch live in Obsidian's settings pane and act at once; the old `OnlyWorlds/Settings.md` note is carried over once, your text kept. **Open a note from an address** takes `www.onlyworlds.com/w/...` without `https://`, and a refused paste says where to copy an address (Open with, Copy link). Locations carry the standard's Vehicle link.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.132.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.106.1: Tactical Tangle opens a world or a Character.** [The tools list](/docs/development/list-your-tool#open-with) gives [Tactical Tangle](https://tangle.onlyworlds.com) a world door and an element door for Characters only (`element_types: ["character"]`, an account needed), so an Open with menu offers it on a world and on a Character, and on no other kind of element. Both packages bake the new list; no code changed.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.132.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.106.0: a tool that opens one kind of element is offered only on that kind.** [The tools list](/docs/development/list-your-tool#open-with)'s `open` gains `element_types` (`["character"]`, or `null` for every kind); on an element of another type the tool has no row, its world door included. Every listed tool says `null` today, so no menu changes.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#getting-started) 1.121.0: a new world's story asks who it is about before what happens.** The questions now go: the story, who it is about, what happens to them, where, and whether they are still there. On Strand each filled box says where it goes next, and on Picture the story and its parts stand first, in the same places every time.
- **[The practice world](/docs/getting-started/keys#the-practice-world): a world anyone can write to without an account.** Its key and PIN are on Keys and PINs, and the [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#write) and [Python](/docs/development/python#write) Write examples now run against it as written. It is a copy of Moppetopia under its own ids that goes back to the same copy every night at 03:00 UTC. It takes no files or members, holds at most 2,000 elements, allows 60 requests a minute per address, and never locks on a wrong PIN.
- **[Your own AI key](/docs/development/ai#ask-your-world-without-a-key-of-your-own): asking and sorting keep running once the day's token rating is spent.** An account can save an Anthropic or OpenAI key on its account page; past the rating, its asks and sorts run on that key, billed by the provider. Each answer's `paid_by` says `rating` or `own_key`, the allowance carries `own_key`, and a key the provider refuses answers [`own_key_refused`](/api/errors/#own_key_refused), never retried.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.131.0: `paid_by` on `ask` and `sort`, `own_key` on `allowance`, and `isOwnKeyRefused`.**
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.33.0: your own AI key, and a signed-out visitor's Open with rows.** `PaidBy` on `AskAsync` and `SortAsync`, `OWAllowance.OwnKey` and `OWApiError.IsOwnKeyRefused`; `OWOpeners.For` gives the public the tools that need an account, each "Needs an account" and opening nothing; `OWMapSpace.Of` reads serial 106 (one row per id); `OWTools` lists Tactical Tangle.
- **[`ow_wire.py`](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy) 0.4.1: every line `mail` and `tally` print is cleaned**, a recipient's or a voter's name included. Before, those names printed as served, so a name holding a terminal escape could rewrite the screen; the message text itself was cleaned already. Nothing on the wire changed.

## 2026-11-02

- **[The Lenses page](/docs/lenses#on-onlyworldscom) proposes a lens from a sentence.** A box beside Start from takes plain words and fills the lens's settings with a proposal you change and save; a line under the box says what the words chose, or names the next step when nothing matched.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#getting-started) 1.120.0 to 1.120.2: a new world's Home asks for its first story, one question at a time.** The story, one event in it, its location, who was involved and where they are now, each a box joined to the one before; a browser-only world asks for a copy once the story is whole.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)'s Open with menu on a public page** now shows Atlas and Lantern grey, with "Needs an account", where before a signed-out visitor did not see them.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn) 4.130.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.105.0: a signed-out visitor sees the tools that need an account.** The Open with menu's public rows now include each tool that needs a sign-in, saying "Needs an account"; the drawn menu shows it grey, and pressing it does nothing. A tool that needs the world on the device is still not offered to a visitor.
- **[The Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#features) 3.17.0: two commands for an element's address.** **Open this note on onlyworlds.com** opens the element's page on the site; **Open a note from an address** opens the vault's note for a pasted address. A sign-in with your browser that fails or times out clears what it left at once.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#map-space) 4.129.2: `mapSpace` reads a number past 2^53 as not whole.** JavaScript parses a larger integer to the nearest double; a value it had to round no longer passes as a coordinate.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) v0.32.0: three profile readers.** `OWLanding.Of` (the landing), `OWMapSpace.Of` (map space, serial 105) and `OWGraphOptions.ReadingAids` (the reading aids back in an unlensed graph); an empty review mark on a record reads as settled; Location's `vehicle`. The producer line's version is 0.32.0.
- **[The map-space profile](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space)** (serial 106): two rows of one type with one id read as the last one served; a measured frame reads only placed points; a `z` that is not a whole number reads as 0; a Zone with no placed Marker counts as `no_markers`; `type` and `shape` are read as written. The fixture holds 34 cases.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#map-space) 4.129.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.104.1: the map-space readers keep one row per id**, the last served, for every type (TypeScript read each repeated Marker and Pin row as one of its own, Python each repeated Marker row); the schema pin moves to serial 106 (`SCHEMA_VERSION` `00.34.00`).
- **[A world's landing file](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees)**: `/w/<address>/landing.json`, the world's first picture as the landing profile reads it from the public graph, with the lenses the owner chose for it and the change number it was read at; the graph page's file carries the same number.
- **[The map-space profile](/docs/schema/profiles#map-space)** (serial 105): how a reader turns a world's Maps, Zones, Markers and Pins into geometry, with a fixture of 24 cases. The answer is `{ maps, zones, pins, out }`: each Map's frame, each Zone's outline per Map, each Pin's point with its element, and what was left aside counted by reason. `z` is a point's elevation, never a height. It adds no field; the standard stays 00.34.00.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#map-space) 4.129.0: `mapSpace`, the map-space profile's reader.** It takes the elements as the reader is served them and returns `{ maps, zones, pins, out }`; the schema pin moves to serial 105.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.104.0: `map_space`, the map-space profile's reader**, the same answer as TypeScript's `mapSpace`; the schema pin moves to serial 105 (`SCHEMA_VERSION` `00.34.00`).
- **[`ow_wire.py`](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy) 0.4.0 reads a message list trimmed for its key**: an empty recipient list with `x_served_trimmed` is to someone you cannot see, so it leaves your mail instead of reading as to everyone, and a sender left out for you is `unseen` ("from someone you cannot see"), never "from nobody". The URL is cached for an hour; a query string fetches the current file.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#compact) becomes compact by default on 2027-05-02** (announced today; the fixed shape stays the default until then): a caller that needs every section sends `compact=false` from now. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.128.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.103.1 already do when you leave it out, so their typed fixed shape holds.
- **[The graph](/docs/lenses/graph) takes a fifth flag, reading aids** (serial 104): a graph without a lens brings the charter, its guide and its domains back when the reader asks, each marked `reading_aid`; never for a public reader. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-as-a-graph) 4.128.0 (`readingAids`) and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.103.0 (`reading_aids`).
- **[The world's graph page](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees) draws what the graph reader reads**: a link from an element to itself is a small loop, a field naming one element twice is two lines, a link to an element of the wrong type for its field is not drawn, and the charter, its guide and its domains are never points.
- **The standard 00.34.00: [Location](/docs/schema/element_categories/location#setting) gains `vehicle`**, a single link in Setting to the Object the place is the inside of: a ship, a train, an airship, a walking city, a station that moves. A place inside another place still uses `parent_location`. An additive change (a minor step), served by onlyworlds.com; the element page regenerated with schema-dist serial 103.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.102.0: `landing_of`, the landing profile's reader**, with `TOOLS` listing Tactical Tangle, `records_of` reading proposed exactly as `proposed_mark_of` does, and the reverse link index built only when a lens hop asks for it; the schema pin moves to serial 103 (`SCHEMA_VERSION` `00.34.00`).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#landing) 4.127.0: `landingOf`, a world's first picture at a door.** It reads what `worldGraph` answered and returns `{ mode, size, clusters, unclustered, gaps }`: the most-linked Characters with what lies near them, or a small world's gaps first. [`recordsOf`](/docs/development/typescript-readers#records) reads a run or a story as proposed exactly when `proposedMarkOf` does, so an empty mark is settled, and never reads an Event or a Narrative whose `id` is not a string; `TOOLS` lists Tactical Tangle; a reading builds its reverse link index only when a lens hop asks for it, so `worldGraph` without a lens is faster, with no answer changed. The schema pin moves to serial 103 (`ONLYWORLDS_VERSION` `'00.34.00'`).
- **[The landing profile](/docs/schema/profiles#landing)** (serial 103): a world's first picture at a door, read from the graph the visitor is served. It adds no field. The [lens profile](/docs/lenses/graph) defines degree and out-degree in the same serial.
- **[A signed-out reader of a closed world's address is sent to sign in](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees)**, and back: a `302` to the sign-in page with the same address, for the world's page, an element's and the graph page of a world with no public page. A signed-in reader who may not read it still meets the `404` page, and a made-up address is sent on the same way.
- **[Listing a tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool): a listed tool's code is read, public or private.** The source is public, or private with read access for the maintainers and one sentence on why it is private; a tool whose code can't be read is not listed. The keys and data lines are always read in the code, marked **read in private source** where nobody outside can check them, and a listed row no longer rests them on the maker's word.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.6.1: the read skill counts a world as the draw skill draws it.** A Zone is counted as an element, with its links; placement, left out of the count unless asked, is Map, Pin and Marker only. Before, a Zone was placement in the count and a node in the drawing, so the two totals differed on any world with Zones.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.6.0: the draw skill's graph is the Python package's `world_graph`** (onlyworlds 0.101.0 or later), so a world drawn by the skill shows the nodes, edges and left-out counts the [graph reader](/docs/lenses/graph) gives. A Zone is a node, a link to itself a small loop.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.31.0: the Open with menu leaves out a tool that cannot open the element, and invite links carry `LandsOn`.** `OWOpeners.For` takes the element (`OWOpenersOptions.Element`) and reads the tools list's `element_skips`, as TypeScript 4.126.0 does; a link says where it opens, `site` or `dashboard`. `OWGraph.Read` without a lens is about twice as fast, with no answer changed.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.101.1: the review readers read any mapping.** `proposed_mark_of` and `hands_on_as_proposed` took only a `dict`, so an element or a mark held in another mapping read as settled; they read any mapping now, as TypeScript reads any object.

## 2026-11-01

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.126.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.101.0: the Open with menu leaves out a tool that cannot open the element.** Pass the element as served (`{ element }`, `element=`); a tool whose `open.element_skips` in the [tools list](/docs/development/list-your-tool#open-with) names a mark it carries (room, hidden, proposed, map space, a reading aid) has no row. The Waymark passes its element on its own.
- **[The Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.16.0: Sign in with your browser on a phone or tablet.** The browser hands you back to Obsidian by a link, and finishes the sign-in if Obsidian was closed meanwhile.
- **[A person link takes where it opens](/docs/development/api/members#managing-members):** `lands_on`, `site` (the default) or `dashboard`, the Dashboard's /join page; every link carries it. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.125.0's `createWorldLink` takes `landsOn`.
- **[Reading a lens as another reader skips a lens that reader cannot see](/docs/development/api/lenses):** its row carries `skipped: "not_found"` and `reading: null`. Typed in [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.125.0 (`OwWorldLens.skipped`).
- **[Which of your worlds holds a story](/docs/development/api/signin#the-account):** `GET /api/v2/account/worlds/holding/{narrative_id}`, the account's own worlds only.
- **[A lost join answer is read by sending the code again](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links):** an invite link's code your own account already used answers `409` [`already_member`](/api/errors/#already_member) and spends nothing, and every `already_member` carries the world's id as `world_id` in the envelope; a code someone else spent stays `404`.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.30.0: `OWRecord`, the record profile's reader, and the account's tokens** (`ListAccountTokensAsync`, `RevokeAccountTokenAsync`); a path value that is not one segment is refused before sending, the link forwarder takes a game's own scheme, and doubles go through the SDK's JSON exactly under Mono.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.124.2 orders tied records by code point, and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.100.2 reads only ASCII digits in a record's `played_at`**, as the record profile says: an id above U+FFFF sorted out of place in TypeScript, and a full-width year read as a date in Python.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.124.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.100.1 read a world's records in one pass**: `recordsOf` and `records_of` give the same answers as before, without scanning the world again for each record.
- **[An account's OnlyWorld](/docs/development/api/signin#the-account)**: `GET /api/v2/account` serves the one world that stands for the account (the person's choice, else the oldest world it owns), and `PATCH` changes it.
- **[The record profile](/docs/schema/profiles#record)** (serial 102): a game's run kept in the world its Characters live in, as an Event carrying `x_record`, so a character carries what they did from run to run.
