[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.124.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.100.0 read a Character's records as `recordsOf` / `records_of`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.123.5 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.99.4 carry Little Lens in their tools list**, so the Open with menu offers it for a world and for an element.

## 2026-10-31

- **[Little Lens](/docs/tools) is on the tools list**: a world as a web of its elements and links, read only, a public world with no account. Read against the [seven lines](/docs/development/list-your-tool) on 2026-10-31.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.123.4: the Waymark's menu closes when focus comes back to the page somewhere else, and Tab reaches every row in Safari.** A window that lost focus kept the menu open, and focus returning on another control left it open over the page; Safari's Tab skipped the rows that are links. Read in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.
- **[A world's proposed Laws](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com) are voted on from its page**: the owner's and the members' pages show each with its tally, and a reader with a Character in the world presses Aye or Nay.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.99.3: a trailing newline no longer passes a path segment or a review day**, as it never did in TypeScript.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.123.3 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.99.2: a value in a request path that is not one segment is refused before sending.** The API decodes an escaped slash before it routes, so 4.122.1's escape alone did not keep an id carrying `/` on its route. Every type, id and field is now checked first (letters, digits, `_`, `.`, `-`) and refused naming it, nothing sent; every id the API serves passes.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.123.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.99.1: an Open with row never carries a script link.** A tools row whose link uses `javascript:`, `data:` or another scheme that opens no app, or does not begin with its scheme, is refused naming the row; the [tools list](/docs/development/list-your-tool) refuses it too, and now takes an app's own scheme with `needs: device`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn) 4.123.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.99.0: the site is "You are here" on itself, and the Open with menu's links open in a new tab.** A surface whose `tool` is `onlyworlds` gets the site's own row as "You are here"; the drawn menu greys your own row, opens a web link in a new tab and draws a smaller mark (0.6em since 4.123.1). schema-dist serial 101 (a sentence on the [strand](/docs/schema/profiles#strand) page) in both.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.122.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.98.2: an id never changes the route asked.** Every type, id and field a call puts in a path is sent as one escaped segment (TypeScript) or refused before sending when it is not one (Python's `draught`, as its other calls already did).
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.29.0: `OWLinkListener` hands the link as received** beside its reading, and its dispatch can decline.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.122.0: `listAccountTokens` and `revokeAccountToken`**, the account's tokens and a [revoke](/docs/development/api/signin#consent-and-revoking) by id, so a tool done with a sign-in can end it.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#write) v0.28.0: sign-in, the draught, the AI routes and `RequestAsync`**; a client with no key and no `World` is built.
- **[The Agent-Council](/docs/tools/council): an AI seat acts on its own key**, as the committed delegate its Character is, paid from its sponsor's rating; votes and endorsements there come from seats.
- **[A world made on the account](/docs/getting-started/keys) takes the World's extension keys**, on `POST /api/v2/account/worlds` and the hand-over, as `PATCH /world` does.
- **[The strand](/docs/schema/profiles#strand) on the world page**: the Overview draws it after the Charter, Start here and the Domains.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#a-world-on-the-account) 4.121.1: `draught` reads with `world` set**, from an account token or with no key, as the [draught](/docs/development/api/draught) is served; it was refused before sending.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#write) v0.27.0: the strand, and most of the TypeScript SDK's client calls.** `OWReadingAids.Strand` and `OWStrand.Of`; upsert, adopt, the hidden mark, your own Character, the roster and health; the account's worlds, keys, invite links and seats; images and files through a ticket. A transport of your own must send `OWRequest.BodyBytes`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-strand) 4.121.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.98.1: the strand at the front door.** schema-dist serial 100: [`reading_aids`](/docs/development/api/members#reading-aids) on `GET /me` and an agent's join reply carries the world's [strand](/docs/schema/profiles#strand) fourth, its six slots as your key reads the world; `OwServedReadingAids` types it.
- **[Keys](/docs/getting-started/keys): a world key's `name` is trimmed and at most 80 characters**, `null` or blank for none, the same rule as a new world's `key_name`; TypeScript 4.121.0's `mintWorldKey` takes `null`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-strand) 4.120.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.98.0: a strand begins when its story is chosen.** schema-dist serial 99: [`x_strand`](/docs/schema/profiles#strand) needs only the story; its event and place may be null or absent, and every tool shows the same half-built strand. TypeScript 4.120.0 also stops sending `X-OW-Agent` on hide, unhide, `setMyCharacter` and `mintWorldKey`, where the API declares and reads none.

## 2026-10-30

- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-strand) 4.119.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.97.0: the strand.** schema-dist serial 98 adds the [strand profile](/docs/schema/profiles#strand): a world's story in miniature, named on the World by `x_strand` (a Narrative, one of its Events and that Event's place), with the story's hero and where the hero is now. `strandOf` and `strand_of` read its five slots as one reader sees the world, and whether then and now are the same place.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.118.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.96.0: the Open with menu knows when you are offline.** `openersFor` and `openers_for` take `online`; offline, every row whose link is a web address says "Needs a connection". The drawn menu reads the browser's connection at each open, greys those rows and leads nowhere, and its sheet and toast now clear a phone's home indicator.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn) 4.117.2: the Open with menu opens as the sheet when neither side has room.** Wider than 480 px, a mark in the middle of a narrow window or pane opened a menu that still crossed the edge; it now takes the sheet from the bottom it already uses on a phone.
- **[A world's address](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees) sends a member to their own page for it.** On onlyworlds.com, `/w/<world id>` takes every signed-in member, at any role, to their page for the world, as an element's address already did; a visitor with no membership still reads the public page.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.117.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.95.2: schema-dist serial 97, no code change.** The [address profile](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses) now states that a writer given an empty world, type, id or lens refuses by that part's own reason, as both packages already did; four new conformance cases hold it.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.26.0: Open with, `ow://` links, the reading aids and list by a link.** `OWOpeners.For` and `OWTools.All` give the rows of an Open with menu; `OWLinkListener` and an opt-in Windows forwarder take an `ow://` link clicked outside Unity; `OWReadingAids` reads the Charter, Guide and Domains as `GET /me` serves them; `OWListParams.Filter` lists by a single link. Schema-dist serials 88 to 96, and an export declares folder format 0.3.21.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn) 4.117.0: the Open with menu keeps its Escape, opens toward its room, and your own row closes it.** A page's Escape no longer also runs, the fan-out opens left or up where its box would clip it, "You are here" is no longer a link, and the bottom sheet starts at 480 px wide.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.116.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.95.1: schema-dist serial 96, no reader change.** A [graph](/docs/lenses/graph) shows a guest's own hidden element only when a lens keeps it, marked hidden; without a lens it is left out with no count, as both packages already read it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#invite-links) 4.116.0: `members()` on an account token.** A client made with the token and `world` reads that world's [members list](/docs/development/api/members) as the token's access there.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants) 4.115.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.95.0: schema-dist serial 95.** A hidden element a guest only knows of is not in its [lens](/docs/lenses) view at all; a read mark on a body read with no key counts for nobody (`readMarkOf`'s `{ keyless }`, `read_mark_of(keyless=True)`); and `dragField(a, b)` gives the link a drag between two types writes, from schema-dist's `pairs.json`.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com#features) draws the Open with menu beside a name.** The world and element pages, the owner's, a member's and the public ones, carry the OnlyWorlds mark at the end of the name, and the mark opens the TypeScript SDK's [Open with menu](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn).
- **[The members list](/docs/development/api/members) answers an account token.** `GET /api/v2/members` with `world=<id>` reads as the token's own access there: the owner as the owner's keys, a member as their own member key, and an account with no access gets a `404` ([Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys#reading-a-world-with-an-account-token)).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.114.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.94.0: schema-dist serial 94 in the lens and charter readers.** An anchor the reader cannot see is counted for the owner before any other reason, and for any other reader it is not there at all; a note goes with its anchor; a `full` row of a hidden element carries `hidden: true`, as the API serves it; an anchor of White_Space alone is no anchor, so a lens of only such strings is a filter; and a proposed element is in no Domain's hubs. The TypeScript menu entry (`@onlyworlds/sdk/waymark`) also no longer loads the world-file code.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#the-open-with-menu-drawn) 4.113.0: the Open with menu, drawn.** The subpath `@onlyworlds/sdk/waymark` puts the OnlyWorlds mark beside a world's or an element's name and opens the menu from it, with its stylesheet at `@onlyworlds/sdk/waymark.css`; the main import never loads it.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.93.1**: `type_word` gives the standard's name when a names map's `types` or `supertypes` is present as `null`, as the names profile says and the npm SDK reads it.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#the-packet) names its reader.** Every draught carries `reader` beside `mode`: the rung the packet was read as (`public`, `owner`, `co_builder`, `contributor`, `guest`, `seat` or `player`), kept under `compact`. `mode` stays what was asked; the public reader reads as itself in either mode.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.112.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.93.0: schema-dist serial 93 in the lens readers.** A link reaches only an element of its field's target type, a Pin's generic pair only by an exact slug and never as a graph edge, an anchor the reader cannot see is counted for the owner as `anchor_not_visible`, a known-of stub keeps its base and public fields with every other unset, and a graph edge's index counts the list as served. `draughtText` / `draught_text` say "Read as the public reader." from the draught's `reader`; the Python package also serves an empty single link as stored.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#getting-started) 1.109.0**: when a new world's first steps send you to the chart or the writing room, the room's first row says which step you are on and offers Back to Grow your world.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#getting-started) 1.108.2**: while a new world shows its first steps, the first press of Tab offers Skip to the first steps.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees): a Zone is a place, not a part of a map.** A public world's page shows its Zones as a section after Locations, a Zone has a page of its own, and the world's graph draws Zones as points; Pins and Markers still belong to their map and send the visitor to the world's page.
- **[A world as a graph](/docs/lenses/graph), [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-as-a-graph) 4.111.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.92.0: a Zone is a node by default** (schema-dist serial 92). A Zone is content that gains its place through linked Markers, so it is no longer map space: `worldGraph` / `world_graph` keep Zones as nodes without a flag, a public reader's graph included, and `mapSpace` / `map_space=True` brings back Maps, Pins and Markers.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.110.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.91.0**: `openersFor` / `openers_for` check the whole address, so an address with a malformed lens, moment or form is refused as the address writer refuses it; the rows are unchanged.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.90.1**: a share (`include_hidden=False`) leaves out only element files for the hidden mark, those directly in a type folder, as the TypeScript writer does; a marked file elsewhere travels as it is.

## 2026-10-29

- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#features) 1.102.0 to 1.108.1**: the Web reads through a lens, and Keep a lens on an element saves one; Bring in a file, notes or JSON makes a world from a file before one exists; an element's page names the tools that last changed it; a message says "someone you cannot see" for a sender or recipient hidden from you; a new world's Home asks Start from nothing or Bring in a world I have, and one card leads through its first steps with Next; and Download the whole world and a JSON export leave out who wrote each element.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.5.3: folder format 0.3.21.** A world set up from a pack is stamped with the current format, and the conventions page lists `doors` among the fields a tool keeps as served and never writes.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees) and [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads): `created_by` is for a world's members.** A keyless read carries no `created_by`, as it already carried none of the four door keys, and a keyless `created_by=` filter is a `422` naming it. Members read and filter as before.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.109.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.90.0: a world file leaves out who wrote each element.** `created_by`, `changed_by`, `changed_via`, `changed_client` and `doors` leave every element file by default, which is then written in the folder's own form; `includeAuthorFields: true` and `include_author_fields=True` keep every file byte for byte, for an archive of your own world.
- **[Filters](/docs/development/api/reads#filters): every single link is a filter on its list**, such as `?parent_location=<id>` for a place's rooms, and an empty value lists the elements that link to nothing (`?characters=` lists the Messages addressed to everyone). [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.108.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.89.1 send an empty filter value; before, they dropped it and listed every element.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.108.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.89.0**: `doorWhere` and `door_where` give a door history entry's where in a "Last shaped" line's words (`in Atlas`, `on onlyworlds.com`, `with an AI`, `by OnlyWorlds`, else `in code`).
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees): a world page says who cites it.** Its **Cited by** section names the public worlds whose public text holds an address of it, each with its count of citing elements and up to five of them; a private citer never shows.
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders) 0.3.21, [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.107.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.88.0 (schema-dist serial 91)**: a member's copy of a world keeps `doors` as served, beside the other door fields, and the folder checker reads it as the server's; the Python writers stamp 0.3.21.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.107.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.87.0**: `doorsOf` / `doors_of` read an element's door history, which tools wrote it, newest first, with the one that made its last write marked.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.106.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.86.1**: the TypeScript types carry `doors` on every element; the Python `push` never sends it, so a folder copy without it is no edit.
- **[Reads](/docs/development/api/reads)**: every element carries `doors` for a world's members, each tool that wrote it with the time of its last write there (at most 16). It and `changed_by`, `changed_via` and `changed_client` are now for members only: a [keyless read](/docs/development/api/public#what-it-does-not-serve) carries none of them.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.105.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.86.0**: `openersFor` / `openers_for` give an Open with menu's rows for a world or an element, onlyworlds.com first, then each listed tool with a door for it, each with its state word; `TOOLS` is the tools list baked at the release.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#your-onlyworlds-account) 3.15.0**: Sign in with your browser on desktop, with no token to copy; a phone keeps the token field.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp)**: `list_elements`'s `supertype` reads the value as the list routes do, trimmed and without regard to case; an empty string lists the elements with none.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.104.3 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.85.2: schema-dist serial 90**: the calendar fixture's new refusal (an intercalary day on a calendar with no months, `after_month` 0 included), which both readers already refused; `supertype` and `subtype` are described as a classification within the element's type.
- **[Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#filters)**: `?supertype=` and `?subtype=` trim the value and compare it without regard to case, as the profiles read them, so `?supertype=message` finds the Messages; an empty value lists the elements with none.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-roster)**: an agent's row names who answers for it, `sponsor_member`: the sponsor's row id, `owner`, or `null`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.104.2**: [`completeSignIn`](/docs/development/api/signin#registered-tools) leaves a sign-in pending when a return carries another state, so a forged link to a native app's scheme cannot cancel it; `OwMember` declares `sponsor_member`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.104.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.85.1**: the review queue calls say the owner's read key reads every row. Comments only.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught)**: with `messages` named, a Message whose `characters` list is empty (addressed to every member) is in the section too, as the reader may see it.
- **[Proposals](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-queue)**: the owner reads the review queue and an element's proposals with the world's read key, or its write key with no PIN: every row. A demo-range key and the keyless public view still get `404`.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#registered-tools)**: the first native app is registered, the [Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) (`obsidian`), with a loopback return and `obsidian://onlyworlds-signin`.
- **A World graph section, and element type as the one word for the 22.** [World graph](/docs/world-graph) sits between Schema and Tools: an overview of a world as typed elements and links and the three ways to read part of one, then [Lenses](/docs/lenses), [the draught](/docs/world-graph/draught) as a concept and [A world as a graph](/docs/lenses/graph). The lens routes move to the API section as [Lenses](/docs/development/api/lenses), and their old address points there; [the draught's route](/docs/development/api/draught) keeps its page and its address, and names its Character "the Character" or "they". The API section moves down the menu, after Games, and [A worked example](/docs/schema/example) closes the Schema section. Every page now says element type, never category, and the docs' word check refuses the old word; [Fields](/docs/schema/fields#base-fields) says how supertype and subtype nest inside an element type, and the addresses under `/docs/schema/element_categories/` stay.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.104.0**: [Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#registered-tools) from a native app: `signInWithOnlyWorlds` takes a loopback address with the port the app bound, or the app's own scheme, and `completeSignIn` takes the returned address as `url`, compared exactly (scheme, host, port and path).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.103.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.85.0 (schema-dist serial 89)**: the calendar readers refuse a `grain` with nothing left after trimming white space; an intercalary day whose `after_month` is below 0 or past the last month, or any intercalary day on a calendar with no months; and they print the default template for a `format` or `format_intercalary` that is not a string, where they used to fail. The message readers' fixture now checks that `created_at` is read as an instant (with an offset and a fraction of a second) and that the newest Note counts wherever it stands in the list; both packages already read them that way.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#registered-tools)**: a registered tool can be a native app (`native: true` in the list): a desktop app may return to a loopback address on any port it picks, and a desktop or mobile app to a custom scheme, matched exactly. A web tool's return addresses stay exact `https://` ones. No native app is registered yet.
- **[Public pages](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees)**: a signed-in member who opens a link to one of the world's elements is sent to their own read-only page for it when their role reads it, as the owner already was; anyone else reads the public page.
- **[Writes](/docs/development/api/writes)**: an element id is unique across every type too. A `POST`, a creating `PUT` or a `/bulk` item whose id this world holds as another type is a `409` [`id_conflict`](/api/errors/#id_conflict), and the message names the type that holds it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.102.5 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.84.3**: a calendar whose `grain` is an empty string is no calendar, as the calendar profile requires a grain; the reader shows bare numbers.
- **[AI agents](/docs/development/agents)**: the join answer's table lists `base_url` (the site the seat joined) and `next` (what to do next), which the answer always carried.
- **[Proposals](/docs/development/api/proposals#the-queue)** and **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links)**: three limits the pages left out, now stated and checked against the API document: a decline's or review's reason is up to 2,000 characters, a proposals list pages up to 1,000 rows (100 when `limit` is left out), and a link expires in 7 days when `days` is left out.
- **[Account tokens](/docs/getting-started/keys#account-tokens)**: a write to the account routes signed in only by onlyworlds.com's own session cookie needs its CSRF token in `X-CSRFToken`, or it is a `403` [`csrf_required`](/api/errors/#csrf_required) and nothing is written; an `ow_a_` token or an `X-Session-Token` needs none.
- **[LLM guide](/docs/development/llm-guide)**: its word mapping sends a group to an institution, a collective or a family by how it holds together, where one line said institution alone; its error codes, SDK names, types and key kinds are now checked against the API document and the SDK on every build, with the key tables on [Getting started](/docs/getting-started) and [Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys).
- **[The API document lists its limits](/docs/development/api-reference#limits)**: `info.x-ow-limits` in `openapi.json` gives the API's limits, each with its value, and the pages' numbers are checked against it. Corrected: a `?head=true` `304` is given back to the request budget up to 120 a minute per key or account token, and a `200` or any past those counts ([Changes](/docs/development/api/changes)); image and file upload tickets are 200 a world in the last 24 hours, not per calendar day ([Images](/docs/development/api/images#limits), [errors](/api/errors/#quota_exceeded)); an image is at most 15 MB, the ticket's `max_bytes`, and an account's 1 GB can be raised ([Images](/docs/development/api/images)); the 50 open proposals a world are the proposer's own ([Proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals), [errors](/api/errors/#too_many_open)); the newest 500 marked elements are what the queue lists, not a refusal; the 50 pending a world counts invites and unused person and player links, and an agent's link refuses `max_uses` ([Members](/docs/development/api/members#managing-members)); the API reference's limits table adds search's `limit`, that a list `limit` over 1000 is read as 1000 while the change feed refuses one, and that sizes count on a 1024 base (2.5 MB is 2,621,440 bytes).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.102.4**: `OwMember.name` and `OwMe.name` say what the server serves: a person's username on the roster only for the owner's keys and co-builders, an agent's or player's name to every reader.
- **[Lenses](/docs/lenses)**: on the Lenses page a lens needs a name before it saves, and a lens you keep can be deleted, after a confirm that says who chose it.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-roster)**: a person's row on the roster carries `name`, the person's username, for the owner's keys and co-builders only, as the site's Access page shows it; every other reader still gets no `name` on a person row.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.102.3 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.84.2: schema-dist serial 88.** The lens fixture gains a graph case where one element's review mark is `{}` and another's `""`: both are drawn as settled nodes with no mark, as both packages already read them; no code changed.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) 0.25.0: schema-dist serials 86 and 87.** A type's colour group is named by its colour, `blue`, `green`, `pink` or `amber` (Construct pink, Ability amber); a colour word the presentation file cannot draw throws `OWPresentationException`; an empty review mark is settled in every reader, and `OWApiError.IsReviewMarkEmpty` names keel's refusal of one.
- **[List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool#open-with)**: the tools list is served as JSON at `https://www.onlyworlds.com/tools.json`, to any origin, cached for an hour, with an `ETag`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.102.1 and 4.102.2, [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.84.1: schema-dist serial 87**: an empty review mark (`""`, white space only, `[]` or `{}`) is settled, never a doubt, in every reader of the mark: the [review profile](/docs/schema/profiles#review)'s readers, the ask and pack readers, a lens and its graph, and `draughtText` / `draught_text`, which no longer labels it proposed. The lens fixture's new graph case holds one element of each of the 22 element types.
- **[A world with its first elements](/docs/getting-started/keys#a-world-with-its-first-elements)**: `POST /api/v2/account/worlds/handover` makes a world on the account and writes its first elements in one transaction, all or nothing, refused as [`handover_refused`](/api/errors/#handover_refused).
- **[CORS](/docs/development/api/cors) names every origin and header the API allows**: `https://onlyworlds.com` and `https://www.onlyworlds.com` with their schemes, and the two `http` ones the page left out (`http://onlyworlds.com`, `http://www.onlyworlds.com`); the request headers in full, the common `Accept`, `Accept-Encoding`, `DNT`, `Origin`, `User-Agent`, `X-CSRFToken` and `X-Requested-With` included.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.5.2: the draw skill names the four colour groups by their colours** (schema-dist serial 86: Construct pink, Ability amber), and a presentation file whose colour word is not in its own order stops the script by name instead of drawing a default colour.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#tools)'s hints sentence says `bulk_apply` is not idempotent**, as the server marks it; `update_element` and `edit_links` are destructive and idempotent.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#roles)' roles table says what a guest also sees**: the messages addressed to it or to everyone, the world's Charter and the Objects it names, and the stubs of what its Character's sheet points to. Under the table: a seat the owner gives a Character to play may change that Character, never delete it, and a Read mark is written only by its own reader, the owner and co-builders included (`403 not_reader`).
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#roles) draws the roles table**: one invented world of 36 elements read by the owner, a co-builder, a contributor, a guest and the public, each with what it sees and what it changes in the table's own words (the public's from the [Hidden elements](/docs/development/api/hidden#view-as) page), and a line that a player reads as a guest of one Character and writes nothing. The dots show the shape; the table stays the rule.
- **An empty review mark is no mark**: a write carrying an empty `x_review_proposed` (`""`, whitespace only, `[]` or `{}`) is refused `422` [`review_mark_empty`](/api/errors/#review_mark_empty), and a stored one reads as absent, so an element is still proposed only while its mark is neither `null` nor empty ([the review profile](/docs/schema/profiles#review), [Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public), [the draught](/docs/development/api/draught), [MCP](/docs/development/mcp)).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.102.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.84.0: schema-dist serial 86**: the four colour groups are named by their colour, `blue`, `green`, `pink` and `amber` (`ELEMENT_GROUPS`, `GROUP_COLORS`, `GROUP_ORDER`, `groupOf`), and Construct is drawn pink and Ability amber; the `FAMILY_*` names stay, deprecated, until 5.0. In both packages a world graph's node carries its type's colour word in `family`. The [schema](/docs/schema/) page's field figure heads each group by its colour, and the [LLM guide](/docs/development/llm-guide) lists the 22 element types A to Z.
- **Sharper definitions on the [Creature](/docs/schema/element_categories/creature), [Collective](/docs/schema/element_categories/collective), [Family](/docs/schema/element_categories/family), [Species](/docs/schema/element_categories/species) and [Title](/docs/schema/element_categories/title) pages**, from an outside review of the docs: a Creature is told from a Character by reasoning and social complexity, not by how much the story follows it; a Collective has no formal governance of its own, though it can have a `composition` and an Institution as `operator`; a Family's tie is one that passes down; a Species is a biological kind or one set apart by origin or culture; and Title describes a convention for who held it when, a Relation with supertype `Tenure`. The schema page, [Conventions](/docs/schema/conventions) and the LLM guide carry the same wording.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.83.2**: the doctor reads a Law with supertype Charter and no mark as lore, not a finding, as the checks profile says; its house rules are never applied.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees)**: an element's page reads its whole address, a moment and a lens included; the owner, signed in, goes on to their own page for it; a public Pin, Marker or Zone leads to the world's page.

## 2026-10-28

- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#getting-started) 1.98.0, 1.98.1, 1.99.0, 1.100.0 and 1.101.0**: the first screen says Create a world and Explore, with Watch how it works and Other ways in below them; a new world's Home shows five steps that light up as the world has them; a world from OnlyWorlds or a world file opens before it is saved to this browser; and Home shows a world's charter, guide and domains first, kept out of the lists, Search and Charts.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.5.1: the graph, map, timeline and survey leave out a world's reading aids** (a Charter, Guide or Domain marked `x_charter_of`) and say so once; a room or reading-aid mark counts by presence, so whitespace alone is no mark.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity) 0.24.0: addresses, lenses and the Charter.** `OWAddress`, `OWLens` with `OWGraph`, and `OWCharter` read the address, lens and charter profiles; the client calls the lens routes, and an account token reads the world it names. An export declares folder format 0.3.20.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.101.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.83.1: schema-dist serial 85**: the field descriptions call supertype and subtype a classification of the element, and say a Title's grant and revoke dates are its own (a holder's span is a Relation); no field or reader changed.
- **[Proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals#accept-decline-withdraw)**: a retry of a proposal after a failed answer never proposes twice, and a key whose proposal is gone since answers `409 idempotency_error`; a decline needs the PIN and refuses a keeper's own proposal (`403 own_proposal`); the queue's `new_element` rows are every element carrying an `x_review_proposed` mark.
- **Easy Mobile leaves the Tools menu.** It is no longer developed; its page still answers at its address and is listed with the [legacy tools](/docs/tools/legacy#easy-mobile).
- **[Proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals) draws a proposal's life**: the propose (`expires` 1 to 365 days from now, default 30), the undecided states `open` and `lapsed`, and the accept, decline and withdraw on them, each with its route, who may call it, its answer, its refusals (`403 own_proposal`, `409 stale_proposal`, `422` once decided) and where it leaves the row; then what the switch makes of an AI seat's `PATCH`, `PUT` or link edit and of its new elements, settled by `review`, and the queue's states and two kinds of row, from the API's own document.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.101.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.83.0: schema-dist serial 84**, whose new lens and charter cases both readers pass as they were; `readingAidOf` / `reading_aid_of` answers which reading aid an element's type and supertype make it (`charter`, `guide`, `domain`), with the mark ignored. TypeScript 4.100.2 types `x_served_trimmed` on `me()` and `members()`, as the API serves it when a lens was left out of `lenses`.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.5.0: a world set up from a pack marks its Charter and Guide as reading aids** (`x_charter_of`) and is stamped folder format 0.3.20; a plan with a second Charter is refused, and a world set up earlier gets the mark from its pack's re-stamp note. The skills say what a lens is and where its readers are.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught)'s figure**: `messages` holds the Messages she sent as well as those that name her, and the lens frames sit beside the parameters, read with no `lens` only when no parameter but `compact` is set.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught) draws a Character in one read**: the ten sections served by default round her (`self`, `role` and `work` never cut by the budget), the three served only when `components` names them (`messages`, `room`, `proposed`) and the lenses beside them, and the nine query parameters with their values and defaults, from the API's own document for `GET /api/v2/character/{id}/context`. The page now says `proposed` holds every element still carrying `x_review_proposed`, not only an AI seat's, and that an account token with no access to the world reads as the public.
- **[Fields](/docs/schema/fields#what-a-write-body-may-carry) draws what a write body may carry**: each key a `POST`, `PUT` or `PATCH` body carries is stored (the base fields with their caps, `hidden` on a create, the category's own fields, the extension prefixes up to 64 KB), tolerated and ignored (`world`, `created_by`, `changed_by`, `changed_via`, `changed_client`, and `id` and `hidden` where a method ignores them) or rejected with `422` (`type`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `change_seq`, `x_served_trimmed` and any other unknown field), from the API's own write schemas. The page now lists `changed_by`, `changed_via` and `changed_client` among the server-kept fields, says the path decides the id on `PUT` and `PATCH`, and names `x_served_trimmed`, the one `x_` key that is the server's own and refused on a write.
- **The conventions page splits in three.** [Modelling conventions](/docs/schema/conventions) keeps the advice on modelling a world at its old address. The extension profiles move to [Profiles](/docs/schema/profiles) in the Schema section, with [Messages](/docs/schema/profiles/messages) and [Addresses](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses) on pages of their own, and lenses to a section of their own: [Lenses](/docs/lenses), [A world as a graph](/docs/lenses/graph) and [Lenses on the API](/docs/development/api/lenses). Profiles, Messages and Lenses each say first what every reader does, then what onlyworlds.com adds. The old anchors on the conventions page still answer, each with a paragraph that points to the new page.
- **[World Starter](/docs/development/#sdks)**, a template repository for a web tool on world data, is public: it reads the demo world out of the box, or your own with a read key. [Build on OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/#sdks) and the [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#install) page point to it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.100.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.82.1**: a lens's rows below the public rung keep a link to a proposed element the reader sees, and a note keyed to one, as the API serves them; no row carries a stored `x_served_trimmed`.
- **Corrections to the [schema](/docs/schema/) pages**, from an outside review of the docs: [Ability](/docs/schema/element_categories/ability) no longer calls itself a passive trait, [Trait](/docs/schema/element_categories/trait) no longer says Constructs are not linked to actors, and [Construct](/docs/schema/element_categories/construct) no longer describes Phenomena as only natural. [Relation](/docs/schema/element_categories/relation) says `actor` is optional, [Narrative](/docs/schema/element_categories/narrative) separates the dates it is told from the time it is about, and [Location](/docs/schema/element_categories/location) says how a building is a Location, an Object or both. [Conventions](/docs/schema/conventions) gains a magic system across three categories and says a Zone with no Markers is valid; [Fields](/docs/schema/fields) says supertype and subtype are a world's own kinds and that a valid element is not always enough for a map or timeline view; every field table says integers are whole numbers; the definitions table on the [schema page](/docs/schema/) comes before the counts. [Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys) no longer says no request passes a world id: an account token and a read of an open world name one. The [LLM guide](/docs/development/llm-guide) names its six headings and the SDK's colour groups as reading aids, not part of the standard, and drops ChatGPT Canvas.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public) draws what a stranger reads of an open world**: the three kinds of element the public reader removes (hidden, still proposed and a parlor's room elements, with the links to them and their counts), the three ways in (the keyless reads, the public page and its graph), what is never shown and the one `404` for a world that answers nothing, from the platform's public reader and the API's own document. The page's opening now names room elements among what the public never reads.
- **[Worlds](/docs/schema/worlds#timeline) draws how a world counts time**: the steps a world names, each a count of its basic unit, and the line of whole numbers from `time_range_min` to `time_range_max` with the world's now and one element's date on it, from the demo world Moppetopia and the field names the standard and the API give them. The now row names both spellings: `time_range_current` in the API, `time_current` in the standard and a world folder. `time_basic_unit` is described as the unit every date counts in, and the page points to the calendar profile.
- **[Idempotency](/docs/development/api/writes#idempotency)**: a retry of `POST /api/v2/{type}` after a lost or failed answer never writes a second element once the first is written; it returns that element with the first status and `Idempotent-Replay: true`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#reading-aids) 4.100.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.82.0: schema-dist serial 83.**
  - `charterOf` / `charter_of` and `domainsOf` / `domains_of` read what a world's front door shows first: the Charter, the Guide and the Domains.
  - The [room and charter marks](/docs/schema/profiles) are read by their presence.
  - A lens's rows for a reader below the owner are the bodies the API serves that reader. The previous releases handed hidden elements' ids, with their sentences, to such a reader in the notes of a lens kept as an element.
  - World folders are written at [format v0.3.20](/docs/schema/folders#worldjson).
  - The Python doctor reads the marked Charter and flags a Guide or Domain that names a hidden or deleted element.
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders#a-list-trimmed-for-its-reader)**: `x_served_trimmed` is written on a message's addressing and a lens's anchor fields only; any other link leaves an unseen id out with no mark.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp) `get_draught`** with no `lens` reads one frame per lens you chose, as the context route does; `/health` and `/health/deep` now declare that they take no credential, which the [credentials figure](/docs/development/api-reference#which-credential-each-route-takes) follows.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#reading-aids): `reading_aids`** on `GET /me` and an agent's join reply: the world's marked Charter with its rules, its Guide and its Domains, each with its sentence and hubs. The front door's `charter` is now the newest Charter that carries the mark `x_charter_of`; only the owner and co-builders may set the mark ([`keeper_only`](/api/errors/#keeper_only)).
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin) draws its four steps** as hops between the tool, the browser and onlyworlds.com, with the authorize page's parameters, the token route's fields and answer and the code's lifetime from the API's own document. The page's refused requests now name every check (a `code_challenge` that is not 43 characters, a `state` over 1024), its pages are sent `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, not `no-referrer`, and a rate-limited exchange leaves the code unspent.
- **[The Lenses page](/docs/lenses#on-onlyworldscom)**: Start from finds elements by a word, the most linked first, and offers a world's Domains as packages of starting points.
- **[A new key](/docs/getting-started/keys)**: the message for your AI also has it keep the `.env` file out of version control.
- **[API overview](/docs/development/api-reference#which-credential-each-route-takes) draws which credential each route takes**: every operation of the API by route family, counted under no key, a key alone, a key and its PIN or an account token as the API's own document lists them, with each route under the credentials it takes; generated from that document when the docs are built, its keyless reads held to the list on [Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#the-keyless-reads), and the docs' check fails when the document moves until the figure follows.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#your-onlyworlds-account) 3.14.0: a Sign in command and a Create account command**; Download world and Create world offer the same Sign in dialog when you are signed out. It takes a pasted account token.
- **[World folders](/docs/schema/folders#layout) draws the example world's folder** under the Layout block, Hyperion's category folders with their file counts and three of its elements' names and ids becoming their file names, from schema-dist's `example/hyperion`; every file name is derived by the page's naming rule when the docs are built, and the build fails if one of the example's files breaks it. The page's naming rule now says what Atlas writes: accents removed (José becomes jose) and no hyphen at either end of the slug.
- **[Choosing no lenses](/docs/development/api/lenses)**: an empty lens list is now a choice that keeps a member off its Titles' lenses (`lenses_from: chosen`), and `null` puts it back to never chosen, where the Titles' lenses apply; `PUT /me/lenses` and the members PATCH take both. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.99.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.81.0 send `null`.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp) `get_draught` takes `lens`**, a lens id beside `policy`, read as the [draught's](/docs/development/api/draught#the-policy) `lens` is. The API document now names the keyless reads (no credential, on a world open to anyone) and `Retry-After` on the in-flight 409 of every route that takes an `Idempotency-Key`; the [errors](/api/errors/#idempotency_in_flight) figure's mark is held to it.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#the-look) draws the five page palettes** under the Palette setting, each in its own colours with its contrast numbers, from the platform's palette table; every ratio, and the claim that each text colour clears 4.5 to 1 on every surface, is computed when the docs are built.
- **[A new key](/docs/getting-started/keys) and [a seat for your AI](/docs/development/agents#giving-your-ai-access)**: the key's box on onlyworlds.com has **Copy instructions for your AI**, a message that has your AI write its own `.env` file, say where it put it and check the key with `GET /api/v2/me`; the raw lines are under **Show the lines**.
- **[Errors](/api/errors/#error-codes) draws every code by family and status** above the summary table, from the same list: the codes that answer with `Retry-After` are marked, and the upload host's own codes are counted on one line.
- **[Reading a lens as a seat](/docs/development/api/lenses)**: a co-builder, or the account that sponsors an agent's or a player's seat, may read a lens as that seat with `read_as`, within its own view; [`read_as_owner_only`](/api/errors/#read_as_owner_only) now answers only a reader whose lenses the caller may not set.
- **[A Title's lenses](/docs/development/api/lenses)**: a member that chose no lenses starts with the lenses whose `titles` name a Title its Character holds; `GET /me` and `GET /members` say where a row's lenses come from (`lenses_from`), and a world's lens list says which you start with through a Title (`from_titles`) and, for the owner, which seats do (`title_seats`). SDK 4.97.0 types them.
- **[Reading aids and a lens by when things changed](/docs/lenses)** (schema-dist serial 82): a world's Charter, Guide and Domains carrying the mark `x_charter_of` are its reading aids, shown first at a tool's front door and left out of lore views, a lens keeping one only when its `types` names the pair or it is an anchor; a lens's `changed_within` cuts what changed longer ago than that many days, counted `stale`. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.98.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.80.0 read both (`isCharterElement`, `is_charter_element`), count a lens's ignored notes only where the reader sees the id, and give an element's out-degree (`outDegreeOf`, `out_degree_of`).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.97.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.79.3: a lens's mail part reads whole messages only**: a Message the reader knows only as a stub is never read as mail to everyone, as the API reads it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.97.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.79.2: an umbrella lens no longer enters a child lens the reader knows only as a stub**, as the API reads it: the child counts as an anchor the reader cannot see, so a guest's reading never follows links it was not served.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity)**: the Android run was an emulated player and the WebGL run a headless browser; no phone has run the package yet.

## 2026-10-27

- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#sync-with-onlyworldscom) 1.96.0, 1.97.0, 1.97.1 and 1.97.2**: a world made online links to its page on onlyworlds.com, signing out keeps your worlds syncing on their own keys, and a link to a world or an element from OnlyWorlds, pasted anywhere outside a text field or into Search, opens it.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#the-policy)**: the SDKs take its nine keys; the MCP tool `get_draught` takes eight, not `lens`. **[Worlds](/docs/schema/worlds)** names every writable field of the world, its extension keys and `source` and `producer` included.
- **[Errors](/api/errors/#own_proposal): `own_proposal` and `proposed_only` are type `permission_error`**, as the summary table says. A list also takes `ids`, `created_by`, `as` and `world` ([invalid_request](/api/errors/#invalid_request), [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#filters), the [LLM guide](/docs/development/llm-guide)).
- **[Proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals#propose): `expires` is a date-time 1 to 365 days out**, never a number of days; the [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#proposed-changes) example sends a date.
- **[Changes and export](/docs/development/api/changes#the-feed)**: the feed example carries `epoch_reason`, the [export file](/docs/development/api/changes#the-export-file) shows `schema_version` `00.33.00`, and [only a restore of the whole platform's database rewinds](/docs/development/api/changes#rewinds-and-head) the change sequence, never an owner's restore to a day.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#the-policy) takes nine policy parameters.** The [OpenAPI document](/docs/development/api-reference) is about 3 MB plain and 300 KB compressed. [CORS](/docs/development/api/cors#headers) allows `X-OW-Client` and `If-None-Match` and exposes `ETag`.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-roster): the roster and `/me` carry `lenses`** (and `x_served_trimmed`), and the [members route](/docs/development/api/members#managing-members) changes an agent seat's `sponsor` and a row's `lenses`. [Sign in](/docs/development/api/signin#in-the-sdk): `completeSignIn` returns `expiresAt`.
- **[Worlds](/docs/schema/worlds#platform-fields) lists every field the platform adds to a world**, and a co-builder may send every writable field but `owner_character`. The [folder example](/docs/schema/folders#worldjson) shows Moppetopia's now, 453.
- **[Conventions](/docs/schema/profiles#spine): the spine profile has thirteen keys and 44 fixture cases.** [Fields](/docs/schema/fields#field-types): a number's range is advisory, never enforced. [Marker](/docs/schema/element_categories/marker): a Zone's `shape` says line or polygon, and no minimum of Markers is set.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers): `ROOM_PAIRS` holds nine pairs and the archive conformance set 99 cases.** [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#write) and [Python](/docs/development/python#write) send `X-OW-Client` on element writes, proposals and reviews, not on every write. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) names the `not_openable` refusal.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#resources): `onlyworlds://world` leaves out room and proposed elements** and answers `proposed_left_out`.
- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#write): an export declares folder format 0.3.18** and the package's own version in `producer`; [an archive loads in an Android IL2CPP player and a WebGL player](/docs/development/unity#install), measured since v0.8.0.
- **[The LLM guide](/docs/development/llm-guide) names the toolkit's skills as 5.x calls them**, and every parameter a list takes.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#the-policy)'s `components` names her own element `self`**, the section the packet serves as `character`; the page now says so at both, since `components=character` is a 422.
- **[Schema](/docs/schema#what-links-to-what) draws what links to what**: select a category to see the categories its link fields point at, with the fields named, generated from the SDK's field table at each build and checked against the element pages; each element page opens it on itself. [Conventions](/docs/lenses) shows a world, a lens, a picture and an address in one figure.
- **[Worlds](/docs/schema/worlds#timeline-examples): the fantasy calendar example fixed.** It counted its now in years while its basic unit is the Sun; it now shows the Suns, and its step equivalents.
- **[Getting started](/docs/getting-started) names every key kind in one table** (what each reads, what each writes), and [Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys) corrects the 10-digit key: its reads need the PIN when the world has one, whether the world is open to the public or not; only the demo keys read with the key alone.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.96.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.79.1: the lens and graph readers read the world's now as the API serves it** (`time_range_current`, beside a folder's `time_current`), so a World body passed straight from `GET /world` fences a lens as it should.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught) reads with an account token and on a world open to anyone**: a signed-in tool reads it as you, and a keyless request with `world=<id>` reads what the public may read, never her messages or the roster ([keys](/docs/getting-started/keys#reading-a-world-with-an-account-token), [public reads](/docs/development/api/public)). The horizon now leaves out a known-of stub by the element's own date, as a lens does.
- **[The Lenses page](/docs/lenses#on-onlyworldscom)**: a Library of templates, Copy to another world, Publish as a template, and a second reading in the browser that says whether it agrees with the site's.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.96.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.79.0: `DATE_FIELD` and `dateOf`, `date_of`**, the [lens profile](/docs/lenses)'s date table and an element's date as it reads it, so a timeline dates elements the way a lens does.
- **[The Lenses page](/docs/lenses#on-onlyworldscom)**: a world's page on onlyworlds.com lists its lenses, opens one to change and read it (what it keeps, and what it cut by reason), and on a world open to everyone reads a lens signed out.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-as-a-graph) 4.95.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.78.0: `degreeOf` and `degree_of`**, each element's count of links in a [world graph](/docs/lenses/graph) as the reader sees it, the "most linked" a search ranks by.
- **[Lenses on an account token](/docs/development/api/lenses)**: `GET /world/{id}/lenses` and `PUT /me/lenses` (with `?world=<id>`) take an account token; a row's lenses are set on the members route with `lenses` alone, answering `{id, role, kind, lenses}`; a lens you can no longer see stays in your list, marked. [Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin) answers `expires_at` for a tool that asks a lifetime on its tokens, and a tool may keep your consent through its own sign-outs. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.94.0: `setMemberLenses`, `worldLenses` and `setMyLenses` on an account token, `completeSignIn`'s `expiresAt`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.93.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.77.1: a lens a guest knows only as a stub reads as nothing when named by id**, in the lens and graph readers, as the server reads it. They read the lens's full body whenever its id was in the guest's view, a stub included, so a guest's reading followed starting points the guest is never shown.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#a-world-as-a-graph) 4.93.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.77.0: [a world as a graph](/docs/lenses/graph) (schema-dist serial 81).** `worldGraph` and `world_graph` read a world snapshot, for one reader, as nodes and edges; room, hidden and proposed elements and map space come back only by their flags, a lens keeps exactly its own elements, and every link left out is counted by reason, `outside_lens` for one the lens never reached. Every world read's `counts` is described as keel serves it, for every reader.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.4.1: a Take is left out of the graph and the count like the rest of a room**, by the room profile's nine type-and-supertype pairs; the company pack names who writes the board (the owner and the co-builders) and asks everyone to keep a Note about themselves; new world folders are stamped format 0.3.19.
- **[A world's counts for every reader](/docs/development/api/reads)**: `GET /api/v2/world` carries `counts` for every key and token, counted as that reader may read, not only for the keyless read.
- **[Agent links from a script](/docs/development/agents)** take the form's fields, `label` and `for_email`.
- **[Lenses on the API](/docs/development/api/lenses)**: the world's lenses resolved for you (`GET /api/v2/world/{world_id}/lenses`), the lenses you start with (`PUT /api/v2/me/lenses`, shown on `GET /me` and `GET /members`), and the [draught](/docs/development/api/draught)'s `lens`. In [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.92.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.76.0, beside schema-dist serial 80.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.75.1**: a folder is read in one order on every platform (file names by code point), so a duplicate id keeps the same file on Windows as elsewhere.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.91.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.75.0: schema-dist serial 79.** Four message cases on Notes (addressed to nobody, never in a thread, never unread; any hidden mark leaves one out), which both readers already answer. Python writes folders at [v0.3.19](/docs/schema/folders), and `onlyworlds.lint` is the dist's checker at serial 79.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.74.1**: the pack and the OKF export read an in-prose reference in every address form (a moment, a lens, the world's own id), so one in another form to a hidden element no longer keeps its label and id.
- **[Lenses](/docs/lenses)**: the lens profile (schema-dist serial 78), a saved way of reading a world, and its reader in [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#lenses) 4.91.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.74.0 (`resolveLens`, `resolve_lens`).
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders#a-list-trimmed-for-its-reader) v0.3.19**: a file keeps `x_served_trimmed` exactly as served; a reader reads the field it names as trimmed, never as empty; a push never sends it.
- **[The keys a sign-in token minted](/docs/development/api/signin#consent-and-revoking)** go with it when it is revoked, unless another live token of the tool remains; a key minted with `keep: true` outlives it, and so do the keys of a token made by hand or through a password sign-in, and of a tool that asks each time. `GET /api/v2/account/tokens` lists `keys_minted`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#a-world-on-the-account) 4.90.0**: `mintWorldKey(worldId, { keep: true })`, and `kept` on the answer.
- **[Sending a message as yourself](/docs/development/agents#in-world-messages)** takes an `Idempotency-Key`: a retry with the same key and body arrives once.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#messages) 4.89.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.73.1**: a Character's line (`noteOf`, `note_of`) leaves out a Note carrying any hidden mark, as every other reader does (it read only `true`).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#addresses) 4.89.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.73.0: [addresses](/docs/schema/profiles/addresses) (schema-dist serial 77).** `parseOwLink` and `elementAddress` (`parse_ow_link`, `element_address`) read and write an element's link in its three spellings, with a moment and a lens, refusing by the profile's reasons.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#a-world-on-the-account) 4.88.0: an account token, or no key, reads the world it names.** `new OwV2Client({ apiKey, world })`: `list`, `listAll`, `get` and `getWorld` read with `world=<id>`, every other world call a `TypeError` before sending; a `signal` on those reads; `isRateLimited` and `isServedTrimmedNotWritable`.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#messages) 4.88.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.72.0: [the message profile at serial 76](/docs/schema/profiles/messages)**: a list trimmed for its reader (unseen recipients, an unseen sender, never "to everyone" without a key), `editedBy`, and Notes, which are never messages.
- **[Reading a world with an account token](/docs/getting-started/keys#reading-a-world-with-an-account-token)**: element lists and reads, search, metadata and the world take `Authorization: Bearer ow_a_…` with `world=<id>`, read only, as your own access in that world; a tool that signs you in needs no key per world. Both credentials on one request is [`one_credential`](/api/errors/#one_credential).
- **Words in the toolkit page**: a system to model is named "a political order", in words that are no stand-in for an element type ([Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit)).
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.23.0: [the message profile at serial 76](/docs/schema/profiles/messages)**: unseen recipients, a keyless read, an unseen sender, an edit by another hand and a member's Note, and `IsServedTrimmedNotWritable`.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.22.0**: `OWMessage.IsReadMark` tells a read mark from other Relations.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas) 1.94.0 and 1.95.0**: signing in again revokes the token Atlas made before, and the graph marks elements waiting for review or hidden.

## 2026-10-26

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.4.0: the read skill's three checks are reports** (the survey report, the links report and the consistency report), since a lens now means a saved way of reading a world; the timeline reads calendars through the [Python package](/docs/development/python) (`onlyworlds>=0.68.1`) and says what to install without it.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.21.0: messages between members.** `OWMessage` reads the message profile (addressing, the proved sender, the read mark, unread and threads) on the shared fixture; `OWFrontDoor.MySeats` lists the seats a person answers for; `OWApiError.IsCharacterPlayed` names a refused delete of a played Character.
- **[Sending a message as yourself](/docs/development/agents#in-world-messages)**: `POST /api/v2/account/worlds/{world_id}/messages` with an account token writes a message sent by your own Character, verified by construction.
- **[A message to someone hidden from you](/docs/development/agents#in-world-messages)** carries `x_served_trimmed`, naming the fields that lost someone, and a write carrying it is [`served_trimmed_not_writable`](/api/errors/#served_trimmed_not_writable).
- **[CORS](/docs/development/api/cors#allowed-origins)**: a page served from GitHub Codespaces (`https://*.app.github.dev`) may call the API.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.87.1**: the draught's recent rows declare `door`, which the server has always sent.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.87.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.71.0: the draught as text names what the date left out** ([As text](/docs/development/api/draught#as-text)): a `Not listed` line above the budget line, and a removed member reads "removed from the world".
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.86.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.70.0: a draught as plain text** ([As text](/docs/development/api/draught#as-text)): `draughtText` and `draught_text` give one Character's context packet as text to paste into a prompt or a note, the same bytes from both packages, full or compact. The title says what the record holds up to the horizon, and the last line counts only what the budget left out.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) 4.85.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.69.0: `resolveCalendar` and `resolve_calendar`** are the new names of `resolveCalendarLens` and `resolve_calendar_lens`, with the same answers; a lens now names a cut of a world, not a chosen calendar. The old names still work, deprecated: TypeScript keeps it until 5.0, and Python warns.
- **[Marking messages read](/docs/development/agents#in-world-messages)**: `POST /api/v2/account/worlds/{world_id}/read` adds message ids to the caller's own Read mark, making it when there is none, and answers `{relation, created, skipped}`; a Read mark is now written only by its maker, so any other key's write to one is `403` [`not_reader`](/api/errors/#not_reader), the owner's included.
- **Our words for world content in three headings** ([TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#find-elements-in-a-world): Find elements in a world; [the example world](/docs/schema/example): Locations within Locations): the 22 types' own words, never place or thing. The old anchors still land.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.84.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.68.1: the calendar validator lists its errors instead of throwing** when `bundles.months` has no names list (`0`, an empty string, `false`, `{}`). Reading a calendar was not affected.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.68.0: the calendar profile's reader**, `onlyworlds.calendar`: `read_calendar_value`, `world_calendars`, `resolve_calendar_lens`, `breakdown_step`, `format_step` and `parse_date`, the TypeScript SDK's calendar reader under snake_case names, giving the same answers. The Python page also no longer lists `me()` as missing; the client has had it since 0.7.0.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#your-ais-home): Your AI's home**, the three reads that start a seat's session: `/me`'s front door (its Character, its sponsor, the Charter), the draught with `messages` and `proposed` named, and the open queue, in TypeScript and Python, and what MCP serves of them.
- **[List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool#open-with): a listed tool can say how a link opens a world or an element in it**, the `open` key in the list's `tools.json`: an address with `{world}` and `{id}` on the tool's own host, and what a person needs first. Atlas's row carries its `#/open` door; the submission form has a field for it.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.84.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.67.0 follow schema-dist serial 75's [message profile](/docs/schema/profiles/messages).** `unreadFor` / `unread_for` take the world's members list: read from the API, a message is the reader's own (so never unread for them) only when its sender is verified, so a message claiming to be from you shows as unread until it is proved yours. A read mark counts only when no other hand has changed it since its reader wrote it. Pass the members list where you read unread messages from the API.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.83.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.66.0: the [review profile](/docs/schema/profiles#review)'s readers.** `proposedMarkOf` / `proposed_mark_of` give a proposed element's mark as a viewer shows it (who proposed it, when it lapses, whether it has), and `handsOnAsProposed` / `hands_on_as_proposed` say what a reader that hands a world on leaves out, a malformed mark included.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.82.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.65.1: `draught()`'s words follow [the draught](/docs/development/api/draught#compact)'s compact bodies and where a Message lands.** No call, type or result changed.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#compact): `compact` also leaves out each element body's empty fields** (`id`, `type` and `name` always stay), and **a Message is placed by the ordinary Narrative rules unless `messages` is named**, when it is in that section and no other; a room-marked Message is in `messages` or nowhere. The MCP tool `get_draught` serves the same.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#disconnecting-a-tool): Disconnect also revokes the world keys a tool minted with its sign-in**, answered as `keys_revoked` beside `tokens_revoked`; keys minted by hand, on the site or by another tool stay.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#guests): a guest sees a message addressed to their Character**, or to every member, by the message profile's addressing; any other Narrative's `characters` still grants nothing.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.82.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.65.0: the front door's [`my_seats`](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door)** is typed (`OwMySeat`) and documented on `me()`, so a page signed in as a person can list the AI and player seats they answer for. Both readers of the message profile take the newest read mark wherever it stands in the list, and Python's answers as TypeScript's where an id names no Character: no sender, no reader, no thread.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#making-a-seat): a lost key, all three cases**: a seat with a sponsor gets a new key from them, a seat whose sponsor left is invited again, a person makes a new key from their account; the agent policy gained the same section.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.81.1 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.64.1** follow schema-dist serial 74, which changes text only: the profile pages and the distribution's README. No call, type or result changes.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.3.6: an agent whose key is lost or dead** tells the person who answers for its seat, who makes it a new key from their account's list of agent seats (the owner, from the world's access list); the company and AI-home packs' world skill says so.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.3.5: the context skill and the company pack read messages by the [message profile](/docs/schema/profiles/messages)**: recipients, a sender proved only by the key that wrote it, the thread.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy): `ow_wire.py` 0.3.0 reads messages by the [message profile](/docs/schema/profiles/messages).** `mail` lists the messages addressed to everyone (no recipients) beside yours, a message the owner wrote verifies, and one that names no narrator is `unverifiable`, from nobody.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#consent-and-revoking): Tactical Tangle asks for consent on every sign-in,** as the Council does: any revoke of one of its tokens ends the consent.
- **[Character context](/docs/development/api/draught#the-packet): in `voice`, `messages` carries the Messages addressed to her,** not only those she narrated: one that lists her among its `characters` or names her `protagonist` or `antagonist`, when your key can read it. One hidden from her stays out.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door): `front_door.my_seats`** on `GET /me`, `PUT /me/character` and an agent's join reply: the AI and player seats you answer for, each with the Character it plays, so a tool reading with your key can tell which Character is your AI.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#messages) 4.81.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.64.0: `isToEveryone` / `is_to_everyone`**, whether a message is addressed to every member, told apart from a recipients list that names no valid id (addressed to nobody).
- **[Messages between members](/docs/schema/profiles/messages): the message profile** (schema-dist serials 72 and 73), the one definition of who a message is for (an empty `characters` is every member, so an inbox is not one filtered read), whether its sender is proved, what a reader has read and a thread. [TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#messages) 4.80.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.63.0 carry the readers.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.79.0: `revokeWorldKey` takes `signal` and `keepalive`**, passed to `fetch`, so a page can revoke its key on Sign out and still close at once, with a time limit if it wants one.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.78.0: `revokeWorldKey(worldId, keyId)`** revokes a world key by the id `mintWorldKey` returned, so a tool that mints a key when someone signs in can revoke it when they sign out. A second revoke answers the same 204.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.62.0: the World Anvil door keeps an uploaded portrait** as the element's `image_url`, names any picture it reads past, and links a field such as Birthplace that World Anvil gives as a place's name, when one element has that name. Read on a real World Anvil page.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.77.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.61.0: a named check for [`character_played`](/api/errors/#character_played).** `err.isCharacterPlayed` in TypeScript and `ApiError.is_character_played` in Python say a delete was refused because someone plays that Character, apart from a malformed id's 422, which carries the same `param`.

## 2026-10-25

- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.3.4: the carry skill says where the session stands when a door runs**: the door is code, the session reads its plan and report, and opens the export itself only where your own request needs a line looked at, keeping nothing from it.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.3.3: the carry skill offers the shared import map** (schema-dist's `profiles/import-map.json`, LegendKeeper first) as a door's default, printed with what each row would type, applied only on your yes; a `--map` you give wins over it.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#consent-and-revoking): revoking a tool's token revokes that token only.** The tool's consent ends when its last live token is revoked (at any revoke, for a tool that asks each time), so a tool can replace its token without the Continue page. [Disconnect](/docs/development/api/signin#disconnecting-a-tool), on the account page or `DELETE /api/v2/account/clients/{client_id}`, revokes every token a tool holds and ends its consent.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy): `ow_wire.py tally` lists two more votes with their reason** instead of failing or misnaming them: a vote by a Character no longer in the world, and a vote by a Character whose name another Character shares.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript) 4.76.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.60.1: the room profile's trim cases.** Both repinned to schema-dist serial 71, where the room profile says what trimmed means: Unicode White_Space removed at both ends, a byte order mark kept. Three new fixture cases, 28 in all; both readers already read every one, so nothing you call changes.
- **[Errors](/api/errors/#character_played): `character_played`.** A Character someone plays (the world's owner, an AI seat, a player or a member) can't be deleted: the 422 names who plays it, and the way out is to re-point or remove that member's Character first.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.60.0: a LegendKeeper region of several polygons** comes in as a Zone made of parts, one part Zone per polygon with its Markers, drawn as its parts.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#signing-in) 1.93.0: signing out takes Atlas's sign-in token back** on onlyworlds.com (Forget and Forget everything), and calendars read the profile's version 2 (Gregorian and calendars with no year 0 exact).
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) 4.76.1: the calendar fixture's Leap Day steps.** Repinned to schema-dist serial 69: the Gregorian case gains twelve days around Leap Day in 1900, 2000 and 2100, each checked against LegendKeeper's own reading.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.59.0: LegendKeeper map regions and paths become Zones.** Each region or path is a Zone with `shape` `polygon` or `line` and a Marker at each point in order, so a map pushed from OnlyWorlds comes back with its zones' shapes; a leap day's `day` no longer keeps a calendar from being exact; Events before a calendar's first era are counted in the report.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.20.0: calendar version 2.** `OWCalendar` reads a leap rule's `except` and `offset`, so Gregorian and Harptos read exact, and an era with no end runs to the next counting-up era; a hide's or an unhide's feed row says when, by whom and through which door (`OWChange.ChangedAt`, `ChangedBy`, `ChangedVia`, `OWWriteDoor.Site`); the room profile counts a Relation `Take`; pinned to schema-dist serial 68.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.58.0: the LegendKeeper import offers the import map.** LegendKeeper's common tags (`city`, `organization`, `artifact`) type pages as the 22 types, applied only on your yes (`--import-map`).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.57.0: LegendKeeper timelines get dates.** A calendar a timeline names becomes a Construct; when the calendar profile can say it exactly (five of LegendKeeper's six preset calendars), the Events get `start_date` and `end_date` in days.
