- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.13.0: links in custom properties.** A property holding element ids downloads as links the graph view draws, and saves back as the ids; links to ordinary notes still save as text.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript-readers#world-calendars) 4.76.0: calendar version 2.** A leap rule with exceptions and an offset (Gregorian: every 4, not every 100, yet every 400, counted from year 1), from the [calendar profile](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/schema-dist/blob/main/profiles/calendar.md)'s serial 68; an era with no end runs to the next up era, whatever the list order.
- **[Public reading](/docs/development/api/public#how-big-onlyworlds-is): `GET /api/v2/public/counts`**, with no key: how many worlds and elements OnlyWorlds holds, the standard's 22 kinds, and the worlds whose owners made them public, each with its public element count.
- **[Agents](/docs/development/agents#ow_wirepy): `ow_wire.py tally`** counts the votes on a Law: a Vote Relation counts only when its maker plays the voter's Character, the newest per voter.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.56.0: a LegendKeeper timeline is a Narrative.** Supertype `Timeline`, holding the Events read on it, each lane a child Narrative holding its own.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.55.0: LegendKeeper boards.** A board becomes a Map with supertype `Board`: page cards Pins, frames Zones with corner Markers, arrows between cards or frames Relations, notes and texts pinned Narratives; the board's records kept whole under `records/legendkeeper/boards/`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.54.0: a world pushed from OnlyWorlds into LegendKeeper comes back as itself.** The LegendKeeper door takes each element's id from its `onlyworlds-id` row, its supertype and subtype from `ow-` tags, and extension keys under their own names, so a round trip gives the same elements.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.53.0: LegendKeeper maps through the API.** `python -m onlyworlds.legendkeeper --project ID` now makes each map a Map and its pins Pins, as the export file does, from the map's bounds and its pins; on LegendKeeper's tutorial map both roads put the same pins on the same pixels. Boards are still named and not imported.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#features) 3.12.1: a dependency shows its real name** in the Depends on list where a file name cannot hold it (`@onlyworlds/sdk`), through an alias; a name that fits is linked as before.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp): the opening instructions carry a parlor room's two rules**, `x_room_of` with its pair on a write and `include_room` on a read, and `create_element`'s description the first.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.3.2: an AI that sits in a parlor by API or MCP** writes the room's mark with its pair and reads with `include_room`, on the principles page every skill loads.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#registered-tools): the parlor is a registered tool**, beside Atlas, Lantern and the Council.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin): the Continue page says when it asks again**: once, and again whenever the tool's last token was revoked, so a tool that revokes after each sign-in is asked each time.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#features) 3.12.0: dependencies in the graph.** A world whose elements carry `x_spine_depends_on` downloads with a Depends on section of links on each such note, never sent; a line you write under it is kept and named at save.
- **[Conventions](/docs/schema/conventions): the room profile's ninth pair, Relation `Take`**, one member's take on a work item, with `x_room_item` naming the item (schema-dist serial 66); the [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.75.0 and the [Python package](/docs/development/python) 0.52.0 carry it in `ROOM_PAIRS`.
- **[Hidden elements](/docs/development/api/hidden#the-feed): a hide reaches the readers who still see the element as a row on `/changes`**: the owner's keys, co-builders and the member who made it get an `upsert` at the hide's place, its body with `hidden: true`, carrying `changed_at`, `changed_by` and `changed_via`; an unhide takes a place too and gives the same row without `hidden`. The [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.74.0 names the three fields.
- **[Council](/docs/tools/council#features): Sign in with OnlyWorlds**, with the account signed in on onlyworlds.com; pick one of your worlds and the Council makes its key. A world's key and PIN still work.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#features) 1.92.0: on a phone, a map's drawing tools are finger-sized**, and inside a submap the way back up keeps its row and its whole name.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin#trading-the-code): the exchange answer carries `token_id`**, so a tool revokes its own token by id with that token as the bearer; the [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.73.0 returns it as `tokenId`.
- **[Errors](/api/errors/#rate_limited): wrong PINs count per account as well as per key**, so ten across all of an account's keys lock them for fifteen minutes; a new key starts no fresh count.
- **[Lantern](/docs/tools/lantern): Sign in with OnlyWorlds**, first on the sign-in screen, with the account signed in on onlyworlds.com in this browser; Settings and an invitation's Join button name the account. Email and password still work below it.
- **[Conventions](/docs/schema/conventions): the checks profile's fixture tests the known-of stub rule** with two cases (schema-dist serial 64); the [Python package](/docs/development/python) 0.51.1 replays them.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.3.0: the folder check is `python -m onlyworlds.lint` after `pip install onlyworlds`**, in carry, import and setup; a fresh copy of schema-dist is the way without pip, and a checker found on the disk is still never run ([World folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools)).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.51.0: `python -m onlyworlds.lint` checks a world folder's shape** with schema-dist's own folder checker, carried in the package, so it needs no YAML library and no copy of the repository; [World folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools) names it beside the dist's command. The package and the [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.72.4 are on schema-dist serial 63, whose checker can be loaded by a program with no side effect.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#joining-from-a-link) 1.91.0: a world from a link whose download did not finish stays in your list**, not downloaded yet; selecting it downloads it with nothing more spent from the link.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#one-file) 1.90.0: a world file of a world already in Atlas opens beside it as a copy to read**, named with the file's day when it says one and never online; a `world.json` that says `writable: false` opens view only ([World folders](/docs/schema/folders#snapshots) names it beside the Unity SDK).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.50.0: the spine reader reads `x_spine_retires`**, the retired row's id as given, and replays the spine profile's 44 cases at schema-dist serial 61; the doctor names a malformed one on its Event. **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.72.3** is on serial 61 too, with no type changed. Since serial 61 the distribution's `schema.json` carries the advisory number ranges of the 15 integer fields that have them, for display only: the API enforces none.
- **[World folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools): the folder checker names the format version it knows** and warns once on a `world.json` stamped newer, never counting it as a problem (schema-dist serial 61).
- **[Conventions](/docs/schema/conventions): the room profile's eight type-and-supertype pairs are listed**, not only counted.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#import-and-export) 1.89.0: an element file Atlas cannot read is named when the world opens**: the element list says how many cannot be read, the files stay as they are, and fixing one brings its element back. Home's What do you have? card names what a world still lacks.
- **[Lantern](/docs/tools/lantern): the world file picker offers `.owworld`**, and a damaged world file is refused in plain words.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#one-file) 1.88.3: a world file named `.owworld` opens**, from Data and from Open a world file, as other tools write it; under any other name but `.json`, a world file opens by what it holds. Atlas still saves `.zip`.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.2.0: the folder check says where its checker comes from**: a fresh copy of schema-dist in a scratch directory, never one found on the disk; the skills keep their names. [World folders](/docs/schema/folders#rules-for-tools) says the same.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 5.1.0: every skill ends on what its answer owes you**, one list drawn from its steps; the eleven skills keep their names.

## 2026-10-24

- **[Unity](/docs/development/unity#ship-a-world-with-your-game) 0.19.1: the `.owworld` reader agrees with every other reader**: a deflate stream that does not end at its declared size is refused, names fold with Unicode 15.1's NFC from Unicode's own files, and all 96 cases of the conformance set read at serial 60.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#import-and-export) 1.88.2: a world file with a damaged entry is refused, never half read**; and a co-builder's change to a shared world's details goes whole, as Atlas has always sent it, with the now-alone fallback only for a contributor or a guest.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 5.0: eleven skills where there were fourteen.** `import`, `model`, `read`, `update` and `develop` take over the 4.8 skills ([the table](/docs/development/toolkit#coming-from-48)), `draw` and `carry` are new, and the old skill names no longer load. Other AIs take the skills with `npx skills add OnlyWorlds/toolkit`, or read nine knowledge files in the page's order.
- **[Games](/docs/development/games#engines): Godot, Unreal and other engines can read a world folder or `.owworld` file from disk**, with no key and no network, beside the API; the toolkit's `develop` skill carries the game paths since 5.0.1.
- **[Atlas](/docs/tools/atlas#hidden-elements): sign-in and hidden elements, as of 1.88.0.** Signed out, Make online starts with Sign in with OnlyWorlds (1.87.0); an element a world hides shows with the word Hidden in any world (1.86.0), and the owner's or a co-builder's mark added after the element went online hides it there too (1.88.0). The page no longer says onlyworlds.com takes a shared world's details only from its owner: a co-builder may change all but the owner's Character. [Proposals](/docs/development/api/proposals#accept-decline-withdraw) writes decline and withdraw in full.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python-import#kanka) 0.49.0: the Kanka import on a second real export.** A tag's Narrative links the elements that carry it, never a hidden one from a visible tag; Kanka's `gallery/` is read as its image store, so an element names its picture as `x_kanka_image` and the report lists the pictures not imported (a picture not visible to all keeps its name off a visible element).
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees): a world with a public page has a graph beside it**, `onlyworlds.com/w/<address>/graph`, linked as Graph on the page's bar: every public element and the links between them, Maps, Pins, Markers and Zones left out. It is the one public page with a script.
- **[TypeScript](/docs/development/typescript#reference) SDK 4.72.2: `hidden`'s type description says what a create does with it.** In the package's types (what an editor shows), a create carrying `hidden: true` creates the element hidden, from the owner's keys or a co-builder's, as [Hidden elements](/docs/development/api/hidden#creating-an-element-hidden) says; every other write ignores it. The SDK already sent the mark; only its description was wrong. The Reference's method descriptions now name no internal tracker numbers.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.48.0: the doctor follows the spine profile's eight new readings.** A Retirement Event names the row it retires in `x_spine_retires` (the profile's thirteenth key) and links it where the type has a field, so a Law's retirement is checked too; a row still at `gone` is a finding; on a projection only the projection's checks run, and its mark's `source` (a world's UUID) and `at` (an ISO time) are checked. Schema pin on schema-dist serial 60.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python-import#notion) 0.47.0: the Notion import reads the layouts real exports use.** A database's rows in a folder named by its title alone, or a flat export with every page and table in one folder, now pair each row with its own page, so no row comes in twice and a relation to a row (a Character's `location`) becomes a link. Read on two real free-account exports, now the import's test fixtures.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.46.0: the doctor follows the spine profile's newest audit, and world files refuse a deflate stream that ends early.** Flag 13 checks each row against one Seat through the Title's field for the row's type, a Retirement Event's typed link for a `legacy` or `provenance` row, and names unique within a type once folded, and reads a projection by the World's `x_spine_projection`. `read_world_file` refuses an entry whose deflate stream ends before its declared compressed size (`not_openable`). `doctor.ListReader` names the three reads `pack_api` needs. Schema pin on schema-dist serial 59.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.72.1: world files refuse a deflate stream that ends early.** `readWorldFile` answers `not_openable` for an entry whose deflate stream ends before its declared compressed size, as the folder format's §4.7 rules; a file whose streams end where their entries say reads as before. Schema pin on schema-dist serial 59, same standard 00.33.00.
- **[Contact](/docs/contact#questions-and-bugs): onlyworlds.com publishes its security route**. `www.onlyworlds.com/.well-known/security.txt` names the standard repository's private reporting as the contact and this page as its policy, so a vulnerability in onlyworlds.com or its API has a published private route beside the docs' own.
- **[Contact](/docs/contact#questions-and-bugs): where a bug, a question and a vulnerability go.** A bug or a question goes to the issues of the package or tool's repository; a vulnerability goes privately through GitHub's private reporting, now on for every public OnlyWorlds repository in use, each with a `SECURITY.md` saying what counts. The docs publish the route at `/.well-known/security.txt` (RFC 9116).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#write) 0.45.3: `push` leaves Atlas's word on the hidden mark at home.** Atlas 1.86.2 keeps `atlas_hidden_shown_online` in an element file it pushed, a local-only field it never sends; the Python push now leaves it out too, as it does Atlas's other local-only fields.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.45.2: on a spine world the doctor counts a dependency as a link.** An element whose only edge is `x_spine_depends_on`, or that another depends on, is no longer reported as having no links in or out. Other worlds are unchanged.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-readers#find-elements-in-a-world) 4.72.0: the ask context leaves out what a world holds back.** `buildAskContext` no longer sends proposed, hidden or room elements to the AI proxy unless asked (`includeProposed`, `includeHidden`, `includeRoom`), lists them in `leftOut`, labels an included proposed element `[proposed, not yet reviewed]`, and drops a link's name when the link points at an element left out. A caller that passed such elements gets fewer sent. `OwWorldLink` gains `minted_by`, the co-builder who made a link.
- **A co-builder makes invite links and changes the world's settings.** With its own world key, a co-builder makes, lists and revokes the world's guest and contributor links at `/api/v2/links`, with the owner's body and answers ([A co-builder's links](/docs/development/api/members#a-co-builders-links)); a co-builder link stays the owner's. `PATCH /api/v2/world` takes every writable field from a co-builder but `owner_character` ([Writes](/docs/development/api/writes)). A key that isn't a co-builder's on `/links` gets the new `403` [`not_co_builder`](/api/errors/#not_co_builder).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.45.1: world-file names fold at Unicode 15.1.** `read_world_file` now checks names with an NFC built from Unicode 15.1's own files, as the folder format rules since serial 57, and no longer with `unicodedata`, which follows the interpreter (Unicode 16.0 on Python 3.14). The NFC is exported as `onlyworlds.worldfile.nfc`; Unicode's own normalization test is its test. The same fix as TypeScript SDK 4.71.0.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.71.0: world-file names fold at Unicode 15.1.** The `.owworld` name check now uses an NFC built from Unicode 15.1's own files, as the [folder format](/docs/schema/folders) rules since schema-dist serial 57, and no longer the runtime's: Node 22's NFC is Unicode 16.0, which joins 20 code points that 15.1 leaves unassigned. It's exported as `nfc`. The schema pin moves to serial 57, and its 93 archive cases are the package's test. No type changed.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.45.0: the doctor checks a spine.** On a world whose elements carry `x_spine_` keys, the doctor reads each element under the spine profile and names an off-list kind, a value the reader left out and a probe off the probe form. It then audits what one element cannot say: each row kept by exactly one `Seat` Title, and every `x_spine_depends_on` id in the world. The `.owworld` reader agrees with all 93 cases of schema-dist serial 57, eight of them new cases on Unicode normalisation.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.44.1: the spine reader on serial 56.** Each entry in `SpineRead.dropped` is now `{"key", "value"}`, with the value exactly as the element held it, where 0.44.0 wrote a string. The profile compares these as JSON values, so no language's JSON formatting matters. A non-string `x_spine_truth` or `x_spine_probe` is now named there too. The fixture's 40 cases are the package's test. This changes code that read the strings.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.44.0: the spine profile's first reader.** `read_spine_element(type, element)` reads one element of a world that maps a software ecosystem, under the [spine profile](/docs/schema/profiles#spine) (schema-dist serial 55): its kind from the closed list of supertypes, each `x_spine_*` key as read or not stated, and every value it left out, named. All 34 of the profile's fixture cases are the package's test. Schema pin on serial 55; no other API change.

## 2026-10-23

- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#the-keyless-reads): an element still proposed is not public**. An element carrying `x_review_proposed` with any value but `null` (a new element an AI seat proposed, or a doubt, that no keeper has settled) is left out of the public page, the keyless reads and `?as=public` as a hidden element is: not listed, counted, linked or found by search, its own address the plain `404`, nothing said about it. A pinned element that turns proposed drops off the page and the card tells the owner. Once settled it is served on the next read; owner and member reads are unchanged. Viewed as the public, Recent changes names no writer.
- **[Agent writes](/docs/development/api/writes#agent-writes): Recent changes says when a new element is still proposed**. On the world page's recent list and on Recent changes, a new element an AI seat proposed reads "proposed" until a keeper settles it, "lapsed" once its `expires` day has passed, where it read "added". Each Restore link carries a name of its own for screen readers. The API and the change feed are unchanged.
- **[List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool): Tools others build leaves out the tools the page already shows**. A listed tool that onlyworlds.com/tools already features in its own cards, matched by its URL, is not repeated under Tools others build; Atlas and Lantern, the first two listed, are both featured, so the section says "No tool from outside is listed yet; the first is yours to submit." A row's name and source read as links, and its facts take up to three short lines. `tools.json` is unchanged.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#ship-a-world-with-your-game) v0.19.0: a world file to hand on leaves hidden elements out.** `OWPayload.IsHidden` and `OWElement.IsHidden` read the hidden mark by the one rule npm and Python use (anything but absent, `null` or `false`); `Pack`'s `includeHidden: false` makes a share, the left-out paths in `hiddenLeftOut`; folders at format 0.3.18. The page also drops a stale line: `Pack` has placed the `mimetype` marker since v0.12.1.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.43.1: schema pin on schema-dist serial 54, and house rules never judge a stub.** The [checks profile](/docs/schema/profiles#checks) gains its list readings and three reader rules; `apply_checks` itself now leaves a known-of stub out (before, only the doctor did, so a direct caller got a guest's stubs reported short). The folder lint the door tests run knows the hidden mark, so they now fail on any lint line. No API change.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.43.0: hidden elements on create, in the pack, the OKF bundle and the world file; the compact draught.** `create`, `upsert` and `bulk` now send `hidden` as the element carries it, so a folder's hidden element is [created hidden](/docs/development/api/hidden#creating-an-element-hidden) (before, they stripped it and it was created visible); a key that may not hide gets `ApiError.is_hidden_not_allowed` and nothing is created. `push` creates nothing and never sends the mark. The [Markdown pack](/docs/development/python#the-markdown-pack) and the [OKF bundle](/docs/development/python#export-to-okf) leave a hidden element out unless `--hidden` (`hidden=True`), count it, and leave out every link to it; `write_world_file` and `world_file_bytes` keep hidden elements unless `include_hidden=False`, the left-out paths into a `hidden_left_out=` list. [`draught(..., compact=)`](/docs/development/api/draught#compact) as the npm SDK's 4.69.0.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.70.0: a world file to hand on leaves hidden elements out.** `writeWorldFile(files, top, { includeHidden: false })` leaves out, whole, every element file carrying `hidden` (any value but absent, `null` or `false`), and `report: r => …` receives `{ hiddenLeftOut }`; by default a world file is a copy and keeps them.
- **[List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool#what-we-read): a listed row links its source**. On onlyworlds.com/tools each row of Tools others build shows its source after the maker: a link when `source` is an https address, or "source: not public." with the row's note. The Access page's keys table names a seat once per key, site only.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public#what-a-visitor-sees): the public page's follow-ups, and the pin picked by name**. Under a live page, an element address that is hidden, deleted or mistyped now answers "This page isn't available." with a link back to the world's page, still a `404`, where it showed the page of a world with no public page. The row of section links and an element page's path show only when the page has two or more sections of their own; a pinned element shows once, in its card; the owner picks the pin from a list by name. A keeper's settle of a seat's new element now names both hands on the site's recent lists, as an accepted proposal does; nothing changes on the wire.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com#features): Restore to a day names each file**. Each row and its button say which file it is, "Daily file, 23 Oct 2026" or "Kept before a restore, 23 Oct 2026 07:24 UTC", and the confirm page's heading and button name the same file.
- **[Hidden elements](/docs/development/api/hidden#creating-an-element-hidden): a create can hide its element from the start**. `hidden: true` on `POST /api/v2/{type}`, on a `PUT` that creates, or on a `/bulk` item that creates writes the mark in the create's own transaction, so a reader who cannot see hidden elements never meets it; MCP's `create_element` takes `hidden` (false). Only the owner's keys and a co-builder's may send it: any other key is `403` [`hidden_not_allowed`](/api/errors/#hidden_not_allowed) and nothing is created. On an update `hidden` is still ignored; an existing element moves only through `PUT` and `DELETE /{type}/{id}/hidden`.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.69.0: the compact draught.** `draught(id, { compact: true })` asks for the packet without its empty sections, typed `OwDraughtCompact` (`cut` optional); without `compact` the answer is the fixed `OwDraught`. A `compact` that is not a boolean is a `TypeError` before anything is sent.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#compact) takes `compact`**. `?compact=true` leaves out every empty section and an empty `cut`; the frame (`world`, `calendar`, `as_of`, `now`, `mode`, `policy`) stays, and so does each null that is an answer (`character`, `style`, `recent_cursor`). The route's default is the fixed shape; [MCP](/docs/development/mcp#read-a-key-or-a-sign-in)'s `get_draught` takes `compact` as its own argument, true unless the caller says false.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.42.0: LegendKeeper maps, and no tool name in a supertype.** The LegendKeeper door brings a map in as a Map and its pins as Pins, placed as LegendKeeper shows them. An untyped page from Kanka, Notion or LegendKeeper is now a Narrative with supertype `Article`, where it named the tool before; ids are unchanged. Folders stamp folder format 0.3.18.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.41.0: the Campfire door.** `python -m onlyworlds.campfire --file EXPORT.html OUT-DIR` reads Campfire's Export Elements html into a world folder. It's a documents door: ids come from type and name (`--project NAME` keys them apart), types are read from each element's panels, and every panel value is kept as `x_campfire_*`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.40.0: the World Anvil door on its owner's rulings.** Four more templates (Ethnicity, Myth / Legend, Vehicle, Profession); an unknown template is a Narrative with supertype `Article`, where 0.39.0 wrote `worldanvil`; an article's dates kept as `x_worldanvil_created`, `_updated` and `_published`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.39.0: the World Anvil door.** `python -m onlyworlds.worldanvil --file EXPORT.html OUT-DIR` reads World Anvil's free Export World page (one print-style HTML page, saved from the browser) into a world folder: each article an element of its template's type, links between articles kept by id in `x_worldanvil_mentions`, the category and unmapped template fields as `x_worldanvil_*`, ids stable across exports. A non-public article comes in hidden; untried on a real file, since a free account can't make one.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.18.0: elements an AI wrote as proposed, and a world made on an account.** The review queue's new-element rows, `ReviewNewElementAsync`, `IsNotProposed`, and the mark's two readers `IsProposed` and `IsProposedOrInDoubt` (schema-dist dist.52); `CreateAccountWorldAsync`; `counts` left out of a world patch.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.38.0: LegendKeeper's own export, and hidden content kept hidden.** `python -m onlyworlds.legendkeeper --file EXPORT.lk OUT-DIR` reads a `.lk` or `.json` export with no key. On both the file and the API road, a hidden resource, page or `secret` block now comes in marked `hidden: true` instead of left out, and the two include switches are gone (a behaviour change). Folder format 0.3.17.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught#the-packet): `room` and `proposed` rows carry `circles`** in the API document, as `knowledge` rows do, when the element came through one of her circles.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.68.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.37.0: a keyless read's `counts` is not sent back.** `patchWorld` and `patch_world` strip `counts`, which only a [keyless](/docs/development/api/public#the-keyless-reads) `GET /world` carries and `PATCH` refuses. TypeScript's `reviewNewElement` also refuses a reason with an accept before sending, and checks that the answer carries `element` or `deleted`, as Python's always has.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.37.0: the Kanka door, and `push` withholds a hidden element.** `python -m onlyworlds.kanka EXPORT.zip OUT-DIR` reads Kanka's free JSON export into a world folder, built on a real export from day one: exact duplicates folded and named, nothing dropped, what Kanka hides carried as `hidden: true`. `push` sends nothing for an element hidden in the folder and not on the server, and names it.
- **[Public worlds](/docs/development/api/public): a world's owner can open it to everyone and give it a public page** (live since 09:4x). Under **Who can see this world** on the world page's Settings, **Anyone can read it, with no account** opens the world to keyless reads, and **Give it a page at onlyworlds.com/w/…** gives it a server-rendered page at an address the owner picks (3 to 40 lowercase letters, numbers and hyphens, unique, first come first served; `/w/<world id>` always works), with a palette, a hero line, sections and a pin, and a page per element. A visitor reads what the public reads: hidden elements, the room, files and the roster never show. For developers, a request with no key and `?world=<id>` reads an open world on the element, search, metadata and world routes (the world GET takes the page's address too, and carries `counts`); every closed door is one `404`, every answer `no-store`, 60 a minute per address. `public_read`, `slug` and `public_page` stay read-only on the API. The lines that said no switch existed are true now ([Members](/docs/development/api/members#sharing-a-world), [Writes](/docs/development/api/writes#the-world), [Worlds](/docs/schema/worlds), [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#the-world), [onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com#features)).
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.67.0: the draught's `room` and `proposed` components, and its rows typed as served.** `OwDraughtComponent` gains `room` (missing from the type until now) and `proposed`. `OwDraughtItem` now nests the element under `element`, as keel has always served it; code that read the old spread form (`row.name`) stops compiling, where it read `undefined` before.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.11.0: `records/` travels, and creating a world needs a sign-in.** A folder import keeps `records/` byte for byte and the export writes it back, private producers left out unless asked, and an unreadable `RECORDS.json` asks before anything goes out; folder format 0.3.16. Create world goes through your account (the SDK's `createAccountWorld`), so a signed-out person is asked to sign in first.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught) and the [MCP server](/docs/development/mcp#read-a-key-or-a-sign-in) leave unreviewed AI elements out**. An element an AI seat proposed and no keeper has settled is not canon: the draught serves it only in a new `proposed` component, off unless named, and `cut.proposed` counts the ones left out; `list_elements` and `search_elements` take `include_proposed` (false) and answer `proposed_left_out`. `get_element` by id still serves an element whole. The draught page also lists the `room` component.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.17.0: the world's own settings.** `PatchWorldAsync` writes a world's settings, its origin (`source`, `producer`) and its tools' extension keys, and a folder export declares folder format 0.3.16 with the world's `origin` as served.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#the-markdown-pack) 0.36.0: the pack leaves unreviewed AI elements out.** An element carrying `x_review_proposed` (a seat's new element under the **AI seats propose, a person accepts** switch, not yet settled by a keeper) is not canon: the Markdown pack leaves it out unless `--proposed`, counts it in `00-world.md`'s opening lines, and labels a kept one "proposed, not yet reviewed" wherever it or a link to it is printed.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com#features): a restore puts the World's tool keys back**. Restore to a day now makes the World's `x_`, `atlas_` and `shadow_` keys exactly what that day's file holds, removing one a tool added since, and never touches the world's `origin`. A file whose keys are over the 64 KB cap is refused before anything moves. See [Restores in the feed](/docs/development/api/changes#restores-in-the-feed).
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#a-world-on-the-account) 4.66.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.35.0 follow proposed changes, round 2.** Both read the queue's two kinds of row and settle a seat's new element (`reviewNewElement`, `review_new_element`), and know `not_proposed`. The SDK adds `createAccountWorld` (an account token makes a world, with `keyName`, `source` and `producer`); Python's `patch_world` takes `source=` and `producer=`. The [SDKs page](/docs/development/packages) states the one origin rule for all three clients.
- **onlyworlds.com's Recent changes leaves a hidden room's deletes out** and counts them with the room: a site change, the feed untouched.
- **[Proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals), round 2**. A proposal stores its diff: `fields` only what differs, `omitted` what a `PUT` left empty, and an accept writes exactly `fields`; a proposal of nothing new is a `422`. The queue lists a seat's marked new elements beside proposals (`kind`: `proposal` or `new_element`), the cursor opaque, the `ETag` three parts; a keeper settles a mark with `POST /{type}/{id}/review`, and an unmarked element answers `422 not_proposed`.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.65.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.34.0 follow the World's extension keys and origin.** `patchWorld` and `patch_world` take back a World body again (its `origin`, a `422` in a `PATCH`, is stripped, and the SDK strips `id` too, which never round-tripped); extension keys go as given. `OwWorldMeta` types `origin` and the extension keys. Python's `push` sets a world's `origin` only on a first fill from a folder made elsewhere.
- **[The World](/docs/development/api/reads#the-world) carries extension keys and its origin**. `x_`, `atlas_` and `shadow_` keys on the World, set with [`PATCH /world`](/docs/development/api/writes#the-world) (`null` removes one; 64 KB in all), so a world's [`x_names`](/docs/schema/conventions) now lives on onlyworlds.com; `origin` `{source, producer}`, read-only, where the world first came from, set by sending `source` and `producer` on the world's create or a `PATCH`. A co-builder may send these beside `time_range_current`.
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders#worldjson) v0.3.16: `origin`.** `world.json` may carry `origin`, `{source, producer}`: where a world first came from, as the tool that made it from a folder gave those lines, kept by the server and written by a server export beside the export's own lines. A reader keeps it like any world key; no writer derives one. onlyworlds.com will serve it (not yet live). [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.33.0 and ow-folder-store 0.3.7 carry it as given and stamp 0.3.16.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.10.0: the server's Properties.** A downloaded note carries the seven keys the server keeps on every element (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `change_seq`, `created_by`, `changed_by`, `changed_via`, `changed_client`), read-only and rewritten from the server's answer after each save, so the folder export shows the server's values; a Zone's `shape` is a Shape section; the plugin reads SDK 4.64.0's schema (Collective `equipment` links Objects); Escape on the PIN prompt cancels the save; the export claims folder format 0.3.15.
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders#folders-and-onlyworldscom) v0.3.15: private records stay home.** A producer may mark its `records/` directory `"private": true` in `RECORDS.json`; an export, a share or a snapshot leaves that directory out whole unless asked, and keeps `RECORDS.json` as it is, so a reader learns a producer was left out. A writer that cannot read the manifest refuses or the caller asks. schema-dist serial 49 carries the case on both sides (the archive set at 85). The Folders page now shows `records/` in the layout.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.63.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.32.0: the writers follow it.** `writeWorldFile(files, top, { includePrivateRecords })` and `write_world_file(..., include_private_records=)`: private records left out by default, the producer's name matched to its folder after NFC and simple case folding, a manifest that cannot be read refused unless asked; both pinned to schema-dist serial 49 and the 85-case set.
- **[The export file](/docs/development/api/changes#the-export-file) carries the World whole**. The owner's one-file download now holds every key `GET /world` serves in its `world` block (description, time settings, units, the switches), so a world's settings travel with it; schema-dist serial 50's `example/world-export.json` shows it.
- **[onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com#features): Restore to a day** (on in production since its drill this morning). Each night a world changed, onlyworlds.com keeps a file of it for 90 days; the owner restores the world to any of those days from the world page, and a file of the world as it was just before is kept first. The [Changes page](/docs/development/api/changes#restores-in-the-feed) says what a client sees: ordinary upserts and deletes through the `site` door, the head never going back, guests resynced.
- **[onlyworlds.com/tools](https://www.onlyworlds.com/tools): Tools others build**. The site's tools page shows the listed rows of [OnlyWorlds/tools](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/tools) with their listed date, from a copy checked in at build time, beside a Submit a tool link to [List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool). No outside tool is listed yet.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.64.0: every options object refuses an unknown key, now truly every one.** 4.52.0 said so, but eleven took a misspelt key silently: `readWorldFile` (a `maxbytes` read with the 2 GiB default), `parseDate`, `resolveCalendarLens`, `lookupWorld`, `renderElement`, `planPhoto`, `preparePhoto`, `request`, `listAll` (which also refuses a `cursor` now: it walks from the first page and set its own), the deprecated `consume` and `OwSignInError`. Each now throws a `TypeError` naming the key before doing anything, and a test reads the package's exports with the TypeScript compiler and fails on an options parameter no fence covers.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#write) v0.16.0: caught up with the TypeScript SDK through 4.63.0.** The client's name on writes (`X-OW-Client`, default `unity-sdk`; `ChangedClient`, `DeletedClient`), `ifMatch` on delete, patch and links, the conditional head poll (`Unchanged` on a `304`), the PIN only where keel checks it, `OWFrontDoor`, `OWRoom.IsRoomElement`, the proposal calls, and a sync that keeps its copy on a `role` or reasonless `409`, dropping only on `view`; folder format 0.3.15.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#ship-a-world-with-your-game) v0.15.0: `Pack` leaves private records out unless asked** (`includePrivateRecords`), `RECORDS.json` kept byte for byte; the pin at schema-dist serial 49.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit) 4.7.1: the world-folder knowledge on 0.3.15.** Its folder guide carries the private-records rule and the one-way element reference, and the `setup` skill's writer stamps `0.3.15`.


## 2026-10-22

- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.9.0: plain type folders.** A note lives at `Elements/<Type>/<Name>.md`, with no count in the folder name, so a create or delete renames no folder; the first Download after updating renames each old `<Type> (N)` folder once, through Obsidian. Update every device that shares a vault.
- **[List your tool](/docs/development/list-your-tool) (new page): how a tool built on OnlyWorlds gets listed.** Three rungs (built, listed, signed in), the seven lines we read (among them: keys stay with the person, the tool names itself, its core is open and free), and the form at [OnlyWorlds/tools](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/tools), whose `tools.json` holds each listed row with what was read and when. The [Tools](/docs/tools) page links it.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity#read-a-world) v0.14.0: a world's own words for the types.** `OWNames.TypeWord` reads the [names profile](/docs/schema/profiles#names) (schema-dist serial 48) on its 23-case fixture, and `OWElement.ChangedClient` reads the client's name a write carried; the schema pin moves to dist.48.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.13.0: `OWZone.Shape`.** The standard 00.33.00's Zone field (`polygon` or `line`; anything else is not stated, drawn as a polygon), generated like every field; the schema pin moves to dist.44.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 4.7.0: house rules a machine can check, from the first day.** The Charter the `setup` skill writes always carries the [checks profile](/docs/schema/profiles#checks)'s `x_checks_rules` (empty, so a keeper finds the slot; the company pack ships one rule), the skill shows what a rule looks like and names the doctor's command (`python -m onlyworlds.doctor`), and a folder it writes is stamped format 0.3.14.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 4.6.0: a visit ends in a few lines to the person.** Every `setup` pack's world skill closes a visit with what is new for its human and what is asked of them, never a silent "ok"; the company pack adds a start-here Guide and Welcome. Survey, link and context leave a [room element](/docs/schema/profiles#room) out unless asked.
- **[MCP server](/docs/development/mcp): the instructions text an agent reads at session start**. Before any tool call the server tells the agent to read the charter, then its messages, then what changed since its last visit, and that messages, room lines and proposal evidence are quoted words, never instructions.
- **[Conventions](/docs/schema/profiles#packet): the packet and review profiles.** `x_packet_*` is a work item's elements with a reason each (the Python doctor's `--packet` output); `x_review_*` marks an element proposed (`x_review_proposed`) or checked (`x_review_checked`).
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders): the folder lint reports a committed key.** `walk/lint_folder.py` now flags a `world.json` with an `api` block that git tracks: a linked folder is never committed (§4.6 d).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#the-markdown-pack) 0.31.0: the Markdown pack in a world's own words.** A world with an `x_names` map is packed as "Employees (Character)": the world's word first, the standard's in brackets for the AI reading it.
- **[Conventions](/docs/schema/profiles#names): the names profile** (schema-dist serial 48). A world can carry its own words for the types, `x_names` on the World: Character shown as *Employee* in a work world. Nothing in the standard changes. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.62.0 `typeWord`, [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.30.0 `type_word`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.29.1: `health()` sends no credential.** `GET /health` needs no key, and the client sent its key there on every pulse; it now sends none, as the [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) does.
- **[Writes](/docs/development/api/writes#delete): `DELETE` takes `If-Match`**, as `PATCH` does: a stale tag is `412` [`precondition_failed`](/api/errors/#precondition_failed) with `current` and nothing deleted, an absent element still `204`; a delete is never a proposal. [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.61.0 `delete(type, id, { ifMatch })`, [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.29.0 `delete(..., if_match=)`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.28.0: the doctor applies the world's Charter only.** House rules come from the one Charter keel names in [`front_door`](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door) (the newest Law with supertype Charter made by the owner or a co-builder), on a folder the owner's newest; every other Law with supertype Charter is reported with its rules not applied, so an agent seat cannot write the rules it is judged by. [Conventions](/docs/schema/profiles#checks) says which Law is the Charter.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.60.0: `OwFrontDoor`.** `front_door` is typed on `me()`'s answer and on an agent's `redeemJoin()` answer; the client already passed it through. Python's `me()` returns it in its dict as before.
- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#the-front-door) and [Agents](/docs/development/agents#the-join-link): the front door**. `GET /me`, `PUT /me/character` and an agent's join reply carry `front_door`: who answers for a seat (`sponsor`, `sponsor_character`), your own Character (`my_character`), the world's Charter (`charter`: the newest Law with supertype Charter made by the owner or a co-builder) and up to five Objects it names (`charter_skill`). Every member reads the Charter and those Objects, guests and player seats included, unless the owner hid them. An agent reads the Charter first; the description comes after.

- **[Members](/docs/development/api/members#invite-links), [Agents](/docs/development/agents#the-join-link) and [onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com): the world page's new Access page.** Every way in is now one card, Let someone in: a person (by email or by a link), players at a table (one or a table) or an AI agent (a link first; a seat with `.env` lines under More options), with uses, expiry and the preview under More options; unused invitations and links sit in one list, Waiting to be used. The Character an agent seat or a player plays is set with Change on its row under Members. The join page says first who a link is for, and a link pasted without its code says so. The pages now name these controls; nothing on the API moved.

- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#write) 4.59.0 and [Python](/docs/development/python#write) 0.27.0: the PIN is sent only where the server checks it.** Both clients put `API-Pin` on every request once a PIN was given; the server verifies a PIN wherever one arrives, a memory-hard hash each time, and a run of reads carrying one can crowd a small instance. Now the PIN goes on writes, on `/me`, and on every call only with a 10-digit legacy key ([Keys and PINs](/docs/getting-started/keys#the-pin)); a prefixed key's reads go without it. No API change; a wrong PIN now shows at the first write, or on `me()`.
- **[MCP server](/docs/development/mcp#read-a-key-or-a-sign-in): `include_room` on `list_elements` and `search_elements`**. Off by default: a [room element](/docs/schema/profiles#room) (`x_room_of`) is left out of both tools' answers unless `include_room` is true, and `get_draught`'s `room` component is off unless named. The REST lists and the change feed still carry the room; onlyworlds.com's own lore views now leave it out the same way, with a Show the room switch.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#the-markdown-pack) 0.26.1: the pack says which kind of key made it.** One line at the top of `00-world.md` (the owner's key, a member's with its role, a Character's view, the public view), from `/me` or the folder's `exported_as`; a label, since a pack holds only what its key was served.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#proposed-changes) 0.26.0: the proposed change.** `propose`, `list_proposals`, `world_proposals` and `walk_world_proposals`, `accept_proposal`, `decline_proposal`, `withdraw_proposal`; a write the world's switch turned into a proposal raises `ProposedError`, `push` counts it as proposed and never files it twice; `StaleProposal` with `conflicts` and `current`, four more predicates; `patch_world` drops `agent_writes_proposed`; the doctor's flag 12, the queue's open and lapsed rows.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript-accounts#proposed-changes) 4.58.0: the proposed change.** `propose`, `listProposals`, `worldProposals`, `acceptProposal`, `declineProposal`, `withdrawProposal`; a write the world's switch turned into a proposal throws `OwProposedError` (202) rather than returning a body that is not the element, a bulk slot keeps its 202; `OwStaleProposalError` with `conflicts` and `current`, and four more code predicates; `agent_writes_proposed` read-only on the world and stripped by `patchWorld`.
- **[Proposed changes](/docs/development/api/proposals): a member proposes, a keeper accepts**. `POST /{type}/{id}/proposals` by any member key that reads the element; accept (the keeper's own write, one feed row; `409 stale_proposal` when a proposed field moved), decline, withdraw; the queue at `GET /world/proposals` with an `ETag` and a refunded `304`. With the world's **AI seats propose, a person accepts** switch on (`agent_writes_proposed` on [the world body](/docs/development/api/reads#the-world)), an agent seat's edits to existing elements answer `202` with the proposal, its new elements carry `x_review_proposed`, and it deletes only what it created. Five new codes on [the errors page](/api/errors#stale_proposal).
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.8.1: a save never undoes another writer's change.** Every save, auto-sync, upload and delete sends `If-Match` with the version the note was last downloaded or saved at (kept in `.ow-seq.json` beside `World.md`); a note that is behind is refused with one notice and keeps the edit, Download brings the current copy; a note with no recorded version saves once without the check and says so. The key texts name the world's Access page; an export writes `exported_as` from `/me` and stamps folder format 0.3.14.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#the-markdown-pack) 0.25.0: the Markdown pack.** `python -m onlyworlds.pack FOLDER OUT` (or `--api OUT`) writes a world as a few merged Markdown files sized for an AI notebook with source caps (NotebookLM's 50 sources and 500,000 words a source, Claude Projects' 30 MB a file): the world's fields and an index, then one file per type, each element a section with its fields in schema order and every link as the target's name and type. Room elements and extension fields out unless asked; a file past a cap splits, small files merge under the file cap, nothing cut or dropped; the same world gives the same bytes.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#check-a-world) 0.24.0: the doctor reads a world's house rules.** The [checks profile](/docs/schema/profiles#checks)'s `x_checks_rules` on the Charter, each rule reported with how many elements it scoped and which fall short, a rule the doctor cannot apply named as not checked; the dist's fixture is the package's own test. A Charter without the key is one line, nothing checked.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants) 4.57.2 and [Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.22.1: schema pin on schema-dist serial 47.** The dist gains the [checks profile](/docs/schema/profiles#checks) (`profiles/checks.md`, a Charter's house rules as `x_checks_rules`, with its fixture) and the room profile's one definition of "trimmed and without regard to case". `isRoomElement` and `is_room_element` now trim by Unicode's White_Space set exactly: a byte order mark or a zero width space at either end is not whitespace, so such a value is not a room element.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python-import#legendkeeper) 0.23.0: import from LegendKeeper.** `python -m onlyworlds.legendkeeper --project ID OUT-DIR` reads a project through LegendKeeper's API v3 into a world folder, GET only, your key never stored: a tag names the type (or `--map`), blocks become Markdown, sidebar keys become fields by name or `x_legendkeeper_*`, titled link lists become link fields or Relations, timeline events keep their raw minutes, hidden and secret content stays out unless asked, and the report names everything not placed. Built on recorded answers from one project; the real-project walk is next.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#client-options) 4.57.1: `patchWorld` strips `public_read` and `agent_narrator_rule`** beside `slug` and `public_page`, since keel answers each as an unknown field; a world body read with `getWorld` and sent back round-trips.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#follow-changes) 4.57.0: the head poll is conditional.** `changesHead()` keeps the `ETag` of its last `200` and sends it back as `If-None-Match`; an unmoved head answers `unchanged: true` with the head, cursor and epoch it holds, which keel does not count against the budget; a `409` clears the tag so the next poll reads `epoch_reason`. The world type gains `slug` and `public_page` (`OwWorldPublicPage`), read-only and stripped from `patchWorld`.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#follow-changes) 0.22.0: `head()`, the conditional poll.** `client.head()` keeps the last `ETag` and sends it back, an unmoved head answers `unchanged=True` for free, a `409` clears the tag. Two fixes: `walk_changes` keeps a numeric tail cursor as a string, so an owner's walk no longer resends its last page; `patch_world` strips `slug` and `public_page` as it strips `public_read`, so an exported `world.json` pushes back without a `422`.
- **[The world body](/docs/development/api/reads#the-world) gains `slug` and `public_page`**, the world's public page as its owner set it (the address `onlyworlds.com/w/<slug>`, `on`, and the levers `palette`, `hero`, `sections`, `pin`, `index`), read-only and a `422` in a `PATCH` like `public_read`; every key present while the page is off. The page itself waits behind a switch.
- **[Change feed](/docs/development/api/changes#walking-the-feed): the head poll answers `304` to its own tag, and a `304` is refunded.** `GET /api/v2/changes?head=true` carries `ETag: "<head>:<epoch>"`; send it back as received in `If-None-Match` and an unmoved head is a `304` with no body, not counted against the request budget within 120 a minute per key. A poller that ticks every two seconds now spends nothing while a world is quiet.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#client-options) 4.56.0: name your tool.** `new OwV2Client({ ..., client: 'atlas' })` sends `X-OW-Client` on the seven element writes (`create`, `upsert`, `patch`, `delete`, `editLinks`, `bulk`, `adopt`), never on a read; default `sdk`; a name outside lower case letters, digits and hyphens, 1 to 32 characters, is a `TypeError` at construction. `changed_client` is typed on every element and `deleted_client` on a change feed delete. The constructor now refuses an unknown key by name, as every options object has since 4.52.0. Schema pin on schema-dist serial 46.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.21.0: name your tool.** `Client(..., client="atlas")`, the same rule and the same writes (a `ValueError` for a name outside it); `push`'s PATCH now carries the write headers, so an agent client's push declares itself with `X-OW-Agent` as its other writes always did; `changed_client` is never pushed. Schema pin on serial 46.
- **[Writes](/docs/development/api/writes#agent-writes): a write can name the tool that sent it.** The `X-OW-Client` header (lower case letters, digits and hyphens, 1 to 32 characters) is read on the writes that record a door and kept on the element as `changed_client`, on a tombstone as `deleted_client`; a value outside the rule is stored as `null`, never a `422`. The client's claim, never proof. [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#what-an-element-carries) and the [change feed](/docs/development/api/changes) name the fields.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#schema-constants) 4.55.0: the room profile.** `ROOM_PAIRS` lists the eight type-and-supertype pairs a parlor keeps in a world, and `isRoomElement(el)` is true when an element matches one (trimmed, case-blind) and carries a non-empty `x_room_of`; a lore view leaves those out unless asked. Schema-dist serial 45, the [convention](/docs/schema/profiles#room); no type, field or wire change.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python#what-it-covers) 0.20.0: the room profile.** `ROOM_PAIRS` and `is_room_element` on the same rule as the TypeScript SDK, for a body or a `FolderElement`; the schema pin moves to schema-dist serial 45.
- **[Toolkit](/docs/development/toolkit#skills) 4.5.0: the `setup` skill.** A world set up for work (a company, an AI's own home, a council, a workshop, a software project) from one of five starter packs: a charter, a world skill, the first elements and the invites.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.19.0: import from Notion.** A Markdown & CSV export becomes a world folder: typed databases into elements, columns into fields by name, relations into links, pages into Narratives, everything unplaced kept as an extension and reported by name; `--plan` first. No AI, no account.
- **The standard 00.33.00: [Zone](/docs/schema/element_categories/zone) gains `shape`.** A string in Scope beside `role`: `polygon` or `line`, lowercase; empty, absent or any other value means not stated, and a reader draws a polygon. An additive change (a minor step), shipped as a schema update. The element page regenerated with schema-dist serial 43; [TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.54.0 (`ZoneV2.shape`, `FIELD_SCHEMA`) and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.18.0 carry the pin.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.53.3 and [Python](/docs/development/python) 0.17.1: schema pin on schema-dist serial 42.** The `.owworld` conformance set gains `fail-OW-6-marker-bom` (a byte order mark before the media type is other bytes); both readers refuse it, and the set runs as each package's own test.
- **[Writes](/docs/development/api/writes#agent-writes): every edit the site makes records the `site` door.** Taking a picture down or changing an element's author on onlyworlds.com now reads "through the site", as a hide already did.
- **[MCP](/docs/development/mcp#tools): `get_changes` takes `after`, and every tool carries its three hints.** `after` is the feed's own time filter on the MCP side; `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint` and `idempotentHint` let a host ask before a write. On the site, Recent changes reads "added" for an element made in the window, marks a hidden one, and says a deleted one's name is not kept.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.17.0: the `.owworld` archive.** `read_world_file` and `write_world_file` on the [folder format](/docs/schema/folders)'s §4.7, the third reader on the conformance set (80 of 80, run as the package's own test) and the second writer; its directory matches the TypeScript writer's entry for entry, and each SDK's reader opens the other's file. Found on the way: the TypeScript reader passed a `mimetype` marker with a byte order mark; fixed in 4.53.2 below.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.53.2: a `mimetype` marker with a byte order mark is refused** (`marker`). The reader compared the entry as decoded text and `TextDecoder` strips a leading BOM; the spec says bytes exactly, so it compares bytes now.
- **[API reference](/docs/development/api-reference): the OpenAPI document answers `ETag` and `304`.** A watcher sends the tag back in `If-None-Match` as received and gets no body when nothing changed; `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` beside it. The document's sentence on the [draught](/docs/development/api/draught)'s `budget` now says per section, as the page already did.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.12.0: the CRC check, the `producer` line, folder format 0.3.14.** An `.owworld` entry failing its CRC-32 refuses the archive; the conformance set runs as the package's own test (79 of 79 at serial 40); an export writes `producer` `{com.onlyworlds.sdk, 0.12.0, server}` and stamps `0.3.14`, and a file export carries keel's line as it is.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.16.1: schema pin on schema-dist serial 41** (the `.owworld` conformance set at 80 cases: an entry failing its CRC and a stored entry shorter than declared both refuse as `not_openable`; no schema file moved).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.16.0: the `producer` line.** An export writes `producer` `{onlyworlds, <version>, server}` beside `source` and stamps `format_version` `0.3.14`; `export_world(producer=...)` lets a tool that runs the export in its own name write its own line (keel's nightly snapshots). A folder written from another folder keeps the line it carries; `patch_world` leaves every folder-only key out of a world write. Schema pin on schema-dist serial 40.
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders) v0.3.14: the `producer` line.** `world.json` says which tool made the folder and from what: `{"tool", "version", "input"}`, `input` one word from a closed list (`server`, `bare`, `azgaar-map`, `worldanvil-export`, `foundry-world`, `obsidian-vault`, `text`, `json`). Written once by the folder's maker beside `source`; copies and snapshots carry it verbatim; absent where no maker wrote it. Writers stamp `format_version` `0.3.14` from their next release.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.15.0: folder format v0.3.13.** A folder the package writes is stamped `0.3.13` at birth; a stamp a folder carries is kept, and older stamps read as before. The schema pin moves to schema-dist serial 39 (no schema file moved; the dist carries the `.owworld` conformance set).
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders) v0.3.13: the `.owworld` archive (§4.7) is ruled**, on the 78-case conformance set three readers agree on (TypeScript 4.49.0, Unity v0.11.0, the reference checker). The set ships in schema-dist from serial 39 at `fixtures/owworld/`. A writer stamps `format_version` `0.3.13` from its next release; readers take older stamps as before.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) v0.11.0: the `.owworld` archive on the [folder format](/docs/schema/folders)'s §4.7**, 78 of 78 on the conformance set, as the TypeScript SDK 4.49.0: a refusal names one of the twelve causes, the marker is read, the default budget is the floor, names are strict with the fold pinned at Unicode 15.1.0. Unity's writer emits no marker yet.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.53.0: the last seven options objects refuse an unknown key** (`patch`, `editLinks` and its `{add, remove}`, `uploadImage`, `createFileTicket`, `uploadFile`, `createWorldLink`, `createSeat`). Every method of the client that takes an object now names a stray key before anything is sent.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.52.0: every options object refuses an unknown key.** What `list()` got in 4.51.0 now holds on `draught`, `get`, `create`, `bulk`, `changes`, `changesAll`, `removeImage`, `redeemJoin`, the three sign-ins, `mintWorldKey`, `ask` and `sort`: a key the method does not know is a `TypeError` naming it and the known keys, before anything is sent. A dropped key was never harmless: `atomc: true` ran a batch non-atomically, a misspelt idempotency key let a retry create twice, `afetr` read the whole feed.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.51.0: `list()` refuses an unknown parameter.** A key `list` and `listAll` do not know (a [filter](/docs/development/api/reads#filters) written at the top level instead of under `filter: { ... }`, or a typo) is a `TypeError` before anything is sent, naming the key and where it goes. Until now it was dropped without a word and the list came back whole, which is how a plain-JavaScript cleanup script deleted 99 Characters on a test world today. TypeScript callers were already refused at compile time.
- **[Members and sharing](/docs/development/api/members#picking-your-character): pick your own Character; the hidden flag; former members; the site's door.** `PUT /api/v2/me/character` with `{"character": id | null}` lets a person member pick the Character that is them, answering `/me`'s body. A [hidden](/docs/development/api/hidden#the-hidden-flag) element's body carries `hidden: true` for every reader who still sees it, never in the export file, world sync or the Classic API. A membership's row now outlives its account's deletion, so an id whose [roster](/docs/development/api/members#the-roster) row reads `removed` is a former member's, never the owner's. A hide made on onlyworlds.com reads `site` in `changed_via` and `deleted_via` ([Agent writes](/docs/development/api/writes#agent-writes)), where it read `server`. TypeScript SDK 4.50.0: `setMyCharacter`, `hidden` on the element types, `site` in the door type.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript#world-files) 4.49.0: the `.owworld` archive as the [folder format](/docs/schema/folders) rules it (§4.7).** The reader checks the archive's directory before inflating anything and gives the spec's verdict and cause on all 78 cases of its conformance set: six new refusal causes (`duplicate`, `collision`, `link`, `method`, `name`, `marker`), files beside the world's folder ignored, a `records/` producer's own `world.json` no longer a second world, the floor's defaults (2 GiB, 500,000 entries); the writer gives one set of bytes for one world, with the `mimetype` marker first.
- **[Sign in with OnlyWorlds](/docs/development/api/signin) (new page): revoking a tool's token takes back its consent.** Revoking a token a tool got through the sign-in, on the account page or with `DELETE /api/v2/account/tokens/{token_id}`, withdraws that tool's consent, so its next sign-in shows the Continue page again; a token minted by hand changes nothing, even when named like a tool's; a tool taken off the registry loses every person's consent at the next deploy and asks afresh if it returns ([Consent and revoking](/docs/development/api/signin#consent-and-revoking)). The page covers the registry, the authorize page, the code exchange and the SDK calls; the [error reference](/api/errors/#invalid_grant) gains `invalid_grant`.
- **[The draught](/docs/development/api/draught) (new page): the horizon fences every section in `voice`.** Every row dated after `as_of` is left out whatever its section (a Relation not yet begun, a story about her that has not happened, a dated Title, Tenure, Working style or message); in `act` the horizon fences only Events and known Narratives; undated rows and stubs still come in, and `cut` counts only what the budget dropped ([The horizon](/docs/development/api/draught#the-horizon)). [Reads](/docs/development/api/reads#filters) now lists the `ids` and `created_by` filters.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.14.3: `hidden` is read-only.** The [hidden flag](/docs/development/api/hidden#the-hidden-flag) is stripped before a write and never pushed, so a read body round-trips.
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.14.2: the doctor finds a charter on any type.** A charter filed as a Construct with supertype Charter is still the charter for the staleness check, and gets its own finding, since onlyworlds.com reads a Law with supertype Charter. 0.14.1 and TypeScript SDK 4.48.1 repin both packages to schema-dist dist.37 (no type moved).
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.48.0: `listHidden()`.** The world's [hidden elements](/docs/development/api/hidden#hiding-and-unhiding) as rows, for a host that marks what it hid.
- **The draught: `GET /api/v2/character/{id}/context`.** One Character's context in one read, what she holds, her Events and Narratives, everything that names her, her sheet, what changed around her and who plays whom, under a seven-key policy; `ids=` and `created_by=` on every list. SDK 4.47.0 `draught()` and `list({ ids, createdBy })`, Python 0.14.0 `draught()` and `list_page(ids=, created_by=)`.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.46.0: Sign in with OnlyWorlds.** `signInWithOnlyWorlds` starts the OAuth code flow with PKCE from a registered tool's page and `completeSignIn` trades the code on the callback page for an account token the tool keeps; the SDK stores nothing.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.45.0.** `hide` and `unhide` (the hidden mark's writes), `listAccountWorlds` and `mintWorldKey` (the account's worlds and a new key on one), for the parlor and Atlas.
- **[Change feed](/docs/development/api/changes): `?after=<ISO time>`.** Only the changes made at or after a time, a filter on the same feed with the same order and cursor; the [AI page](/docs/development/ai)'s "since yesterday" names it. Python 0.13.0 `changes(after=)` and `walk_changes(after=)`, SDK 4.44.0 `changes({ after })` and `changesAll(since, { after })`.
- **[Members and sharing](/docs/development/api/members): an invite link for people takes Uses.** `max_uses` 1 to 50 on a person link lets in that many accounts, each joining once with the link's role; the link stops when every use is taken, it expires or it is revoked, and who already joined stays.
- **[Members and sharing](/docs/development/api/members): change who made an element.** `POST /api/v2/{type}/{id}/adopt` with `{"member"}` (a membership id, or `owner`) sets `created_by`, for the owner or a co-builder; a contributor's key is `403 not_author`. The write takes a new `change_seq`, so the feed carries it. On the site, an Author card on the element page.
- **[TypeScript SDK](/docs/development/typescript) 4.43.0.** `adopt(type, id, member)` for the route above; `createWorldLink`'s `maxUses` documented for person links.


## 2026-10-21

- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.12.0: the doctor's packet.** `python -m onlyworlds.doctor PATH --packet` prints the findings as work packets in the `packet` extension profile (schema-dist dist.36): a Relation with supertype `Work item`, one entry per element with its reason under `x_packet_elements`, the same ids on the typed links, source and producer lines; empty when clean. Under [Check a world](/docs/development/python#check-a-world).
- **[Python](/docs/development/python) 0.11.0: the doctor.** `python -m onlyworlds.doctor` checks a world folder or an account world and reports what a keeper should look at, each finding with the element and the reason; it never writes. Under [Check a world](/docs/development/python#check-a-world).
- **[AI page](/docs/development/ai): what changed since your last visit.** The recipe for a scheduled agent: keep the feed's cursor, walk from it, read who changed what through which door; the same walk over MCP (`get_changes`) and in both packages. No new route.
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) 0.10.0.** A known-of stub is exported as the server hands it and never written back (`IsKnownOf` on read); exports declare folder format 0.3.12; a typed calendar era refuses a date it does not hold, as the TypeScript SDK 4.42.4 does. The [Python page](/docs/development/python) now names the format its package stamps, 0.3.12 (it said 0.3.11 since 0.10.4 shipped).
- **[Folder format](/docs/schema/folders) 0.3.12.** A folder's `records/RECORDS.json`, where it has one, is a plain list of producer entries. Writers now stamp `"0.3.12"`: the Python package 0.10.4 (nothing else in it changed).
- **[Unity SDK](/docs/development/unity) 0.7.0 to 0.9.0.** Join by a link, a table of players with a pick, the downloaded world file read and written as a folder, an export that says where it came from and which slice it is, a world as one `.owworld` file, and the calendar reader on the `atlas_calendar` profile.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.8.0.** Two fixes that keep a world whole: a nameless element's note is named by its type and the last eight characters of its id, so two made in the same moment no longer share one file (a world's markers, most often); a downloaded world's export keeps the server's world id, every world setting and the `source` and `producer` lines, so the folder opens in Atlas as the same world (an export made before 3.8.0 arrives as a different world once). A value that is not a link on a link field (another plugin's `location: [lat, long]`) is left alone instead of clearing the link. Folder format 0.3.8 as before.
- **[Obsidian plugin](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin) 3.7.0.** A player's or guest's known-of elements download as notes marked known of, never sent; with 3.6.0's hidden elements, under [Hidden and known-of elements](/docs/tools/obsidian-plugin#hidden-and-known-of-elements). Command names are in sentence case (Save element, Download world).
- **TypeScript 4.42.4.** `parseDate` refuses a date the typed era does not hold ("10 Thaw, 1 BG" when year 1 BG ends in Frost) rather than reading it in another era.
- **TypeScript 4.42.3.** `parseDate` reads a down era's date back to the step it was written from when the era's pivot falls mid-year; since 4.42.2 such dates parsed one year early.
- **[Keys](/docs/getting-started/keys).** A world's owner can retire its legacy 10-digit key for good from the world page; a retired key answers `401` on every door. Demo keys can't be retired.
- **[Changelog](https://www.onlyworlds.com/changelog).** The shared What's new page is now the Changelog, back to the beginning; its old paths redirect ([onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com), [Contact](/docs/contact)).
- **[The world export file](/docs/development/api/changes#the-export-file).** The owner's download now carries the provenance lines, `world.source` and `world.exported_as`. Its `schema_version` has named the standard's version since 2026-10-18; this page said the envelope's until today.
- **[Handing a seat over](/docs/development/agents#the-human-sponsor).** The owner can give an agent seat to another member with `sponsor` on the member's `PATCH`; the seat then follows that member's role and leaves with them, and keeps its key, Character and work.

## 2026-10-20

- **TypeScript 4.42.2.** A calendar's down era (a count toward a pivot, like BC) has no year zero when its pivot falls mid-year: the year holding the step just before the pivot reads `year_base`. Eras starting on the same step resolve by a stated rule: an up era over a down one, then the later in the list.
- **TypeScript 4.42.1.** The calendar reader refuses what the [`atlas_calendar` profile](https://github.com/OnlyWorlds/schema-dist/blob/main/profiles/calendar.md) refuses: an `eras` or `intercalary` that is present but not a list (`false` threw before), an era start or end that is not whole, a `year_base` other than 0 or 1, and names that are not strings. The Unity SDK reads the same way.
- **[Extension profiles](/docs/schema/profiles).** A named extension field that tools agree to read the same way, published as a page in schema-dist's `profiles/` folder (serial 30). The first two: `x_identity_key`, the same being in several worlds, and `atlas_calendar`, a world's own calendar. Nothing in the schema changed.

- **Schema 00.32.02.** A Narrative's `start_date` is when it is first told or recorded and its `end_date` when it stops being told (empty while it is still told); the span it recounts lives on its Events ([Narrative](/docs/schema/element_categories/narrative)). No field, type or link changed, so nothing moves on the wire. The TypeScript SDK 4.42.0 and the Python package 0.10.3 carry it.
- **[What's new](https://www.onlyworlds.com/whats-new).** One page for every release across OnlyWorlds: the site, Atlas, the Obsidian plugin, the toolkit, both SDKs, the Python package and schema-dist, with an Atom feed (`/whats-new.xml`) and the merged list as JSON (`/whats-new.json`, a `sources` row per tool with its count and an `ok` flag). The TypeScript SDK and the Python package now publish a GitHub release for each version, which is where the page reads them ([Contact and resources](/docs/contact), [onlyworlds.com](/docs/tools/onlyworlds-com)).
- **Python 0.10.2.** The OKF export keeps a known-of stub's `known_of: true` in its frontmatter; it was dropped before, so a stub read as a whole element.

- **World folders, format v0.3.11.** A tool that re-keys an element (gives it a new id and rewrites every reference) never rewrites `x_identity_key`, even when its value equals the moved id: the key says two elements in different worlds are the same being, and a re-key in one world would split them. Nothing else changed; Python 0.10.1 declares the new version.
- **[Public fields on a stub](/docs/development/api/members#known-of).** A known-of Character now also carries what anyone could see: `physicality`, `height`, `weight`, `species` and `reputation`, with their values. schema-dist serial 28 lists each type's public fields in `presentation.json`, and TypeScript 4.41.0 and Python 0.10.0 export them as `PUBLIC_FIELDS`. A stub's search row carries `known_of: true` ([Search](/docs/development/api/reads#search)). A write carrying `known_of` is refused by name: [`known_of_not_writable`](/api/errors#known_of_not_writable) (`isKnownOfNotWritable`, `is_known_of_not_writable`). A folder keeps a stub's served values ([Known-of elements](/docs/schema/folders#known-of-elements)).
- **[Known of](/docs/development/api/members#known-of).** A guest or player also reads what its own Character's sheet links to, one step out, as stubs marked `known_of: true`: name, description and picture kept, every other field unset. Never write a stub back (`422`). World folders, format 0.3.10: a stub on disk keeps `known_of` and only its kept keys ([Known-of elements](/docs/schema/folders#known-of-elements)). TypeScript 4.40.0 (`isKnownOf`) and Python 0.9.0 (`is_known_of`) refuse to write a stub back before sending.
- **[A table of players](/docs/development/api/members#a-table-of-players).** One player link can seat up to 50 players, each picking one Character from the owner's list, once (`max_uses`, `characters`). The preview says what is left to pick; a taken pick is `422` [`character_taken`](/api/errors/#character_taken). A world now holds at most 50 players, and a player link opened in a full one is `422` [`world_full`](/api/errors/#world_full). TypeScript 4.39.0: `maxUses` and `characters` on `createWorldLink`, the pick on `redeemJoin`, `isCharacterTaken`, `isCharacterInvalid` and `isWorldFull`.
- **Schema 00.32.01.** An Event's `characters` are now the Characters who know about it: they were present, aware of it, or involved in it ([Event](/docs/schema/element_categories/event)). No field, type or link changed. The TypeScript SDK 4.38.0 and the Python package 0.8.1 carry it.
- **[Hidden elements](/docs/development/api/hidden)** is a new page. The owner or a co-builder can hide an element (`PUT` and `DELETE /api/v2/{type}/{id}/hidden`, `GET /api/v2/world/hidden`). To every other reader it answers as a missing element: `404`, never `410`, its links left out of every body, and a hidden Character's id `null` on the roster, `/me` and `owner_character`.
- **The feed says why a delete happened.** Every `delete` op on [`/changes`](/docs/development/api/changes) carries `reason`: `deleted`, or `hidden` for an element hidden from this key.
